The higher-algebraic skeleton of the superstring – a case study
Rafał R. Suszek

Abstract
. A novel Lie-superalgebraic description of the superstring in the super-Minkowskian background is extracted from the Cartan–Eilenberg super-1-gerbe geometrising the higher gauge field (the Green–Schwarz super-3-cocycle) that couples to the supercharge carried by the superstring. The description assumes the form of a hierarchy of Lie superalgebras integrable to a hierarchy of Lie supergroups and provides a manifestly supersymmetric model of a family of supermanifolds defining a trivialisation of the super-1-gerbe over the embedded superstring worldsheet. The trivialisation, obtained in a purely topological formulation of the superstring dynamics dual to the standard Nambu–Goto-type one, conforms with the gerbe-theoretic representation of extended sources of higher gauge fields known from previous studies of the σ \sigma -model of the bosonic string.



1.
Introduction

The idea to probe spacetime geometry with the dynamics of distributions of charged matter has been around for a long time,
cp
Refs.
[
FI29b
,

,
Wey29
,

\,(M,{\rm g})\,
accessible to a neutral pointlike particle. The enhancement incorporates the ‘higher’ geometry of the gauge fields
\,{\rm H}\in Z^{p+2}_{\rm dR}(M),\ p\in{\mathbb{N}}\,
(and their nonabelian counterparts) coupling to the respective charges, first neatly packaged by Lubkin
[
Lub63
]
, Trautman
[
Tra70
]

et al.

whose local sections provide us with the (suitably extended) Deligne–Beilinson cohomological data of the gauge fields, employed in the construction of simple models of charge dynamics and their geometric quantisation already by Alvarez
[

and Gawȩdzki

In the presence of Killing vector fields
\,\mathcal{K}_{A}\in\Gamma({\mathsf{T}}M),\ A\in\overline{1,K}\,
of the background metric
\,{\rm g}\,
whose flows preserve the action functional defining the charge dynamics, the enhancement may take the form of a
deformation
or an
extension
of the Lie algebra of these vector fields. Notable instantiations of the former include the algebra
\,[P_{\mu},P_{\nu}]=2qH_{\mu\nu}\,,\ \mu,\nu\in\overline{0,3}\,
of lifts of translations
\,\{P_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in\overline{0,3}}\,
to the space of states of a pointlike particle of charge
\,q\,
in a constant electromagnetic field
\,{\rm H}=H_{\mu\nu}\,{\mathsf{d}}x^{\mu}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}x^{\nu}\in\Omega^{2}({\rm Mink}(3,1))
, and the Poisson germ of the Drinfeld–Jimbo quantum-group structure in the (chiral) Wess–Zumino–Witten model on
\,{\rm SU}(2)\,
(with the Cartan 3-form as the gauge field) in Ref.
[
Gaw91
, Sec. 4]
. The latter are amply exemplified by the infinite sequence of extensions
\,{\rm Maxwell}_{n},\ n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\times}\,
of the Poincaré algebra presented in Refs.
[
BG10
,
GK17
]
as algebraic structures encoding the rich dynamics of a (possibly backreacting) multipole distribution of charges in an external electromagnetic field (generalising the pioneering constructions: the kinematical algebras of Ref.
[

and the Maxwell algebra of Ref.
[

]
) and by the Free (super-)Differential-Algebra (FsDA) extensions of the super-Poincaré algebra considered in Ref.
[

]
in the context of superstring theory in Minkowskian spacetime that build upon the earlier constructions of Refs.
[

,

BS95
]
. The supersymmetric extensions, of central relevance to us in what follows, seem to be encompassed by the structure of the Free Lie super-Algebra (FLsA) laid out in the recent study
[
GKP19
]
. The common source of the enhancement in the examples listed is an interplay between the geometry (topology) of the distribution of charge consistent with its dynamics and the intrinsic cohomology of the gauge field
\,{\rm H}\,
(tied intimately with the aforementioned higher geometry), the latter being typically assumed to satisfy the strong invariance condition
\,\mathcal{K}_{A}\righthalfcup{\rm H}\in B^{\bullet}_{\rm dR}(M)\,
(ensuring quasi-invariance of the lagrangian density) in the case of charge distributions localised on closed submanifolds of
\,M
,
cp

[

,
[

and

-branes, to be investigated using methods of higher (super)geometry that we review systematically below.

The algebraic mechanisms from the previous paragraph have been encountered and employed extensively as model-building tools in the setting of (super)field theory with non-linearly realised symmetry
[

\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}\,
of a (super)symmetry group
\,{\rm G}\,
relative to its distinguished closed subgroup
\,{\rm H}\,
with the tangent Lie algebra
\,\mathfrak{h}\equiv{\rm Lie}({\rm H})\,
defining a
reductive

\,\mathfrak{t}\oplus\mathfrak{h}=\mathfrak{g}\equiv{\rm Lie}({\rm G})\,
of the tangent Lie (super)algebra
\,\mathfrak{g}\,

\,[\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{t}]\subset\mathfrak{t}

{\rm H}

taken from the tensor algebra of the linear space of (

\,{\rm G}\,
and pulled back to the field space
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}\,
along local sections of the principal

-bundle
\,{\rm G}\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{{\rm G}/{\rm H}}\ }{\rm G}/{\rm H}\,
(

-graded setting in
[

classical solution

[

i.e.

[

The specific choice, referred to in the last paragraph, of a constructive paradigm of study of charge dynamics with the help of the Cartan calculus on a Lie (super)group paves the way for a systematic application of the techniques of Free (
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded) Differential Algebras (FDA) of Refs.
[
DF82
,
vN83
,

]

\,\mathcal{L}\mathcal{I}({\rm G})\,
of LI (super-)1-forms on the Lie (super)group
\,{\rm G}\,
by the (super-)
(p+1)
-form potential of the relevant gauge field. For these, a finite ladder of
integrable
(super)central extensions
\,{\boldsymbol{0}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{a}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{n+1}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{n}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{0}}\,
(altogether combining into a generically
non
-(super)central extension
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{g}\equiv\mathfrak{g}_{N}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{0}\equiv\mathfrak{g}\,
of the original Lie (super)algebra
\,\mathfrak{g}
) is sought that yields a resolution of the gauge field in the Cartan–Eilenberg (CaE) cohomology
\,{\rm CaE}^{p+2}({\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G})\equiv H^{p+2}_{\rm dR}({\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G})^{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G}}\,
of the Lie (super)group
\,{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G}\,
which integrates
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{g}
. Each rung of the ladder is determined by a (super-)2-cocycle in the decomposition of the pullback of the gauge field in terms of elements of
\,\mathcal{L}\mathcal{I}({\rm G}_{n})\,
(for
\,{\rm G}_{n}\,
the Lie (super)group of
\,\mathfrak{g}_{n}
) in conformity with the standard classification of (super)central extensions of a Lie (super)algebra
\,\mathfrak{g}_{n}\,
by a (super)commutative Lie (super)algebra
\,\mathfrak{a}_{n}\,
by elements of the group
\,H^{2}(\mathfrak{g}_{n},\mathfrak{a}_{n})\,
in the
\mathfrak{a}_{n}
-valued cohomology of
\,\mathfrak{g}_{n}
,
cp
Ref.
[
Lei75
]
. While it is
not

a priori
that a finite resolution
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{g}\,
of this kind exists

Attaining a similar goal for physically relevant

, the homogeneous spaces:

\,({\rm SU}(1,1|2)\times{\rm SU}(1,1|2))_{2}/({\rm SO}(2,1)\times{\rm SO}(3))\equiv{\rm s}({\rm AdS}_{3}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{3})\,

One natural possibility, suggested by the prime rôle of the asymptotic correspondence between the curved dynamics (its supergeometric data and the lagrangian model) and its flat-superspace counterpart in the construction of the former, cp Ref.
[
MT98

when viewed as solutions to a cohomological problem in
\,{\rm CaE}^{p+2}({\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|ND_{d,1}))\,
(their argument exploits the assumed triviality of the de Rham cohomology of the extension).

In order to be able to interpret the extension
\,{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G}\longrightarrow{\rm G}\,
as a partial geometrisation of the GS super-
(p+2)
-cocycle
\,{\rm H}\in Z^{p+2}_{\rm dR}({\rm G})^{\rm G}\,
in the (standard) sense of Murray, one should put the CaE cohomology of
\,{\rm G}\,
on the same footing as the underlying de Rham cohomology. That this makes sense is suggested by an old argument due to Rabin and Crane
[
RC85
,
Rab87
]
that essentially explains the discrepancy between
\,{\rm CaE}^{\bullet}({\rm sMink}(d,1|ND_{d,1}))\,
(for
\,N=1
) and
\,H^{\bullet}_{\rm dR}({\rm sMink}(d,1|ND_{d,1}))\equiv 0\,
as coming,
via
the standard duality, from the homology of an orbifold
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|ND_{d,1})/\Gamma_{\rm KR}\,
of
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|ND_{d,1})\,
relative to a discrete subgroup
\,\Gamma_{\rm KR}\subset{\rm sMink}(d,1|ND_{d,1})\,
that had been encountered previously by Kostelecký and Rabin in their study of supersymmetric field theory on the lattice
[
KR84
]
. The orbifold has the topological structure of a fibration over its body
\,{\rm Mink}(d,1)\,
with compact Graßmann-odd fibers
[
RC85
]
. The argument led Rabin to postulate that the GS super-
\sigma
-model with the supertarget
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|ND_{d,1})\,
be interpreted as describing propagation of loop-like distributions of supercharge within
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|ND_{d,1})/\Gamma_{\rm KR}
. But then, by a standard argument (
cp
,
e.g.
, Refs.
[
DHVW85
,
DHVW86
]
and, in particular,
[
Sus12
, Sec. 8.3]
and Ref.
[
Sus13
]
in which the idea of a worldvolume orbifold was formalised with reference to the universal gauge principle derived in Refs.
[
GSW10
,
GSW13
]
), one has to incorporate the
\Gamma_{\rm KR}
-twisted sector in the superfield theory on the cover
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|ND_{d,1})
, and, indeed, this yields,
e.g.
, a Graßmann-odd wrapping anomaly in the canonical picture of
[
Sus18a
, Sec. 4.2]
that reproduces the Green extension of the
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|ND_{d,1})\,
superalgebra resolving the GS super-3-cocycle. While vital for internal consistency of our treatment, the last result shows quite explicitly how an enhancement of a neutral Killing algebra in the presence of a distribution of charged matter
combines
information on the cohomology of the gauge field and the topology of the distribution. We may now be more specific in defining our goal: We wish to extract a clearcut signature of the localisation of a classical superstring (and more generally super-
p
-brane) trajectory in the supertarget from the superalgebraic description of the gauge field that couples to it. To this end, we first need to complete the geometrisation of that field and recall from the extensive study of analogous geometrisations in the non-
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting the higher-geometric representation of extended objects to which the gauge field of the
\sigma
-model couples – the D-branes
[
Pol95
]
, with the coupling encoded in the effective Dirac–Born–Infeld (DBI) lagrangian density
[
FT85
,
ACNY87
,
Lei89
]
.

The train of reasoning restated above has laid the foundation for the geometrisation programme, initiated by the Author in Ref.
[
Sus17
]
, elaborated in Refs.
[
Sus19
,
Sus18a
,
Sus18b
,
Sus20
]
and recently reviewed in Ref.
[
Sus21
]
, which sets out to associate with the physically relevant CaE gauge-field super-
(p+2)
-cocycles that determine the known GS-type super-
\sigma
-models on homogeneous spaces
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}\,
of supersymmetry Lie supergroups
\,{\rm G}
, as well as with the attendant supersymmetric defects
[
FSW08
,
RS09
,
Sus11a
,
Sus11b
]
,
concrete
higher-geometric objects of the type conceived by Murray
et al.
in the non-
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting (and recently reconsidered in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting by Huerta
[
Hue20
]
), and to lift all essential geometric properties of the underlying superfield theories (such as,
e.g.
, their
\kappa
-symmetry) and constitutive relations between them (such as,
e.g.
, the fundamental asymptotic relation between the super-
p
-brane models with the super-
{\rm AdS}_{m}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{m}
targets and their super-Minkowskian counterparts) to those higher (super)geometries and the associated higher categories. The rationale for the goal thus delineated is an early observation, due to Gawȩdzki
[
Gaw88
]
, that the higher-geometric objects canonically determine, through the so-called cohomological transgression, a geometric (pre)quantisation scheme for the simple charge geometrodynamics of the
\sigma
-model. Thus, the existence of the said lifts is to be viewed as a condition of quantum-mechanical consistency of the structures, properties and relations lifted. In the gerbe-theoretic picture, the D-branes of string theory are represented by trivialisations of the 1-gerbe of the gauge field of the
\sigma
-model over submanifolds of the target space
\,M\,

[
Gaw99
,
FW99
,
CJM02
,
GR02
,
Gaw05
]
– these are described by certain vector bundles whose connection acquires the interpretation of the (gerbe-twisted) gauge field of the DBI theory.

The basic geometric substrate of the principle of descent that lies at the core of Murray’s geometrisation of the class
\,[{\rm H}]\in H^{p+2}_{\rm dR}(M,{\mathbb{Z}})\,
of a gauge field
\,{\rm H}\,
(assumed integral)
[
Mur96
,
Mur10
]
is a
surjective submersion

\,{\mathsf{Y}}M\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}M}\ }M\,
whose total space supports a smooth primitive
\,{\rm B}\in\Omega^{p+1}({\mathsf{Y}}M)\,
for the pullback of
\,{\rm H}
,
i.e.
, such that
\,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}M}^{*}{\rm H}={\mathsf{d}}{\rm B}
. The
(p+1)
-form
\,{\rm B}\,
can then be viewed as the trivial
p
-gerbe
\,\mathcal{I}^{(p)}_{\rm B}\,
of curvature
\,{\mathsf{d}}{\rm B}\,
and curving
\,{\rm B}\,
over
\,{\mathsf{Y}}M
. The
p
-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}^{(p)}\,
for
\,[{\rm H}]\,
is subsequently erected over the nerve of the small category
\,\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 16.23613pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.23613pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.15099pt\raise 7.56187pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.048pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pts\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.23613pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.52773pt\raise-7.9861pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.47223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447ptt\equiv{\rm pr}_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.23613pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 40.23613pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathsf{Y}}M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}
, defined by the (
\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}M}
-)fibred square
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}M\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}M\times_{M}{\mathsf{Y}}M\,
of the surjective submersion (
cp
App.
A
), as a family
\,\mathscr{G}\equiv\{\mathcal{G}^{(p-k)}\}_{k\in\overline{1,p+1}}\,
of
(p-k)
-gerbes (the
l
-gerbes with
\,l\in\{0,-1\}\,
being identified with principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundles with a compatible connection (
l=0
) and connection-preserving isomorphisms between them (
l=-1

\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[k+1]}M\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}^{[k]}M\times_{M}{\mathsf{Y}}M
, the members of
\,\mathscr{G}\,
being subject to various coherence constraints. Accordingly, and in keeping with the underlying (super)field-theoretic paradigm in which the (super)field theory over
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}\,
is modelled over
\,{\rm G}
, the point of departure of the programme advocated above is the
epimorphism of Lie supergroups

\,{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G}\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G}}\ }{\rm G}\,
(alongside the LI primitive for the pullback of the LI gauge field
\,{\rm H}\,
along
\,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G}}
) returned by the integrable-extension algorithm described in the previous paragraph. From this point onwards, one simply turns the crank of Murray’s machine of descent and, recursively, that of de Azcárraga’s extension procedure, insisting that all extensions are consistently
{\rm ad}_{\mathfrak{h}}
-equivariant in the latter (a condition essentially built into the FDA techniques employed in the procedure in the guise of the so-called minimal subalgebra
[
Sul77
]
,
cp
Ref.
[
vN83
, Sec. 6]
), and – in the former – that all secondary surjective submersions that arise in the process are Lie-supergroup epimorphisms, and that all (connection-preserving) isomorphisms of principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundles that mark the penultimate stage of the construction and of its sub-constructions are Lie-supergroup isomorphisms, so that, by the end of the long day, we obtain a ‘bundle
p
-gerbe object in the category of Lie supergroups’. The ensuing
Cartan–Eilenberg super-
p
-gerbe

\,\mathcal{G}^{(p)}\,
still has to be descended to the relevant homogeneous space
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}
. As demonstrated by Gawȩdzki, Waldorf and the Author in Refs.
[
GSW10
,
GSW13
,
Sus12
,
Sus11b
,
Sus13
]
, this requires that
\,\mathcal{G}^{(p)}\,
carry a
descendable

{\rm H}
-equivariant structure. One of the crucial features of the advocated geometrisation scheme is that such a structure is inscribed in the very definition of the super-
p
-gerbe, which lends weight to the claim to naturality of the scheme in the (super)field-theoretic context under consideration. To date, the results of the programme include an
explicit
construction
[
Sus17
]
of the CaE super-
p
-gerbes over
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
for the GS super-
p
-branes with
\,p\in\{0,1,2\}
, an extensive study
[
Sus19
]
of their equivariance properties inspired by the analogy with the purely Graßmann-even WZW
\sigma
-model
[
HM85
]
, and an explicit construction
[
Sus18b
]
of a super-0-gerbe over
\,{\rm s}({\rm AdS}_{2}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2})\,
for the Zhou superparticle that provides a constructive application of the principle of İnönü–Wigner contractibility proposed in Ref.
[
Sus18a
]
(
cp
the footnote on p.
4
). The CaE super-
p
-gerbes (for a large class of known super-
p
-brane species) were also shown
[
Sus19
,
Sus20
]
to carry a canonical and canonically (linearised-)supersymmetric linearised
\kappa
-symmetry-equivariant structure, in conformity with an interpretation of
\kappa
-symmetry, worked out
ibid.
, purely in terms of the supertarget geometry. In fact, it is from the latter interpretation that provides a solution to the problem posed in the present Introduction, and so we conclude the section with a recapitulation of the physical idea behind it.

The objective of the present study is to extract a supersymmetric
target-space
higher-geometric description of the fundamental dynamical object of the GS super-
\sigma
-model with the supertarget
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}
,
i.e.
, of the (closed) super-
p
-brane trajectory, from the CaE super-
p
-gerbe over
\,{\rm G}\,
associated with the GS super-
(p+2)
-cocycle that determines its Wess–Zumino term – all that in the much tractable model setting: for the superstring (
p=1
) in the superspace
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\equiv{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)
. In the light of the hitherto discussion, the task boils down to identifying a (higher-)superalgebraic object related to the Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
of the supersymmetry supergroup
\,{\rm G}\equiv{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
that captures a classical (
i.e.
, critical) embedding of the superstring worldsheet in the supertarget and, in particular, the supersymmetry that survives such localisation. That the well-posedness of this task is non-obvious is best illustrated by the discussion of a local (tangential) Graßmann-odd supersymmetry of the GS super-
\sigma
-model in the standard NG formulation, aka
\kappa
-symmetry, discovered by de Azcárraga and Lukierski (for the superparticle) in Ref.
[
dAL83
]
and subsequently rediscovered and elaborated by Siegel (for the superstring) in Refs.
[
Sie83
,
Sie84
]
, whose existence is tied with the mechanism of restitution of equibalance of the internal degreees of freedom of both Graßmann parities in the vacuum of the GS superfield theory through a removal of fermionic Goldstone modes, consistent with the structure of the supersymmetry Lie superalgebra of the theory: The symmetry couples the metric and topological terms in the action functional (
i.e.
, they are not invariant
separately
), and that only for a finely tuned relative normalisation of the two. It also bracket-generates a (super)algebra whose on-shell closure requires incorporation of generators of diffeomorphisms of the worldsheet
[
McA00
]
. A path to
target-space
geometrisation of
\kappa
-symmetry and field equations of the GS super-
\sigma
-model on
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}
, and so also towards a meaningful formulation of the problem of interest, was paved in Refs.
[
Sus19
,
Sus20
]
where a duality – first noted in Ref.
[
HP86
]
, later elaborated substantially in Ref.
[
GIT90
]
and employed in a rederivation of a variety of (super-
\sigma
-)models of charge dynamics in Refs.
[
McA10
,
Wes00
,
GKW06b
,
GKW06a
]
– was formalised, geometrised and exploited that exists between the original NG formulation of the GS super-
\sigma
-model and a
purely topological
(super)field theory, termed the Hughes–Polchinski (HP) formulation of the GS super-
\sigma
-model by the Author, with the superfield space
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}_{\rm vac}\,
associated to another reductive decomposition
\,\mathfrak{g}=(\mathfrak{t}\oplus\mathfrak{d})\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{\rm vac},\ \mathfrak{d}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{\rm vac}=\mathfrak{h}\,
with
\,\mathfrak{h}_{\rm vac}={\rm Lie}({\rm H}_{\rm vac})\,
that encodes the spontaneous breakdown
\,{\rm H}\searrow{\rm H}_{\rm vac}\,
of the ‘invisible’ gauge symmetry
\,{\rm H}\,
of the superfield theory. The field space of the new formulation contains additional degrees of freedom, to wit, the bosonic Goldstone fields transverse to (the body of) the vacuum of the GS super-
\sigma
-model and modelled on the vector space
\,\mathfrak{d}\cong\mathfrak{h}/\mathfrak{h}_{\rm vac}
. Its topologicality is reflected by the replacement of the original metric term of the NG by the pullback of an
{\rm H}_{\rm vac}
-basic LI super-
(p+1)
-form on
\,{\rm G}
, itself (a distinguished scalar multiple of) a volume form
\,{\rm Vol}(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac})\,
on a fixed algebraic model
\,\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}\subset\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}\,
of the body of the vacuum, to the worldvolume of the super-
p
-brane along suitably
\mathfrak{d}
-augmented sections of the NG formulation. On the higher-geometric side, this means that the dual HP dynamics is entirely determined by a (
{\rm H}_{\rm vac}
-equivariant) CaE super-
p
-gerbe – the tensor product of the original super-
p
-gerbe for the GS super-
(p+2)
-cocycle with the trivial one with the curving given by the volume form on
\,\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}\,
that we shall call, after Ref.
[
Sus19
]
, the
extended Hughes–Polchinski super-
p
-gerbe over
\,{\rm G}\,
and denote as (
\lambda_{p}^{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}\,
is the scalar mentioned earlier)

(1.1)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(p)}\equiv\mathcal{G}{}^{(p)}\otimes\mathcal{I}^{(p)}_{\lambda_{p}^{*}\,{\rm Vol}(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac})}\,.

The removal of the extra Goldstone modes through the Inverse Higgs Effect of Ref.
[
IO75
]
puts us back in the original NG formulation. It is realised by imposition of a subset of superfield equations of the HP formulation that can be interpreted as geometric constraints on the tangents of the fields of the model restricting the latter to a superdistribution in the tangent sheaf
\,\mathcal{T}{\rm G}\,
of the target supermanifold
\,{\rm G}
, dubbed the HP/NG correspondence superdistribution and denoted as
\,{\rm Corr}(\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{B}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{p,\lambda^{*}_{p}})
. That
all
superfield equations geometrise in an analogous manner, as do the gauge-fixing conditions for the ‘invisible’ local-symmetry group
\,{\rm H}_{\rm vac}
, altogether giving rise to what was named the HP vacuum superdistribution and denoted as
\,{\rm Vac}(\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{B}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{p,\lambda^{*}_{p}})\,
in Ref.
[
Sus20
]
, is a structural feature of the dual HP formulation that turns out to be instrumental in resolving the above-posed problem of extraction of super-
p
-brane data from
\,\mathcal{G}^{(p)}\,
in a manner that we outline below in the closing paragraph of the Introduction.

The (classical) vacuum of the GS super-
\sigma
-model in the HP formulation emerges as a sub-supermanifold within
\,{\rm G}\,
defined as the intersection of the HP local sections of the principal
{\rm H}_{\rm vac}
-bundle
\,{\rm G}\longrightarrow{\rm G}/{\rm H}_{\rm vac}\,
used in the definition of the lagrangean superfield of the theory with an integral supermanifold of
\,{\rm Vac}(\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{B}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{p,\lambda^{*}_{p}})
. The existence of a foliation of the sections by such integral leaves calls for involutivity of
\,{\rm Vac}(\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{B}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{p,\lambda^{*}_{p}})\,
that was examined in Ref.
[
Sus20
]
. There is yet another superdistribution whose regular behaviour is of essence for the consistency of the entire framework, namely, the limit of the weak derived flag (in the sense of Tanaka
6
6

6

The concept is very closely related to that of the FLsA of Ref.
[
GKP19
]
.

[
Tan70
]
) of the projection to
\,{\rm Vac}(\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{B}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{p,\lambda^{*}_{p}})\,
of the linear span of the set of generators of an enhanced (right) gauge supersymmetry that arises upon restriction of the superfield of the super-
\sigma
-model to
\,{\rm Corr}(\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{B}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{p,\lambda_{p}^{*}})
. The projection removes the obvious Graßmann-even component modelled on
\,\mathfrak{d}\,
(reflecting the enhancement
\,\mathfrak{h}_{\rm vac}\nearrow\mathfrak{h}\,
of the ‘invisible’ gauge-symmetry algebra that accompanies the transition between the two formulations) and leaves us with a superdistribution
\,\kappa(\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{B}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{p,\lambda_{p}^{*}})\,
that contains a generic Graßmann-odd component – the latter
is
the target space-geometric realisation of the
\kappa
-symmetry of the GS super-
\sigma
-model in the topological formulation, whence the name
\kappa
-symmetry superdistribution
given to it in Ref.
[
Sus20
]
. The regularity alluded to above simply means that the limit should stay within
\,{\rm Vac}(\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{B}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{p,\lambda^{*}_{p}})
, so that it can be given the interpretation of a
gauge supersymmetry of the vacuum
, engendered by
\,\kappa(\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{B}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{p,\lambda_{p}^{*}})
. This happens iff the vacuum superdistribution is involutive, in which case
\,\kappa(\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{B}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{p,\lambda_{p}^{*}})\,
is readily seen to bracket-generate
\,{\rm Vac}(\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{B}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{p,\lambda^{*}_{p}})\,
– the vacuum supermanifold becomes a single orbit of the gauge-symmetry supergroup obtained through integration of the Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{vac}\bigl(\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{B}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{p,\lambda^{*}_{p}}\bigr)\,
modelling the limit. The last fact, taken in conjunction with the higher-geometric interpretation and implementation of gauge symmetries worked out in Refs.
[
GSW10
,
GSW13
,
Sus12
,
Sus11b
,
Sus13
]
, leads us to the following hypothesis of Refs.
[
Sus20
,
Sus21
]
:

Upon restriction to the vacuum, the extended HP super-
p
-gerbe
\,\widehat{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(p)}\,
trivialises flatly as
\,\widehat{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(p)}\cong\mathcal{I}_{0}
.

In the present paper, we prove the hypothesis for
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}={\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)\equiv{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
and
\,p=1\,
with the relevant super-3-cocycle of the GS super-
\sigma
-model for the superstring (whose physical content and supersymmetry, both global and local (
\kappa
-symmetry), in the dual HP formulation is reviewed for later reference in Secs.
2
and
3
, respectively) as in Ref.
[
dAT89
]
. We do that by first lifting the CaE super-1-gerbe of Ref.
[
Sus17
, Sec. 5.2]
, erected directly over the Lie supergroup
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
, to the mother Lie supergroup
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
in Sect.
4
(
Theorem
1
), and subsequently deriving the trivialisation 1-isomorphism in Sec.
5.2
(
Theorem
2
) upon demonstrating briefly the higher-geometric realisation of global supersymmetry in Sec.
5.1
. This yields the most basic solution to the extraction problem posed at the beginning of the Introduction, which is readily seen by rewriting the trivialisation (symbolically, and for the value
\,\lambda_{1}^{*}=2\,
determined in Sec.
3
) as

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}{}^{(1)}\mathord{\restriction}_{\rm vacuum}\cong\mathcal{I}_{-2{\rm Vol}(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac})}\mathord{\restriction}_{\rm vacuum}\,,

cp
Eq. (
1.1
). The solution has an essential weakness: In consequence of the lack of an obvious Lie-supergroup structure on the vacuum, the 1-isomorphism can only be and is a non-supersymmetric one, and so it exists outside the framework systematically constructed in the first part of the paper (and introduced in the original papers). We strengthen the ‘raw’ result stated in Theorem
2
in the last part of Sec.
5.2
by passing to the tangent sheaf of the higher-geometric object that represents the 1-isomorphism and extracting a hierarchy of Lie superalgebras (
5.17
) associated with the various supermanifold components of that object and interrelated analogously but by Lie-superalgebra homomorphisms (
Theorem
3
) –
this
is the structure encoding the supersymmetric supergeometry of the vacuum that we have been after, and it seems appropriate to call it the
{\bf sLieAlg}
-skeleton of the vacuum
. We conclude our study with one further step in which we integrate the
{\bf sLieAlg}
-skeleton to a hierarchy of Lie supergroups (
Theorem
5
), whereby the
{\bf sLieGrp}
-model of the vacuum
(
5.74
) arises.

Theorems
2
–
5
constitute the main results of the present study, consistent with the higher-geometric representations of physical objects charged under the gauge field geometrised, and form a solid basis for further investigation of geometrisations of gauge fields of the GS super-
\sigma
-models for super-
p
-branes on homogeneous spaces of Lie supergroups that we intend to take up in the future.

2.
The geometrodynamics of the HP superstring

In this opening section, we recall the definition of the supersymmetric field theory of interest, modelling the propagation in Minkowskian spacetime of a loop-like distribution of charges of both Graßmann parities in equibalance. The definition calls for a supermanifold with the Minkowskian body and a supersymmetric de Rham 3-cocycle field that couples to the supercharge current engendered by the propagating loop. In our presentation, we emphasise, purposefully, the underlying Lie-supergroup structure and the associated tangential Lie-superalgebra structure.

The point of departure of our discussion is the
(d+1)
-dimensional Minkowski space (for some
\,d\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\times}
)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{d,1}\equiv\bigl({\mathbb{R}}^{\times d+1},\eta\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\eta=\eta_{ab}\,E^{a}\otimes E^{b}\,,\qquad\qquad(\eta_{ab})=\textrm{diag}\,(-1,\underbrace{1,1,\ldots,1}_{d\ \textrm{times}})

with its structure of an abelian Lie group determined by the binary operation (written in terms of the global cartesian coordinates
\,\{x^{a}\}^{a\in\overline{0,d}}
)

\displaystyle|\underline{{\rm m}}|\ :\ {\mathbb{R}}^{d,1}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d,1}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d,1}\ :\ \bigl(\bigl(x_{1}^{a}\bigr),\bigl(x_{2}^{b}\bigr)\bigr)\longmapsto\bigl(x_{1}^{a}+x_{2}^{a}\bigr)\,,

with the commutative Lie algebra

\displaystyle\mathfrak{mink}(d,1)=\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,\left\langle P_{a}\right\rangle\,,\qquad\qquad[P_{a},P_{b}]=0

and the associated left-invariant (LI) Maurer–Cartan form

\displaystyle E\equiv E^{a}\otimes P_{a}\,,\qquad\qquad E^{a}(x)={\mathsf{d}}x^{a}\,.

To the above, there corresponds the Clifford algebra

\displaystyle{\rm Cliff}\bigl({\mathbb{R}}^{d,1}\bigr)=\left\langle\ \Gamma_{a}\ |\ a\in\overline{0,d}\ \right\rangle\,,\qquad\qquad\{\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b}\}=2\eta_{ab}\,{\boldsymbol{1}}

that contains the spin group
\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)
, the universal cover of the connected component
\,{\rm SO}_{0}(d,1)\,
of the identity element of the Lorentz group
\,{\rm SO}(d,1)\equiv{\rm SO}({\mathbb{R}}^{d,1})\,
of
\,{\mathbb{R}}^{d,1}
,

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{1}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}\longrightarrow{\rm Spin}(d,1)\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{\rm Spin}\ }{\rm SO}_{0}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{1}}\,.

We pick up a vector space

\displaystyle S_{d,1}\cong{\mathbb{R}}^{\times D_{d,1}}

that carries a Majorana-spinor realisation

\displaystyle S\ :\ {\rm Spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\rm End}(S_{d,1})

of
\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)
, assuming the following identities to be satisfied in this realisation: the Fierz identities

(2.1)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\Gamma^{a}_{\alpha(\beta}\,\Gamma_{a\,\gamma\delta)}=0\,,\qquad\qquad\qquad\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}\,,\qquad\qquad\qquad\Gamma^{a}\equiv\eta^{-1\,ab}\,\Gamma_{b}\,,

and – for the corresponding charge-conjugation operator
\,C\in{\rm End}(S_{d,1})\,
– the symmetry relations

\displaystyle C^{\rm T}=-C\,,\qquad\qquad\bigl(C\,\Gamma_{a}\bigr)^{\rm T}=C\,\Gamma_{a}\equiv\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}

which, in particular, rule out the possibilities
\,d\in\{5,6,7\}
.

Given these, we consider the associated super-Poincaré (super)group, that is the supermanifold

\displaystyle{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})=\bigl(\widetilde{{\rm ISO}}(d,1)\equiv{\mathbb{R}}^{\times d+1}\rtimes_{L}{\rm Spin}(d,1),\mathcal{O}_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\equiv C^{\infty}(\cdot,{\mathbb{R}})\circ{\rm pr}_{1}\otimes C^{\infty}(\cdot,{\mathbb{R}})\circ{\rm pr}_{2}\otimes\bigwedge\hskip-3.0pt{}^{\bullet}\,S_{d,1}\bigr)

with the crossed product in the definition of its body
\,\widetilde{{\rm ISO}}(d,1)\,
determined by the vector realisation

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.40279pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 34.85553pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{:\ }$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 77.08604pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Spin}(d,1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 228.1097pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 120.54291pt\raise-24.0944pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.52084pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{\rm Spin}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 160.3071pt\raise-29.20274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 173.53366pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 228.1097pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm End}\bigl({\mathbb{R}}^{d,1}\bigr)}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-37.20274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 38.74442pt\raise-37.20274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 98.02184pt\raise-37.20274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 153.41037pt\raise-37.20274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm SO}_{0}(d,1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 193.14304pt\raise-29.20274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 236.127pt\raise-8.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 250.77446pt\raise-37.20274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

of the spin group, and the structure sheaf
\,\mathcal{O}_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\,
written in terms of the structure sheaf
\,C^{\infty}(\cdot,{\mathbb{R}})\circ{\rm pr}_{1}\otimes C^{\infty}(\cdot,{\mathbb{R}})\circ{\rm pr}_{2}\equiv\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{{\rm ISO}}(d,1)}\,
of the body. The binary operation

\displaystyle{\rm m}\ :\ {\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\longrightarrow{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})

of the Lie-supergroup structure on
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
is customarily described, in the
\mathcal{S}
-point picture, in terms of the anticommuting generators
\,\theta^{\alpha},\ \alpha\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}\,
of
\,\bigwedge\hskip-3.0pt{}^{\bullet}\,S_{d,1}
, the global (cartesian-coordinate) generators
\,x^{a},\ a\in\overline{0,d}\,
of the structure sheaf of
\,{\mathbb{R}}^{\times d+1}
, and local (Lie-algebra) coordinates
\,\phi^{ab}\equiv\phi^{[ab]}\,
on
\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)\,
(their ensemble being identified with a point in the spin group by a mild abuse of the notation) as

(2.2)

\displaystyle{\rm m}\bigl(\bigl(\theta_{1}^{\alpha},x_{1}^{a},\phi_{1}^{bc}\bigr),\bigl(\theta_{2}^{\alpha},x_{2}^{a},\phi_{2}^{bc}\bigr)\bigr)=\bigl(\theta_{1}^{\alpha}+S(\phi_{1})^{\alpha}_{\ \beta}\,\theta_{2}^{\beta},x_{1}^{a}+L(\phi_{1})^{a}_{\ b}\,x_{2}^{b}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,S(\phi_{1})\,\theta_{2},\bigl(\phi_{1}\star\phi_{2}\bigr)^{ab}\bigr)\,,

(2.3)

where

\displaystyle\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,S(\phi_{1})\,\theta_{2}\equiv\theta_{1}^{\alpha}\,C_{\alpha\beta}\,\Gamma{}^{a\,\beta}_{\ \ \ \gamma}\,S(\phi_{1})^{\gamma}_{\ \delta}\,\theta_{2}^{\delta}

and where
\,\star\,
represents the standard binary operation on the spin group. In this picture, it is straightforward to write out coordinate expressions for the basis LI vector fields on the Lie supergroup
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
,

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha}(\theta,x,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S(\phi)^{\beta}_{\ \alpha}\,\bigl(\tfrac{\vec{\partial}\ }{\partial\theta^{\beta}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta^{\gamma}\,C_{\gamma\delta}\,\Gamma^{a\,\delta}_{\ \ \ \beta}\,\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial x^{a}}\bigr)=:S(\phi)^{\beta}_{\ \alpha}\,\underline{Q}{}_{\beta}(\theta,x)\,,

(2.5)

\displaystyle P_{a}(\theta,x,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L(\phi)^{b}_{\ a}\,\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial x^{b}}=:L(\phi)^{b}_{\ a}\,\underline{P}{}_{b}(\theta,x)\,,

(2.7)

\displaystyle J_{ab}(\theta,x,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{{\mathsf{d}}\ }{{\mathsf{d}}t}\mathord{\restriction}_{t=0}\phi\star t\phi_{ab}\,,\qquad\qquad\bigl(\phi_{ab}\bigr)^{cd}=\delta_{a}^{\ c}\,\delta_{b}^{\ d}-\delta_{a}^{\ d}\,\delta_{b}^{\ c}\,,

spanning the tangent sheaf
\,\mathcal{T}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
of
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. These obey the superalgebra

\displaystyle\{Q_{\alpha},Q_{\beta}\}=\overline{\Gamma}{}_{\alpha\beta}^{a}\,P_{a}\,,\qquad\qquad[P_{a},P_{b}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[Q_{\alpha},P_{a}]=0\,,

(2.8)

(2.9)

\displaystyle[J_{ab},Q_{\alpha}]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl(Q\,\Gamma_{ab}\bigr){}_{\alpha}=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{ab}{}^{\beta}_{\ \alpha}\,Q_{\beta}\,,\qquad\qquad[J_{ab},P_{c}]=\eta_{bc}\,P_{a}-\eta_{ac}\,P_{b}\,,

(2.10)

(2.11)

\displaystyle[J_{ab},J_{cd}]=\eta_{ad}\,J_{bc}-\eta_{ac}\,J_{bd}+\eta_{bc}\,J_{ad}-\eta_{bd}\,J_{ac}\,,

expressed in terms of the antisymmetric products

\displaystyle\Gamma_{ab}=\tfrac{1}{2}\,[\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b}]

and called the super-Poincaré (super)algebra and denoted as (
D={\rm dim}\,\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})-1
)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle Q_{\alpha}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,\left\langle P_{a}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{a<b=0}^{d}\,\left\langle J_{ab}=-J_{ba}\right\rangle\equiv\bigoplus_{A=0}^{D}\,\left\langle t_{A}\right\rangle\,.

When referring symbolically to its (supercommutation) structure equations, we shall write (for homogeneous generators
\,t_{A},\ A\in\overline{0,D}\,
of the respective Graßmann parities
\,|t_{A}|\equiv|A|
)

\displaystyle[t_{A},t_{B}\}=f_{AB}^{\ \ \ C}\,t_{C}=(-1)^{|A|\,|B|+1}\,[t_{B},t_{A}\}\,.

The latter superalgebra is the key ingredient in the alternative (and equivalent) definition of the Lie supergroup
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
à la Kostant that identifies the supergroup with the super-Harish–Chandra pair

\displaystyle{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\equiv\bigl(\widetilde{{\rm ISO}}(d,1),\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)\,,

with the body Lie group
\,\widetilde{{\rm ISO}}(d,1)\,
realised on the Graßmann-odd component

\displaystyle\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{(1)}\equiv\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle Q_{\alpha}\right\rangle

of the Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
as

\displaystyle\rho\ :\ {\mathbb{R}}^{\times d+1}\rtimes_{L}{\rm Spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\rm End}\bigl(\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{(1)}\bigr)\ :\ (x,\phi)\longmapsto S(\phi)^{\rm T}\equiv\rho(x,\phi)\,.

The cotangent sheaf
\,\mathcal{T}^{*}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
of
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
, dual to
\,\mathcal{T}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
(as a
\,\mathcal{O}_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}
-module), is globally generated by the duals of the vector fields
\,Q_{\alpha},\ P_{a},\ J_{bc}
,
i.e.
, the LI super-1-forms with the coordinate presentation

\displaystyle q^{\alpha}(\theta,x,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S(\phi)^{-1\,\alpha}_{\ \ \ \ \beta}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta^{\beta}=:S(\phi)^{-1\,\alpha}_{\ \ \ \ \beta}\,\underline{q}{}^{\beta}(\theta,x)\,,

(2.13)

\displaystyle p^{a}(\theta,x,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L(\phi)^{-1\,a}_{\ \ \ \ b}\,\bigl({\mathsf{d}}x^{b}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{b}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\bigr)=:L(\phi)^{-1\,a}_{\ \ \ \ b}\,\underline{p}{}^{b}(\theta,x)\,,

(2.15)

\displaystyle j^{ab}(\theta,x,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L(\phi)^{-1\,a}_{\ \ \ \ c}\,{\mathsf{d}}L(\phi)^{c}_{\ d}\,\eta^{-1\,db}\,.

Through the ensuing super-Maurer–Cartan equations

(2.16)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}q^{\alpha}=-\tfrac{1}{4}\,j^{ab}\wedge\bigl(\Gamma_{ab}\,q\bigr)^{\alpha}\,,\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{d}}p^{a}=\tfrac{1}{2}\,q\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,q-\eta_{bc}\,j^{ab}\wedge p^{c}\,,\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{d}}j^{ab}=-\eta_{cd}\,j^{ac}\wedge j^{bd}\,,

(2.17)

they generate the Cartan–Eilenberg cochain complex of
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
,

\displaystyle\bigl(\Omega^{\bullet}\bigl({\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)^{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\equiv\left\langle\ q^{\alpha},p^{a},j^{bc}\ |\ (\alpha,a)\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}\times\overline{0,d}\,,\ b<c\in\overline{0,d}\ \right\rangle,{\mathsf{d}}^{\bullet}\equiv{\mathsf{d}}\bigr)\,.

Its cohomology,

\displaystyle H_{\rm dR}^{\bullet}\bigl({\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1}),{\mathbb{R}}\bigr)^{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\equiv{\rm CaE}^{\bullet}\bigl({\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)\,,

the supersymmetric refinement of the de Rham cohomology of
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
, is termed the Cartan–Eilenberg cohomology of
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. By the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded version of the classic Lie-algebraic result, it is isomorphic with the Chevalley–Eilenberg cohomology of the Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
with values in the trivial
\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})
-module
\,{\mathbb{R}}
,

\displaystyle{\rm CaE}^{\bullet}\bigl({\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)\cong H^{\bullet}\bigl(\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1}),{\mathbb{R}}\bigr)\,,

a fact of prime significance for the geometrisation of physically relevant Cartan–Eilenberg (super-)cocyles discussed in Sec.
4
. Among nontrivial classes in
\,{\rm CaE}^{\bullet}({\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1}))
, we find that of the the Green–Schwarz super-3-cocycle

(2.18)

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\chi}=q\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,q\wedge p^{a}

whose closedness follows directly from the Fierz identities (
2.1
).

Consider, next, the two homogeneous spaces of the super-Poincaré group associated with the respective reductive decompositions of its tangent Lie superalgebra:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})=\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)\,,

(2.19)

(2.20)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{spin}(d,1),\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})]\subset\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,,

with

\displaystyle\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle Q_{\alpha}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,\left\langle P_{a}\right\rangle\,,\qquad\qquad\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)=\bigoplus_{a<b=0}^{d}\,\left\langle J_{ab}\right\rangle\,,

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})=\bigl(\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\mathfrak{d}\bigr)\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,,

(2.21)

(2.22)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac},\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\mathfrak{d}]\subset\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\mathfrak{d}\,,

with

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}=\bigoplus_{(\underline{a},\widehat{b})\in\{0,1\}\times\overline{2,d}}\,\left\langle J_{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}\right\rangle\,,\qquad\qquad\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}=\left\langle J_{01}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\widehat{a}<\widehat{b}\in\overline{2,d}}\,\left\langle J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}\right\rangle\equiv\mathfrak{spin}(1,1)\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d-1)\,,

coming with the supervector-space projections

\displaystyle p\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ \mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,,

(2.24)

\displaystyle p_{\rm vac}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ \bigl(\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\mathfrak{d}\bigr)\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\mathfrak{d}\,.

The former is the super-Minkowski space

\displaystyle{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})={\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)\,,

and the latter is

\displaystyle{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\equiv{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,,

where

\displaystyle{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\equiv{\rm Spin}(1,1)\times{\rm Spin}(d-1)\,.

They are bases of the respective principal (super-)bundles

(2.25)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.93579pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.93579pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Spin}(d,1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.61491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 66.61491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\equiv{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\rtimes_{L,S}{\rm Spin}(d,1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 180.52469pt\raise-25.33955pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.048pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 180.52469pt\raise-42.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-50.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 139.60402pt\raise-50.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

and

(2.26)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 29.84207pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.84207pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 102.97437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 102.97437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 140.97836pt\raise-25.33955pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{\rm vac}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 140.97836pt\raise-42.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-50.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 72.5212pt\raise-50.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

on which the Lie supergroup acts (from the left) as

\displaystyle[\ell]^{\rm K}\ :\ {\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm K}\longrightarrow{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm K}

in such a manner that

\displaystyle[\ell]^{\rm K}\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\times\pi_{\rm K}\bigr)=\pi_{\rm K}\circ\ell\,,\qquad\qquad\ell\equiv{\rm m}\,,

where
\,{\rm K}\in\{{\rm Spin}(d,1),{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\}\,
and
\,(\pi_{{\rm Spin}(d,1)},\pi_{{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}})=(\pi,\pi_{\rm vac})
. This is a simple example of the general situation described at length in Ref.
[
Kos77
]
and, more recently, in Ref.
[
FLV07
]
. The Lie supergroup
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
acquires the interpretation of the
supersymmetry group
in this context, and its tangent Lie superalgebra becomes the
supersymmetry algebra
. It acts on itself also from the
right
,

\displaystyle\wp\equiv{\rm m}\ :\ {\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\longrightarrow{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})

but the latter action does not descend to the homogeneous space.

The existence of the surjective submersions (in
\,{\rm{\bf sMan}}
)
\,\pi_{\rm K}\,
can also be used to descend LI tensors from the mother Lie supergroup
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
to the homogeneous space
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm K}\,
along local trivialising sections, an observation behind the long-established model-building technique of the so-called nonlinear realisations of (super)symmetry of Refs.
[
Sch67
,
Wei68
,
CWZ69
,
CCWZ69
,
SS69a
,
SS69b
,
ISS71
,
VA72
,
VA73
,
IK78
,
LR79
,
UZ82
,
IK82
,
SW83
,
FMW83
,
BW84
,
Wes00
,
GKW06a
,
McA00
,
McA10
]
. In the case of a covariant tensor
\,T\,
(of rank
n
), for the descent to yield a globally defined object on the homogeneous space,
\,T\,
must be (right-)
{\rm K}
-basic. Denote the tangent Lie algebra of the structure group
\,{\rm K}\,
as
\,\mathfrak{k}\in\{\mathfrak{spin}(d,1),\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\}
, with the understanding that
\,\mathfrak{k}\subset\mathfrak{m}\oplus\mathfrak{k}=\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
(where
\,\mathfrak{m}\in\{\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1}),\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\mathfrak{d}\}\,
is the formerly indicated direct-sum complement of
\,\mathfrak{k}
, such that
\,[\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{m}]\subset\mathfrak{m}
), and consider the induced
element
wise realisation of the body Lie group
\,\widetilde{{\rm ISO}}(d,1)\,
on
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
by automorphisms in the category
\,{\rm{\bf sMan}}\,
of supermanifolds, given by

\displaystyle|\wp|_{\cdot}\ :\ \widetilde{{\rm ISO}}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\rm Aut}_{{\rm{\bf sMan}}}\bigl({\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)\ :\ g\longmapsto\wp\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\times\widehat{g}\bigr)\equiv|\wp|_{g}\,,

where

\displaystyle\widehat{g}\in{\rm Hom}_{\rm{\bf sMan}}\bigl({\mathbb{R}}^{0|0},{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)

is the topological point in
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
corresponding to
\,g\in\widetilde{{\rm ISO}}(d,1)
, whence

(2.28)

\displaystyle|\wp|_{g}\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ {\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\equiv{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\xrightarrow{\ {\rm id}_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\times\widehat{g}\ }{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\ {\rm m}\ }{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,,

as desired. With these in hand, we can make the concept of
{\rm K}
-basicness precise. Thus, a covariant tensor
\,T\,
on
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
is (right-)
{\rm K}
-basic if it is
\mathfrak{k}
-horizontal,

\displaystyle\forall_{(X_{1},X_{2},\ldots,X_{n})\in\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{\times n}}\ :\ \bigl(\ \exists_{i\in\overline{1,n}}\ :\ X_{i}\in\mathfrak{k}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad T(X_{1},X_{2},\ldots,X_{n})=0\ \bigr)\,,

and (right-)
{\rm K}
-invariant,

\displaystyle\forall_{(k,X)\in{\rm K}\times\mathfrak{k}}\ :\ \bigl(\ |\wp|_{k}^{*}T=T\quad\land\quad\,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{X}T=0\ \bigr)\,.

It is now easy to see that the distinguished super-3-cocycle (
2.18
) is
{\rm Spin}(d,1)
-basic, and so also
{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
-basic, as is the degenerate metric tensor

\displaystyle\widehat{\eta}=\eta_{ab}\,p^{a}\otimes p^{b}\,.

Indeed, the LI super-1-forms
\,\theta_{\rm L}^{\zeta},\ \zeta\in\overline{0,{\rm dim}\,\mathfrak{m}-1}\,
dual to the basis LI vector fields from
\,\mathfrak{m}\,
are
\mathfrak{k}
-horizontal by definition and transform linearly as

\displaystyle|\wp|_{k^{-1}}^{*}\theta_{\rm L}^{\zeta}=\rho(k)^{\zeta}_{\ \zeta^{\prime}}\,\theta_{\rm L}^{\zeta^{\prime}}

in consequence of the assumed reductivity of the decomposition
\,\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})=\mathfrak{m}\oplus\mathfrak{k}
. Hence, it suffices to take a linear combination of their tensor products,

\displaystyle T=\lambda_{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}\ldots\zeta_{n}}\,\theta_{\rm L}^{\zeta_{1}}\otimes\theta_{\rm L}^{\zeta_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\theta_{\rm L}^{\zeta_{n}}\,,

with components
\,\lambda_{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}\ldots\zeta_{n}}\,
of a constant
\rho({\rm K})
-invariant tensor as coefficients,

\displaystyle\forall_{k\in{\rm K}}\ :\ \lambda_{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}\ldots\zeta_{n}}\,\rho(k)^{\zeta_{1}}_{\ \zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\,\rho(k)^{\zeta_{2}}_{\ \zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\,\cdots\,\rho(k)^{\zeta_{n}}_{\ \zeta_{n}^{\prime}}=\lambda_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}\zeta_{2}^{\prime}\ldots\zeta_{n}^{\prime}}\,,

to obtain a
{\rm K}
-basic tensor
\,T
. The
\rho({\rm Spin}(d,1))
-invariance of the Minkowski metric is obvious, and that of
\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a\,\alpha\beta}\,
follows straightforwardly from the elementary properties of the generators of the Clifford algebra and of the charge-conjugation operator:

(2.29)

\displaystyle S(\phi)^{-1}\,\Gamma^{a}\,S(\phi)=L(\phi)^{a}_{\ b}\,\Gamma^{b}\,,\qquad\qquad C^{-1}\,S(\phi)^{\rm T}\,C=S(\phi)^{-1}\,.

An example of a
{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
-basic tensor that is
not

{\rm Spin}(d,1)
-basic is provided by the volume super-2-form on the subspace

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}:=\left\langle P_{0},P_{1}\right\rangle\,,

that is (
\epsilon_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,
is the totally antisymmetric tensor with
\,\epsilon_{01}=1
)

\displaystyle{\rm Vol}\bigl(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}\bigr)=p^{0}\wedge p^{1}\equiv\tfrac{1}{2}\,\epsilon_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,p^{\underline{a}}\wedge p^{\underline{b}}\,.

Its (right-)
{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
-invariance is ensured by the fact that
\,\rho({\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac})\,
restrict to
\,\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}\,
as unimodular automorphisms,

\displaystyle\forall_{\phi\equiv(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})\in{\rm Spin}(1,1)\times{\rm Spin}(d-1)}\ :\ {\rm det}\,\bigl(\rho(\phi)\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}}\bigr)\equiv{\rm det}\,\bigl(L(\phi)\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}}\bigr)\equiv{\rm det}\,L(\phi_{1})=1\,.

Hence, in particular, there exist: a super-3-form
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{{\rm H}}\,
and a symmetric
(2,0)
-tensor
\,\widehat{\underline{\eta}}\,
on
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
, as well as a super-3-form
\,\widetilde{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{{\rm H}}}
, a super-2-form
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\upsilon}\,
and a symmetric
(2,0)
-tensor
\,\widehat{\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}}\,
on
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
such that

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\chi}=\pi^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{{\rm H}}\,,\qquad\qquad\widehat{\eta}=\pi^{*}\widehat{\underline{\eta}}\,,

(2.30)

(2.31)

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\chi}=\pi_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{{\rm H}}}\,,\qquad\qquad\widehat{\eta}=\pi_{\rm vac}^{*}\widehat{\underline{\widetilde{\eta}}}\,,\qquad\qquad{\rm Vol}\bigl(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}\bigr)=\pi_{\rm vac}^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\upsilon}\,.

Upon putting together the explicit coordinate presentations of the various LI super-1-forms involved and identities (
2.29
), we readily derive

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{{\rm H}}=\underline{q}\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\underline{q}\wedge\underline{p}^{a}\,,\qquad\qquad\widehat{\underline{\eta}}=\eta_{ab}\,\underline{p}^{a}\otimes\underline{p}^{b}\,.

Inspection of the group law (
2.3
) reveals that the homogeneous space
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\subset{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
is, in fact, a Lie sub-supergroup with the binary operation

\displaystyle\underline{{\rm m}}\ :\ {\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\longrightarrow{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})

admitting the coordinate presentation

\displaystyle\underline{{\rm m}}\bigl(\bigl(\theta_{1}^{\alpha},x_{1}^{a}\bigr),\bigl(\theta_{2}^{\alpha},x_{2}^{a}\bigr)\bigr)=\bigl(\theta_{1}^{\alpha}+\theta_{2}^{\alpha},x_{1}^{a}+x_{2}^{a}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\theta_{2}\bigr)\,,

with the corresponding basis LI vector fields

\displaystyle\underline{Q}{}_{\alpha}(\theta,x)=\tfrac{\vec{\partial}\ }{\partial\theta^{\alpha}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta^{\beta}\,C_{\beta\gamma}\,\Gamma^{a\,\gamma}_{\ \ \ \alpha}\,\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial x^{a}}\,,\qquad\qquad\underline{P}{}_{a}(\theta,x)=\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial x^{a}}

spanning the super-minkowskian Lie superalgebra

\displaystyle\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\underline{Q}{}_{\alpha}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,\left\langle\underline{P}{}_{a}\right\rangle

with the structure equations

\displaystyle\{\underline{Q}{}_{\alpha},\underline{Q}{}_{\beta}\}=\overline{\Gamma}{}_{\alpha\beta}^{a}\,\underline{P}{}_{a}\,,\qquad\qquad[\underline{P}{}_{a},\underline{P}{}_{b}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\underline{Q}{}_{\alpha},\underline{P}{}_{a}]=0\,.

Clearly, the super-1-forms
\,\underline{q}{}^{\alpha}\,
and
\,\underline{p}{}^{a}\,
are their (respective) duals, and the descended Green–Schwarz super-3-cocycle
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{{\rm H}}\,
defines a nontrivial class in

\displaystyle{\rm CaE}^{\bullet}\bigl({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)\cong H^{\bullet}\bigl(\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1}),{\mathbb{R}}\bigr)\,.

As the de Rham cohomology of
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
is trivial,

\displaystyle H^{\bullet}_{\rm dR}\bigl({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)=H^{\bullet}_{\rm dR}\bigl({\rm Mink}(d,1)\bigr)={\boldsymbol{0}}\,,

the Green–Schwarz super-3-cocycle admits a global primitive, albeit only a quasi-invariant one that can be chosen in the explicit form

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\rm B}}(\theta,x)=\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\underline{q}(\theta,x)\wedge\underline{p}{}^{a}(\theta,x)\,.

We shall write

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\beta}:=\pi^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\rm B}}\,.

The hitherto discussion provides us with all the ingredients of the two formulations of the
Green–Schwarz (GS) super-
\sigma
-model of the superstring in
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. The first of these is the
Nambu–Goto
(NG)
formulation
in which we have the theory of (inner-
{\rm Hom}
) functorial embeddings

\displaystyle\xi\in[\Sigma,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})]\equiv{\rm Hom}_{\rm{\bf sMan}}\bigl(\Sigma\times-,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)

of a closed orientable two-dimensional manifold
\,\Sigma\,
(the worldsheet) in
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
determined by the principle of least action applied to the Dirac–Feynman (DF) amplitude (with an obvious interpretation of the codomain)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\rm DF}^{\rm(NG)}\ :\ [\Sigma,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})]\longrightarrow{\rm U}(1)\ :\ \xi\longmapsto{\rm exp}\left[\tfrac{{\mathsf{i}}}{\hbar}\,\left(\mu_{1}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\sqrt{{\rm det}\,\bigl(\xi^{*}\widehat{\underline{\eta}}\bigr)}+\int_{\Sigma}\,\xi^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\rm B}}\right)\right]\,,

in which
\,\mu_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}\,
is a parameter whose numerical value is fixed by the correspondence with the other formulation stated below,
cp
Refs.
[
GS84b
,
GS84a
]
. The amplitude is to be evaluated on the Graßmann-odd hyperplanes
\,{\mathbb{R}}^{0|N},\ N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\times}
, whereby an
{\mathbb{N}}^{\times}
-indexed family of supersymmetric two-dimensional field theories is obtained,
cp
Ref.
[
Fre99
]
. The other one is the
Hughes–Polchinski
(HP)
formulation
, first postulated in
[
HP86
]
, developed in Refs.
[
GIT90
]
, applied in Refs.
[
McA00
,
Wes00
,
GKW06a
,
GKW06b
,
McA10
]
and elaborated in
[
Sus19
,
Sus20
]
, in which we deal with the theory of (inner-
{\rm Hom}
) functorial embeddings

\displaystyle\widetilde{\xi}\in\bigl[\Sigma,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\bigr]\equiv{\rm Hom}_{\rm{\bf sMan}}\bigl(\Sigma\times-,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\bigr)

of the same worldsheet
\,\Sigma\,
in
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
determined by the principle of least action applied to the DF amplitude

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\rm DF}^{\rm(HP)}\ :\ \bigl[\Sigma,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\bigr]\longrightarrow{\rm U}(1)\ :\ \widetilde{\xi}\longmapsto{\rm exp}\left[\tfrac{{\mathsf{i}}}{\hbar}\,\int_{\Sigma}\,\widetilde{\xi}{}^{*}\bigl(\lambda_{1}\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\upsilon}+p_{1}^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\rm B}}\bigr)\right]\,,

in which

\displaystyle p_{1}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ {\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times\bigl({\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\bigr)\longrightarrow{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})

and
\,\lambda_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}\,
is a parameter whose numerical value we establish through a symmetry analysis in Sec.
3
. Here, it is presupposed that
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\upsilon}\,
is nondegenerate on classical field configurations (termed the vacua of the theory). In other words, we model the body of the vacuum on
\,\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}
. In virtue of Ref.
[
Sus19
, Prop. 5.3]
(
cp
also Ref.
[
Sus20
, Thm. 3.4]
for a more general result), the two formulations become equivalent upon partial reduction of the latter one through imposition of a subset of its Euler–Lagrange equations. This is quite nontrivial as the super-
\sigma
-model in the HP formulation is purely topological, unlike its NG counterpart. A careful analysis of the equivalence between the two formulations sets the stage for all our subsequent higher-geometric considerations, and so we shall now spend some time studying the relevant details in the spirit of Ref.
[
Sus20
]
. In so doing, we shall refer to the two pairs:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(NG)}}_{1}=\bigl({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1}),\widehat{\eta},\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\chi}\bigr)\,,

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,\lambda_{1}}=\bigl({\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac},\lambda_{1}\,{\mathsf{d}}{\rm Vol}\bigl(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}\bigr)+\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\chi}\equiv\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\widehat{\chi}}{}^{(\lambda_{1})}\bigr)

as the
NG superbackground
and
HP superbackground
, respectively.

The key to understanding the equivalence lies in a patchwise smooth realisation of the two homogeneous spaces:
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
and
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)\,
within the mother Lie supergroup
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
by means of judiciously chosen local sections of the respective principal bundles (
2.26
) and (
2.25
), along the lines of Refs.
[
FLV07
]
and
[
Sus20
]
. Let
\,O_{0}\ni e\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
be an open subset of
\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
that supports local (normal) coordinates
\,(\phi^{\underline{a}\widehat{b}})\ :\ O_{0}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }V_{0}\subset\mathfrak{d}\,
centred on the unital coset
\,e\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
(
i.e.
,
\,\phi^{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}(e\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac})=0
) and a local section of the principal
{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
-bundle
\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
(and so also its local trivialisation), and let
\,\{h_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subset{\rm Spin}(d,1),\ I\ni 0\,
(with
\,h_{0}\equiv e
) be such that the translates
\,O_{i}=[|l|]_{h_{i}}(O_{0})
, written in terms of the induced action

\displaystyle[l]\ :\ {\rm Spin}(d,1)\times{\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\longrightarrow{\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}

of the Lie group
\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)\,
on the homogeneous space
\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
, satisfying

\displaystyle[l]\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm Spin}(d,1)}\times\pi_{{\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}}\bigr)=\pi_{{\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}}\circ l\,,\qquad\qquad l\equiv\star\,,

cover
\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
,

\displaystyle\bigcup_{i\in I}\,O_{i}={\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,.

The sets
\,U_{i}:={\rm Mink}(d,1)\times O_{i},\ i\in I\,
compose an open cover of the base
\,{\rm Mink}(d,1)\times{\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
over which the principal
{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
-bundle
\,\widetilde{{\rm ISO}}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\rm Mink}(d,1)\times{\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
trivialises. That base is the body of the base
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
of the principal
{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
-bundle (
2.26
), and it is over the
\,U_{i}\,
that we define local sections of the latter. These we take in the (shifted-)exponential form
7
7

7

The sheaf-theoretic meaning of the
\,\sigma^{\rm vac}_{i}\,
was given in Ref.
[
Sus20
, Sec. 2]
.

(2.32)

\displaystyle\sigma^{\rm vac}_{i}=|\ell|_{h_{i}}\circ{\rm e}^{\theta^{\alpha}\circ{\rm pr}_{1}\otimes Q_{\alpha}}\cdot{\rm e}^{x^{a}\circ{\rm pr}_{1}\otimes P_{a}}\cdot{\rm e}^{\phi^{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}\circ{\rm pr}_{2}\otimes J_{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}}\circ[|\ell|]_{h_{i}^{-1}}\ :\ \mathcal{U}^{\rm vac}_{i}\equiv\bigl(U_{i},\mathcal{O}_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}}\mathord{\restriction}_{U_{i}}\bigr)

(2.33)

(2.34)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,,

defined in terms of the left action

\displaystyle|\ell|_{\cdot}\ :\ \widetilde{{\rm ISO}}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\rm Aut}_{{\rm{\bf sMan}}}\bigl({\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)\ :\ g\longmapsto{\rm m}\circ\bigl(\widehat{g}\times{\rm id}_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\bigr)\equiv|\ell|_{g}

and the corresponding induced action

(2.36)

\displaystyle[|\ell|]_{\cdot}\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ \widetilde{{\rm ISO}}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\rm Aut}_{{\rm{\bf sMan}}}\bigl({\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\bigr)

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ g\longmapsto[\ell]\circ\bigl(\widehat{g}\times{\rm id}_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}}\bigr)\equiv[|\ell|]_{g}\,.

They give rise to the
Hughes–Polchinski section

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\rm HP}:=\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,\mathcal{V}_{i}\,,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{V}_{i}:=\sigma^{\rm vac}_{i}\bigl(\mathcal{U}_{i}^{\rm vac}\bigr)\,.

Upon choosing a tessellation
\,\triangle_{\Sigma}\,
of
\,\Sigma\,
(composed of plaquettes that make up a set
\,\mathfrak{T}_{2}\subset\triangle_{\Sigma}
, edges and vertices) subordinate to
\,\{\mathcal{U}^{\rm vac}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
,

\displaystyle\forall_{\tau\in\mathfrak{T}_{2}}\ \exists_{i_{\tau}\in I}\ :\ |\widetilde{\xi}|(\tau)\subset U_{i_{\tau}}\,,

we may, next, rewrite the DF amplitude of the above GS super-
\sigma
-model in the HP formulation as (
\widetilde{\xi}_{\tau}\equiv\widetilde{\xi}\mathord{\restriction}_{\tau}
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\rm DF}^{\rm(HP)}[\widetilde{\xi}]={\rm exp}\left[\tfrac{{\mathsf{i}}}{\hbar}\,\sum_{\tau\in\mathfrak{T}_{2}}\,\int_{\tau}\,\widetilde{\xi}{}_{\tau}^{*}\sigma^{{\rm vac}*}_{i_{\tau}}\bigl(\lambda_{1}\,{\rm Vol}\bigl(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}\bigr)+\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\beta}\bigr)\right]\,.

With the degrees of freedom of the super-
\sigma
-model (super)field thus separated into the super-minkowskian sector
\,(\theta^{\alpha},x^{a})\,
and the spin-group sector
\,(\phi^{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}_{i})\,
(we use the subscript to mark the local coordinates on
\,O_{i}
), the equivalence is completely straightforward to state: Upon expressing the non-dynamical Goldstone spin-group fields
\,(\phi^{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}_{i})\,
in
\,\mathcal{A}_{\rm DF}^{\rm(HP)}\,
in terms of the remaining degrees of freedom
\,(\theta^{\alpha},x^{a})\,
as dictated by the Euler–Lagrange equations of the GS super-
\sigma
-model in the HP formulation obtained by varying the DF amplitude for
\,\widetilde{\xi}\,
in the direction of the spin-group fields
\,(\phi^{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}_{i})
,

(2.37)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\xi}{}_{\tau}^{*}\sigma^{{\rm vac}*}_{i_{\tau}}p^{\widehat{a}}=0\,,\qquad\widehat{a}\in\overline{2,d}\,,

we recover the DF amplitude of the NG formulation for
\,p_{1}\circ\widetilde{\xi}
, written in terms of the global coordinates
\,(\theta^{\alpha},x^{a})\,
on
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
for a value
\,\mu_{1\,*}\equiv\mu_{1}(\lambda_{1})\,
of the parameter
\,\mu_{1}
determined unquely by
\,\lambda_{1}\,
– this is the so-called
Inverse Higgs Effect
of Ref.
[
IO75
]
. It permits us to restrict our subsequent discussion to the HP formulation, with the understanding that conclusions pertinent to the standard NG formulation of the super-
\sigma
-model can be drawn only upon restricting the tangents of the field configurations
\,\sigma^{\rm vac}_{i_{\tau}}\circ\widetilde{\xi}\,
to the
NG/HP correspondence superdistribution

\displaystyle{\rm Corr}_{\rm HP}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,\lambda_{1}}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{T}\Sigma^{\rm HP}\,,\qquad\qquad{\rm Corr}_{\rm HP}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,\lambda_{1}}\bigr)\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}=\bigcap_{\widehat{a}=2}^{d}\,{\rm ker}\,p^{\widehat{a}}\cap\mathcal{T}\mathcal{V}_{i}\,.

Advantages of this approach shall become clear along the way.

We conclude the present field-theoretic introduction by deriving the Euler–Lagrange equations of the super-
\sigma
-model in the HP formulation. To this end, we write the variation
\,\mathcal{V}_{\tau}\in[\tau,\mathcal{T}\sigma^{{\rm vac}}_{i_{\tau}}(\mathcal{U}^{\rm vac}_{i_{\tau}})]\,
of the composite embedding
\,{\mathbb{X}}_{\tau}\equiv\sigma^{{\rm vac}}_{i_{\tau}}\circ\widetilde{\xi}{}_{\tau}\,
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\tau}=\delta\theta^{\alpha}\,Q_{\alpha}({\mathbb{X}}_{\tau})+\delta x^{a}\,P_{a}({\mathbb{X}}_{\tau})+\delta\phi^{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}_{i_{\tau}}\,J_{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\tau})+\Delta^{\underline{S}}_{\tau\,1}\,J_{\underline{S}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\tau})\,,

in which the last term

\displaystyle\Delta^{\underline{S}}_{\tau\,1}\,J_{\underline{S}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\tau})\equiv\Delta^{01}_{\tau\,1}\,J_{01}({\mathbb{X}}_{\tau})+\Delta^{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}_{\tau\,2}\,J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\tau})

represents
\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
-vertical corrections that render
\,\mathcal{V}_{\tau}\,
tangent to the local section
\,\sigma^{{\rm vac}}_{i_{\tau}}(\mathcal{U}^{\rm vac}_{i_{\tau}})
,
cp
Ref.
[
Sus20
, Prop. 3.6]
, and calculate, with the help of the super-Maurer–Cartan equations (
2.17
),

(2.39)

\displaystyle-{\mathsf{i}}\,\hbar\,\mathcal{V}_{\cdot}\righthalfcup\delta\log\,\mathcal{A}_{\rm DF}^{\rm(HP)}[\widetilde{\xi}]=\sum_{\tau\in\mathfrak{T}_{2}}\,\int_{\tau}\,\mathcal{V}_{\tau}\righthalfcup\widetilde{\xi}{}_{\tau}^{*}\sigma^{{\rm vac}*}_{i_{\tau}}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\widehat{\chi}}{}^{(\lambda_{1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau\in\mathfrak{T}_{2}}\,\int_{\tau}\,\mathcal{V}_{\tau}\righthalfcup\widetilde{\xi}{}_{\tau}^{*}\sigma^{{\rm vac}*}_{i_{\tau}}\bigl(-\lambda_{1}\,\epsilon_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,\delta_{\widehat{c}\widehat{d}}\,j^{\underline{a}\widehat{c}}\wedge p^{\widehat{d}}\wedge p^{\underline{b}}+q\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}_{\widehat{a}}\,q\wedge p^{\widehat{a}}+2\eta_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,q\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,\bigl(\tfrac{{\boldsymbol{1}}_{D_{d,1}}-\tfrac{\lambda_{1}}{2}\,\Gamma^{0}\,\Gamma^{1}}{2}\bigr)\,q\wedge p^{\underline{b}}\bigr)\,,

so that for
\,(\delta\theta^{\alpha},\delta x^{a})=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle-{\mathsf{i}}\,\hbar\,\mathcal{V}_{\cdot}\righthalfcup\delta\log\,\mathcal{A}_{\rm DF}^{\rm(HP)}[\widetilde{\xi}]=-\sum_{\tau\in\mathfrak{T}_{2}}\,\int_{\tau}\,\lambda_{1}\,\epsilon_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,\delta_{\widehat{c}\widehat{d}}\,\delta\phi^{\underline{a}\widehat{c}}_{i_{\tau}}\,\widetilde{\xi}{}_{\tau}^{*}\sigma^{{\rm vac}*}_{i_{\tau}}\bigl(p^{\widehat{d}}\wedge p^{\underline{b}}\bigr)\,.

At this stage, we invoke the assumption of nondegeneracy of the volume form
\,{\rm Vol}(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac})\,
in the vacuum (which can be viewed as a condition of its partial localisation), implying that the tangent sheaf of the latter (in
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
) is (locally) spanned on the vector fields

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\alpha\,i}=\Delta_{\alpha\,i}^{\beta}\,Q_{\beta}\,,\qquad\alpha\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}\,,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{W}_{\underline{a}\,i}=P_{\underline{a}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}+\Delta_{\underline{a}\,i}^{\widehat{b}}\,P_{\widehat{b}}+\Delta_{\underline{a}\,i}^{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}\,J_{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}+\Delta_{\underline{a}\,i}^{\underline{S}}\,J_{\underline{S}}\,,\qquad\underline{a}\in\{0,1\}\,,

written in terms of certain (even) sections
\,\Delta_{\alpha\,i}^{\beta},\Delta_{\underline{a}\,i}^{\widehat{b}},\Delta_{\underline{a}\,i}^{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}\,
and
\,\Delta_{\underline{a}\,i}^{\underline{S}}\,
of the structure sheaf of
\,\mathcal{V}_{i}\,
to be determined below, with the
\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
-vertical component correcting the one along
\,\mathfrak{d}\,
in such a way that the sum is in
\,\mathcal{T}\mathcal{V}_{i}
,
cp
Ref.
[
Sus20
, Prop. 3.6]
. Taking the above into account, we obtain the formerly stated Eq. (
2.37
), or

\displaystyle\Delta_{\underline{a}\,i}^{\widehat{b}}=0\,,\qquad(\underline{a},\widehat{b})\in\{0,1\}\times\overline{2,d}\,,

which we write as

(2.40)

\displaystyle p^{\widehat{a}}\approx 0\,,\qquad\widehat{a}\in\overline{2,d}

henceforth. Imposition of the latter leaves us with the reduced expression for the variation

\displaystyle-{\mathsf{i}}\,\hbar\,\mathcal{V}_{\cdot}\righthalfcup\delta\log\,\mathcal{A}_{\rm DF}^{\rm(HP)}[\widetilde{\xi}]=2\eta_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,\sum_{\tau\in\mathfrak{T}_{2}}\,\int_{\tau}\,\mathcal{V}_{\tau}\righthalfcup\widetilde{\xi}{}_{\tau}^{*}\sigma^{{\rm vac}*}_{i_{\tau}}\bigl(q\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,\bigl(\tfrac{{\boldsymbol{1}}_{D_{d,1}}-\tfrac{\lambda_{1}}{2}\,\Gamma^{0}\,\Gamma^{1}}{2}\bigr)\,q\wedge p^{\underline{b}}\bigr)\,,

from which we readily extract the remaining Euler–Lagrange equations (written in the above notation)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}_{\lambda_{1}}\,q\approx 0\,,\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{P}}_{\lambda_{1}}\equiv\tfrac{{\boldsymbol{1}}_{D_{d,1}}-\tfrac{\lambda_{1}}{2}\,\Gamma^{0}\,\Gamma^{1}}{2}

by setting
\,\delta x^{a}=0
. If (and only if)
\,\lambda_{1}\in\{-2,2\}
, then the operator
\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\lambda_{1}}\,
is a projector,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}^{2}={\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}

of rank

\displaystyle{\rm rk}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}=\tfrac{D_{d,1}}{2}\,,

with the additional properties

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\,\Gamma^{\underline{a}}=\Gamma^{\underline{a}}\,\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}_{D_{d,1}}-{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\,\Gamma^{\widehat{a}}=\Gamma^{\widehat{a}}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\,,\qquad\qquad C^{-1}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}^{\rm T}\,C={\boldsymbol{1}}_{D_{d,1}}-{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}

which leads to the emergence of a Lie sub-superalgebra

\displaystyle\bigl\{\bigl(Q\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\bigr)_{\alpha},\bigl(Q\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\bigr)_{\beta}\bigr\}=\bigl(\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\bigr)_{\alpha\beta}\,P_{\underline{a}}\,,\qquad\qquad[P_{\underline{a}},P_{\underline{b}}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad\bigl[\bigl(Q\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\bigr)_{\alpha},P_{\underline{a}}\bigr]=0\,,

(2.41)

(2.42)

\displaystyle[J_{01},J_{01}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[J_{01},J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},J_{\widehat{c}\widehat{d}}]=\delta_{\widehat{a}\widehat{d}}\,J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}-\delta_{\widehat{a}\widehat{c}}\,J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{d}}+\delta_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\,J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{d}}-\delta_{\widehat{b}\widehat{d}}\,J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{c}}\,,

(2.43)

(2.44)

\displaystyle\bigl[J_{01},\bigl(Q\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\bigr)_{\alpha}\bigr]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl(\bigl(Q\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\bigr)\,\Gamma_{01}\bigr)_{\alpha}\,,\qquad\qquad\bigl[J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},\bigl(Q\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\bigr)_{\alpha}\bigr]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl(\bigl(Q\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\bigr)\,\Gamma_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}\bigr)_{\alpha}\,,

(2.45)

(2.46)
