\displaystyle[J_{01},P_{\underline{a}}]=\eta_{\underline{a}1}\,P_{0}-\eta_{\underline{a}0}\,P_{1}\,,\qquad\qquad[J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},P_{\underline{a}}]=0

within
\,\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. The above emphasises the indispensability of the projector
\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 2}\,
for the consistency of the field theory under consideration, and so fixes the absolute value of
\,\lambda_{1}
, leaving us only the immaterial choice of its sign, which we declare to be “
+
”, with

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}:={\mathsf{P}}_{-2}

and

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\widehat{\chi}}\equiv\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\widehat{\chi}}{}^{(2)}=2\eta_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,q\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}_{D_{d,1}}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\bigr)\,q\wedge p^{\underline{b}}+2\epsilon_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,\delta_{\widehat{c}\widehat{d}}\,p^{\widehat{c}}\wedge p^{\underline{a}}\wedge j^{\underline{b}\widehat{d}}+q\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}_{\widehat{a}}\,q\wedge p^{\widehat{a}}\,.

Indeed, the projector enforces a reduction of the Graßmann-odd degrees of freedom necessary for the restoration of balance between them and their Graßmann-even counterparts in the vacuum, the latter being subject to the (even) Inverse Higgs Constraints (
2.40
). The Constraints are transmitted unto the Graßmann-odd sector
via
the anticommutator of supercharges in the supersymmetry superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})
, and so for the sake of a residual supersymmetry in the vacuum, we need a subspace in the odd component
\,\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{(1)}\subset\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
which the superbracket maps to the surviving Graßmann-even supersymmetries
\,P_{0}\,
and
\,P_{1}
. The ratio

\displaystyle{\rm BPS}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)=\tfrac{{\rm rk}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}}{D_{d,1}}\equiv\tfrac{1}{2}

goes under the name of the
BPS fraction of the vacuum
.

Upon fixing a basis
\,\{\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\}_{\underline{\alpha}\in\overline{1,\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}}\,
in
\,{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}{}^{\rm T}
,

\displaystyle{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}{}^{\rm T}=\left\langle\ Q_{\beta}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}{}^{\beta}_{\ \alpha}\ |\ \alpha\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}\ \right\rangle\equiv\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle\,,

we may write down (local) generators of the the
vacuum superdistribution

\displaystyle{\rm Vac}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\subset{\rm Corr}_{\rm HP}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{T}\Sigma^{\rm HP}

within the tangent sheaf
\,\mathcal{T}\Sigma^{\rm HP}\,
of the HP section
\,\Sigma^{\rm HP}\,
that is determined by the Euler–Lagrange equations of the super-
\sigma
-model,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\alpha}\,i}=\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\,,\qquad\alpha\in\overline{1,\tfrac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{W}_{\underline{a}\,i}=P_{\underline{a}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}+\Delta_{\underline{a}\,i}^{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}\,J_{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}+\Delta_{\underline{a}\,i}^{\underline{S}}\,J_{\underline{S}}\,,\qquad\underline{a}\in\{0,1\}\,.

In the light of the physical interpretation of the superdistribution, it is natural to demand involutivity of the latter, so that it defines – in virtue of the Frobenius Theorem, Ref.
[
CCF11
, Thm. 6.2.1]
– a foliation of the HP supertarget by embedded sub-supermanifolds, identified as the vacua of the supersymmetric field theory under consideration. Transform the matrices
\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,
and
\,\Gamma_{\underline{S}}
, commuting with
\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}
, into a basis of the Majorana-spinor module adapted to the decomposition of the dual module
\,\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{(1)}\,
into
\,{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)\,{\rm T}}\,
and its direct-sum complement,

\displaystyle{\rm im}\,\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}_{D_{d,1}}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\bigr)^{\rm T}\equiv\bigoplus_{\widehat{\alpha}=\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}+1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\widehat{Q}{}_{\widehat{\alpha}}\right\rangle\,,

whereupon they become block-diagonal, and denote the ensuing vacuum blocks as

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}}=:\bigl(\overline{\gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\bigr)_{\underline{\alpha},\underline{\beta}\in\overline{1,\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}}\equiv\overline{\gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,,\qquad\qquad{\rm det}\,\overline{\gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\neq 0\,,\qquad\qquad\overline{\gamma}{}^{1}=-\overline{\gamma}{}^{0}\,,

(2.47)

(2.48)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{01}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}}\equiv-{\boldsymbol{1}}_{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,,\qquad\qquad\Gamma_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}}=:\bigl(\gamma_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ \underline{\beta}}\bigr)_{\underline{\alpha},\underline{\beta}\in\overline{1,\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}}\equiv\gamma_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}\,.

Our discussion of involutivity of
\,{\rm Vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})\,
begins with the inspection of the anticommutators

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\alpha}\,i},\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\beta}\,i}\}=\overline{\gamma}{}^{0}_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\,(P_{0}-P_{1})\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\equiv\overline{\gamma}{}^{0}_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\,\bigl(\mathcal{W}_{0\,i}-\mathcal{W}_{1\,i}\bigr)+\overline{\gamma}{}^{0}_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\,\bigl(\Delta_{1\,i}^{ab}-\Delta_{0\,i}^{ab}\bigr)\,J_{a<b}

from which we read off the constraints

\displaystyle\Delta_{1\,i}^{ab}=\Delta_{0\,i}^{ab}\equiv\Delta_{i}^{ab}\,,\qquad a<b\in\overline{0,d}\,,

implying

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\underline{a}\,i}=P_{\underline{a}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}+\Delta_{i}^{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}\,J_{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}+\Delta_{i}^{\underline{S}}\,J_{\underline{S}}\,.

Next, we compute the commutators

\displaystyle[\mathcal{W}_{0\,i},\mathcal{W}_{1\,i}]=\Delta_{i}^{01}\,\bigl(\mathcal{W}_{0\,i}-\mathcal{W}_{1\,i}\bigr)-\bigl(\Delta_{i}^{0\widehat{a}}+\Delta_{i}^{1\widehat{a}}\bigr)\,P_{\widehat{a}}+(P_{0}-P_{1})\righthalfcup{\mathsf{d}}\Delta_{i}^{ab}\,J_{a<b}\,,

and infer the constraints

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}^{0\widehat{a}}=-\Delta_{i}^{1\widehat{a}}\equiv\Delta_{i}^{\widehat{a}}\,,\qquad\widehat{a}\in\overline{2,d}\,,\qquad\qquad(P_{0}-P_{1})\righthalfcup{\mathsf{d}}\Delta_{i}^{ab}=0\,,\qquad a<b\in\overline{0,d}\,.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\underline{a}\,i}=P_{\underline{a}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}+\Delta_{i}^{\widehat{b}}\,\bigl(J_{0\widehat{b}}-J_{1\widehat{b}}\bigr)+\Delta_{i}^{\underline{S}}\,J_{\underline{S}}\,,

with

\displaystyle(P_{0}-P_{1})\righthalfcup{\mathsf{d}}\Delta_{i}^{\widehat{b}}=0=(P_{0}-P_{1})\righthalfcup{\mathsf{d}}\Delta_{i}^{\underline{S}}\,.

Finally, we examine the commutators

\displaystyle[\mathcal{W}_{\underline{a}\,i},\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\alpha}\,i}]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Delta_{i}^{01}\,\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\alpha}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Delta_{i}^{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\,\gamma_{\widehat{b}<\widehat{c}}{}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\ \underline{\alpha}}\,\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\beta}}-\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\righthalfcup{\mathsf{d}}\Delta_{i}^{bc}\,J_{b<c}\,,

whereby we arrive at the constraints

\displaystyle\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\righthalfcup{\mathsf{d}}\Delta_{i}^{ab}=0\,,\qquad a<b\in\overline{0,d}\,.

We conclude that an
involutive
vacuum superdistribution (of the type assumed) is spanned on fields

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\alpha}\,i}=\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\,,\qquad\alpha\in\overline{1,\tfrac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{W}_{\underline{a}\,i}=P_{\underline{a}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}+\Delta_{i}^{\widehat{b}}\,\bigl(J_{0\widehat{b}}-J_{1\widehat{b}}\bigr)+\Delta_{i}^{\underline{S}}\,J_{\underline{S}}\,,

with

\displaystyle(P_{0}-P_{1})\righthalfcup{\mathsf{d}}\Delta_{i}^{\widehat{b}}=0=(P_{0}-P_{1})\righthalfcup{\mathsf{d}}\Delta_{i}^{\underline{S}}\,,\qquad\qquad\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\righthalfcup{\mathsf{d}}\Delta_{i}^{\widehat{b}}=0=\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\righthalfcup{\mathsf{d}}\Delta_{i}^{\underline{S}}\,.

Note that for the special choice

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}^{\widehat{b}}\equiv 0\qquad\bigl(\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\Delta_{i}^{\underline{S}}\equiv 0\bigr)

the vacuum superdistribution is modelled on the super-minkowskian component of the Lie sub-superalgebra

(2.49)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{vac}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)=\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle P_{0},P_{1}\right\rangle\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}

(the hidden gauge-symmetry algebra
\,\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
is realised trivially) with the superbrackets

\displaystyle\bigl\{\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\beta}}\bigr\}=\overline{\gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\,P_{\underline{a}}\equiv\overline{\gamma}{}^{0}_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\,(P_{0}-P_{1})\,,\qquad\qquad[P_{\underline{a}},P_{\underline{b}}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad\bigl[\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}},P_{\underline{a}}\bigr]=0\,,

(2.50)

(2.51)

\displaystyle[J_{01},J_{01}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[J_{01},J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},J_{\widehat{c}\widehat{d}}]=\delta_{\widehat{a}\widehat{d}}\,J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}-\delta_{\widehat{a}\widehat{c}}\,J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{d}}+\delta_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\,J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{d}}-\delta_{\widehat{b}\widehat{d}}\,J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{c}}\,,

(2.52)

(2.53)

\displaystyle\bigl[J_{01},\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\bigr]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\,,\qquad\qquad\bigl[J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\bigr]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl(\breve{Q}\,\gamma_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}\bigr)_{\underline{\alpha}}\,,

(2.54)

(2.55)

\displaystyle[J_{01},P_{\underline{a}}]=\delta_{\underline{a}1}\,P_{0}+\delta_{\underline{a}0}\,P_{1}\,,\qquad\qquad[J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},P_{\underline{a}}]=0

that we call the
vacuum algebra of the superstring in
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. In the next section, we shall interpret the departure from the simple model
\,\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})\,
in the structure of the vacuum superdistribution in terms of gauge symmetries of the field theory. In the meantime, we mark the presence of the above sheaf parameters
\,\Delta^{\widehat{b}}\,
and
\,\Delta^{\underline{S}}\,
(with restrictions
\,\Delta^{\widehat{b}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}=\Delta_{i}^{\widehat{b}}\,
and
\,\Delta^{\underline{S}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}=\Delta_{i}^{\underline{S}}
, respectively) in our notation as

\displaystyle{\rm Vac}_{(\Delta)}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\equiv\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\alpha}}\equiv\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\Sigma^{\rm HP}}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{a}\in\{0,1\}}\,\left\langle\mathcal{W}_{\underline{a}}\equiv P_{\underline{a}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\Sigma^{\rm HP}}+\Delta^{\widehat{b}}\,\bigl(J_{0\widehat{b}}-J_{1\widehat{b}}\bigr)+\Delta^{\underline{S}}\,J_{\underline{S}}\right\rangle\,.

The disjoint union of integral leaves
\,\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\subset\mathcal{V}_{i}\,
(indices
\,\upsilon_{i}\,
from an index set
\,\Upsilon_{i}\,
enumerate the different leaves within
\,\mathcal{V}_{i}
) of the involutive vacuum superdistribution shall be denoted as

(2.56)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\rm HP}_{\rm vac}=\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,\bigsqcup_{\upsilon_{i}\in\Upsilon_{i}}\,\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\,,

and termed the
Hughes–Polchinski vacuum foliation
. It is embedded in the HP section, which we write as

\displaystyle\iota_{\rm vac}\ :\ \Sigma^{\rm HP}_{\rm vac}\hookrightarrow\Sigma^{\rm HP}\,,

and projects to the
physical vacuum foliation

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\rm HP}_{\rm phys\ vac}\equiv\bigcup_{i\in I}\,\bigcup_{\upsilon_{i}\in\Upsilon_{i}}\,\pi_{\rm vac}\bigl(\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\bigr)\,.

An alternative interpretation of the vacuum superdistribution and the vacuum algebra shall be given in the next section.

3.
Supersymmetries of the super-
\sigma
-model

The principle of supersymmetry lies at the core of the construction of the super-
\sigma
-model. Its field-theoretic implementation has its peculiarities that we review below upon identifying the various species of supersymmetry present.

3.1.
Global supersymmetry

The GS super-
\sigma
-model in either formulation has a built-in global supersymmetry realised by the respective induced actions
\,[\ell]^{\rm K}\,
under which the integrand of the (metric) volume term is manifestly invariant (being defined in terms of the left invariant super-1-forms
\,p^{a}
), whereas that of the topological term is
quasi
-invariant,
i.e.
, invariant up to a total exterior derivative, as demonstrated explicitly (in the
\mathcal{S}
-point picture) in

\displaystyle[\ell]^{*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\rm B}}(\theta,x)=\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\rm B}}(\theta,x)+{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(S(\psi)^{-1}\,\varepsilon\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\theta\,\bigl({\mathsf{d}}x^{a}+\tfrac{1}{6}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\bigr)\bigr)\,,

whence the said invariance of the DF amplitude for
\,\Sigma\,
closed. The global supersymmetry of the field theory under consideration is reflected in the existence of a
\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})
-indexed family of Noether hamiltonians
\,\{h_{X}\}_{X\in\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\,
on its space of states. These we derive in the NG formulation in which a state is represented by the Cauchy data
\,\Psi\equiv((\theta,x)\mathord{\restriction}_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}}\equiv\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}},P)\,
(a configuration
\,\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}}\in[{\mathbb{S}}^{1},{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})]\,
and its LI
kinetic
momentum
\,P
) of a vacuum localised on an equitemporal slice of the spacetime
\,\Sigma\,
which we take to be (modelled on)
\,{\mathbb{S}}^{1}\subset\Sigma
. The presymplectic form of the super-
\sigma
-model in this formulation reads

\displaystyle\Omega_{\sigma}[\Psi]=\delta\vartheta[\Psi]+\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}}\,{\rm ev}^{*}\xi^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{{\rm H}}\,,

where

\displaystyle\vartheta[\Psi]=\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}}\,{\rm Vol}({\mathbb{S}}^{1})\,P_{a}\,\xi^{*}\underline{p}{}^{a}

is the canonical (kinetic-)action 1-form on the space(s) of states
\,\mathcal{T}^{*}[{\mathbb{S}}^{1},{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})]
, and

\displaystyle{\rm ev}\ :\ {\mathbb{S}}^{1}\times\bigl[{\mathbb{S}}^{1},{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr]\longrightarrow{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})

is the evaluation mapping. The presymplectic form defines a Poisson superbracket on the space of hamiltonians on the space of states,
i.e.
, on those sections
\,h\,
of the structure sheaf of the latter that satisfy the relation

\displaystyle\delta h=-\mathcal{V}_{h}\righthalfcup\Omega_{\sigma}

for some vector field
\,\mathcal{V}_{h}
, termed hamiltonian – the Poisson superbracket of two such sections:
\,h_{1}\,
and
\,h_{2}\,
with the respective hamiltonian vector fields
\,\mathcal{V}_{h_{1}}\,
and
\,\mathcal{V}_{h_{2}}\,
is given by

\displaystyle[h_{1},h_{2}\}_{\Omega_{\sigma}}=\mathcal{V}_{h_{2}}\righthalfcup\mathcal{V}_{h_{1}}\righthalfcup\Omega_{\sigma}\,.

In particular, upon contracting
\,\Omega_{\sigma}\,
with the
covariant
8
8

8

Here, covariance is determined by the super-1-form
\,\vartheta\,
and expressed by the requirement:
\,\,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}{}_{X}}\vartheta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0
.
lift

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}{}_{X}[\Psi]\equiv\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}}\,{\rm Vol}({\mathbb{S}}^{1})\,\mathcal{K}_{X}(\xi)+\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{X}[\Psi]

to
\,\mathcal{T}^{*}[{\mathbb{S}}^{1},{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})]\,
of the suitably
\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)
-vertically corrected right-invariant (RI) vector field
\,\mathcal{K}_{X}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{T}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})),\ X\in\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
on
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\equiv{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times\{1\}\subset{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X}=R_{X}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}+\Xi_{X}^{\ \ \ \underline{S}}\,J_{\underline{S}}\,,\qquad\qquad X\equiv X^{A}\,t_{A}\in\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,,

expressed in terms of the sections
\,\Xi_{X}^{\ \ \ \underline{S}}\,
of
\,\mathcal{O}_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
of Ref.
[
Sus20
, Prop. 5.1]
, we obtain the corresponding hamiltonian

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}{}_{X}\righthalfcup\Omega_{\rm HP}=:-\delta h_{X}\,.

The relevant basis RI vector fields are

\displaystyle R_{Q_{\alpha}}(\theta,x,0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{\vec{\partial}\ }{\partial\theta^{\alpha}}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta^{\beta}\,C_{\beta\gamma}\,\Gamma^{a\,\gamma}_{\ \ \ \alpha}\,\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial x^{a}}\,,

(3.2)

\displaystyle R_{P_{a}}(\theta,x,0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial x^{a}}\,,

(3.4)

\displaystyle R_{J_{ab}}(\theta,x,0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{c}\,\bigl(\eta_{cb}\,\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial x^{a}}-\eta_{ca}\,\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial x^{b}}\bigr)+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{ab\,\beta}^{\alpha}\,\theta^{\beta}\,\tfrac{\vec{\partial}\ }{\partial\theta^{\alpha}}+\tfrac{{\mathsf{d}}\ }{{\mathsf{d}}t}\mathord{\restriction}_{t=0}t\phi_{ab}\star 0

and give rise to the hamiltonians

\displaystyle X^{A}\,h_{A}\equiv h_{X}=\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}}\,\bigl({\rm Vol}({\mathbb{S}}^{1})\,P_{a}\,\xi^{*}\bigl(R_{X}\righthalfcup\theta^{a}_{\rm L}\bigr)+{\rm ev}^{*}\xi^{*}\kappa_{X}\bigr)\,,

with

\displaystyle\kappa_{Q_{\alpha}}(\theta,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a\,\alpha\beta}\,\theta^{\beta}\,\bigl(2{\mathsf{d}}x^{a}-\tfrac{1}{3}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\bigr)\,,

(3.6)

\displaystyle\kappa_{P_{a}}(\theta,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\,,

(3.8)

\displaystyle\kappa_{J_{ab}}(\theta,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-x^{c}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{ab}\,\Gamma_{c}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\,.

These furnish a realisation of a centrally extended supersymmetry Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
within the Poisson superalgebra of observables on the space of states of the super-
\sigma
-model,

\displaystyle[h_{B},h_{A}\}_{\Omega_{\rm HP}}=f_{AB}^{\ \ \ C}\,h_{C}+\mathcal{W}_{AB}

(here, the
\,f_{AB}^{\ \ \ C}\,
are the structure constants of
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
), with the components of the
wrapping anomaly
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{AB}=-\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}}\,{\rm ev}^{*}\xi^{*}\bigl(R_{t_{A}}\righthalfcup{\mathsf{d}}\kappa_{t_{B}}+f_{AB}^{\ \ \ C}\,\kappa_{t_{C}}\bigr)=:\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}}\,{\rm ev}^{*}w_{AB}\,,

given by

(3.9)

\displaystyle w_{\alpha\beta}={\mathsf{d}}\bigl(2\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a\,\alpha\beta}\,x^{a})\,,\qquad\qquad w_{ab}=0\,,\qquad\qquad w_{a\alpha}={\mathsf{d}}\bigl(-2\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a\,\alpha\beta}\,\theta^{\beta}\bigr)\,,

(3.10)

(3.11)

\displaystyle w_{ab\,cd}={\mathsf{d}}\bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\,x^{e}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{ab}\,\Gamma_{e}\,\Gamma_{cd}\,\theta\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad w_{ab\,c}={\mathsf{d}}\bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{abc}\,\theta\bigr)\,,

(3.12)

\displaystyle w_{ab\,\alpha}={\mathsf{d}}\bigl(-2x^{c}\,\bigl(\eta_{ca}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{b}-\eta_{cb}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\bigr)_{\alpha\beta}\,\theta^{\beta}-\tfrac{1}{6}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{abc}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{c}_{\alpha\beta}\,\theta^{\beta}\bigr)\,,

cp
Ref.
[
Sus18a
]
. Clearly, in the trivial super-minkowskian topology, we obtain the result

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{AB}\equiv 0\,.

Its refinement, to be considered in Sect.
4
, is the first step towards geometrisation of the cohomological content of the super-
\sigma
-model.

3.2.
Local supersymmetry of the vacuum

The GS super-
\sigma
-model with the physical supertarget
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm K}\,

realised within

\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
by means of the local sections has an implicit local symmetry modelled on the right action of
\,{\rm K}
. In particular, in the NG formulation, we have the large hidden gauge group
\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)
. Therefore, we anticipate an enhancement of the (tangential) local symmetry in the HP formulation according to the scheme

\displaystyle\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\nearrow\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)

upon restriction to the correspondence superdistribution
\,{\rm Corr}_{\rm HP}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})
. Inspection of the expression

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\widehat{\chi}}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\rm Corr}_{\rm HP}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})}=2\eta_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,q\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}_{D_{d,1}}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\bigr)\,q\wedge p^{\underline{b}}

immediately corroborates our expectation: The vector fields

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}\in\Gamma\bigl(\mathcal{T}\Sigma^{\rm HP}\bigr)\,,\qquad\mathcal{T}_{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}=J_{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}+T_{\underline{a}\widehat{b}\,i}^{\underline{S}}\,J_{\underline{S}}\equiv\mathcal{T}_{\underline{a}\widehat{b}\,i}\,,\qquad\qquad(\underline{a},\widehat{b})\in\{0,1\}\times\overline{2,d}\,,

with correcting sections
\,T_{\underline{a}\widehat{b}\,i}^{\underline{S}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma^{\rm HP}}(\mathcal{V}_{i})
uniquely determined by the condition
\,\mathcal{T}_{\underline{a}\widehat{b}\,i}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{T}\mathcal{V}_{i})
,
cp
Ref.
[
Sus20
, Prop. 3.6]
, satisfy

\displaystyle\forall_{(\underline{a},\widehat{b})\in\{0,1\}\times\overline{2,d}}\ :\ \mathcal{T}_{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}\righthalfcup\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\widehat{\chi}}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\rm Corr}_{\rm HP}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})}=0\,.

This enhancement does not carry any physically nontrivial information as it merely reflects the residual redundancy of our
realisation
of the physical supertarget within the mother Lie supergroup. Accordingly, we are inclined to fix the hidden gauge by augmenting the set of Euler–Lagrange equations derived formerly with

\displaystyle j^{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}\approx 0\,,\qquad(\underline{a},\widehat{b})\in\{0,1\}\times\overline{2,d}\,,

so that altogether we arrive at the conjunction of constraints

\displaystyle\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}_{D_{d,1}}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\bigr)\,q\approx 0\,,\qquad\qquad p^{\widehat{a}}\approx 0\,,\qquad\widehat{a}\in\overline{2,d}\,,\qquad\qquad j^{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}\approx 0\,,\qquad(\underline{b},\widehat{c})\in\{0,1\}\times\overline{2,d}

as the definition of the (
hidden
)
gauge-fixed vacuum superdistribution

\displaystyle{\rm Vac}_{\rm hgf}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\equiv{\rm Vac}_{(\Delta=0)}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)=\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\alpha}}\equiv\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\Sigma^{\rm HP}}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{a}\in\{0,1\}}\,\left\langle\mathcal{W}_{\underline{a}}\equiv P_{\underline{a}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\Sigma^{\rm HP}}\right\rangle\,.

Clearly, the hidden gauge-symmetry (sub)distribution
\,\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
is a symmetry of the above vacuum superdistribution,

\displaystyle\bigl[\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac},{\rm Vac}_{\rm hgf}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\bigr]\subset{\rm Vac}_{\rm hgf}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\,,

and so the vacuum foliation descends to the supertarget
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
.

But that is not all. Indeed, the very mechanism responsible for the restitution of supersymmetry in the vacuum gives rise to an extra and physically nontrivial local supersymmetry on restriction to
\,{\rm Corr}_{\rm HP}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})
, to wit, tangential Graßmann-odd translations along
\,{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}{}^{\rm T}
,

\displaystyle\forall_{\underline{\alpha}\in\overline{1,\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}}\ :\ \mathcal{W}_{\underline{\alpha}}\righthalfcup\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\widehat{\chi}}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\rm Corr}_{\rm HP}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})}=0\,.

Furthermore, we readily establish

\displaystyle(\mathcal{W}_{0}+\mathcal{W}_{1})\righthalfcup\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\widehat{\chi}}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\rm Corr}_{\rm HP}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})}=0\,,

and so, altogether, we obtain the
enhanced gauge-symmetry superdistribution

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}_{1}\equiv\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)=\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\mathcal{W}_{0}+\mathcal{W}_{1}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{(\underline{a},\widehat{b})\in\{0,1\}\times\overline{2,d}}\,\left\langle\mathcal{T}_{\underline{a}\widehat{b}}\right\rangle\subset{\rm Corr}_{\rm HP}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\,,

modelled on the sub-space

\displaystyle\mathfrak{gs}_{1}\equiv\mathfrak{gs}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)={\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}{}^{\rm T}\oplus\left\langle P_{0}+P_{1}\right\rangle\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)\,,

with the component along
\,\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
realised trivially. Actually, in order to be able to interpret
\,\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}_{1}\,
as a proper local-symmetry structure of the theory, we should demand that the limit of its weak derived flag, as introduced in Ref.
[
Sus20
, Def. 4.9]
, stays in the correspondence superdistribution. This is, clearly,
not
the case for
\,\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}_{1}
, and so we are led to extract from it a sub-superdistribution that satisfies this extra condition – in this manner, we arrive at the
\kappa
-symmetry superdistribution

\displaystyle\kappa\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)=\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\mathcal{W}_{0}+\mathcal{W}_{1}\right\rangle\,.

The latter immediately reveals its peculiarity: The limit of its weak derived flag,

\displaystyle\kappa\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)^{-\infty}=\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\mathcal{W}_{0}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\mathcal{W}_{1}\right\rangle

is contained not only in
\,{\rm Corr}_{\rm HP}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})
, but in the vacuum superdistribution, or, more accurately,

\displaystyle\kappa\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)^{-\infty}\equiv{\rm Vac}_{\rm hgf}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\,

i.e.
, the
\kappa
-symmetry superdistribution is superbracket-generating for the tangent sheaf of the gauged-fixed HP vacuum foliation
\,\Sigma^{\rm HP}_{\rm vac}
, its flows enveloping the integral leaves of the latter. Hence, it makes sense to think of
\,\kappa(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})^{-\infty}\,
as the gauge (super)symmetry of the vacuum. The generating nature of the Graßmann-odd component

\displaystyle\kappa\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)^{(1)}\equiv\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\mathcal{W}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle\subset\kappa\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)

won it its name – the square root of (the chiral half of) the vacuum – in Ref.
[
Sus20
]
. It is the HP counterpart of the odd gauge symmetry of the super-
\sigma
-model in the NG formulation, known under the name of
\kappa
-symmetry, that was originally found and studied by de Azcárraga and Lukierski in Refs.
[
dAL82
,
dAL83
]
in the setting of the super-
\sigma
-model of superparticle dynamics, and subsequently rediscovered and elaborated by Siegel in Ref.
[
Sie83
]
and, in the two-dimensional setting, in Ref.
[
Sie84
]
. In the present context, the Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})\,
acquires the interpretation of the gauge-symmetry algebra of the vacuum, confirmed trivially by its inclusion in the kernel of the suitably restricted presymplectic form

\displaystyle\Omega_{\rm HP}=\sum_{\tau\in\mathfrak{T}_{2}}\,\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}\cap\tau}\,{\rm ev}^{*}\sigma_{i_{\tau}}^{{\rm vac}\,*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\widehat{\chi}}

of the GS super-
\sigma
-model in the HP formulation (in which the space of states is parametrised by configurations
\,\widetilde{\xi}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}}
). The crucial feature of the gauge supersymmetry modelled by
\,\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})\,
is its target space-geometric nature, to be contrasted with the mixed target-space/worldsheet and hence somewhat obscure nature of its NG predecessor,
cp
Refs.
[
McA00
,
GKW06a
]
– this, in conjunction with the geometrisation of the superfield equations, makes the HP formulation perfectly suited for a fully fledged higher-geometric analysis that we carry out in the remainder of this paper. We readily convince ourselves that it integrates to a Lie sub-supergroup of the supersymmetry group
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
represented by the super-Harish–Chandra pair

\displaystyle{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}=\bigl({\rm Mink}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac},\mathfrak{vac}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\bigr)\subset{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})

with

\displaystyle{\rm Mink}(d,1)_{\rm vac}=\{\ \bigl(x^{a}\bigr)\in{\rm Mink}(d,1)\quad|\quad\forall_{\widehat{a}\in\overline{2,d}}\ :\ x^{\widehat{a}}=0\ \}

and with the action of the body Lie group on the Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})\,
inherited from
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. We shall, henceforth, refer to this Lie supergroup by the name of the
\kappa
-symmetry group of the superstring in
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. Its action on
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
(by right translations) splits the latter into orbits – the integral leaves of the (integrable) superdistribution
\,\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})\subset\mathcal{T}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
, the very ones whose ‘intersections’ with the
\,\mathcal{V}_{i},\ i\in I\,
model the vacua within the mother Lie supergroup.

4.
A geometrisation of the superbackground – the super-1-gerbe(s)

Behind every two-dimensional (super-)
\sigma
-model, there is a 1-gerbe. A 1-gerbe is a geometrisation, proposed by Murray in Ref.
[
Mur96
]
and recalled exhaustively in Ref.
[
Sus17
, Sec. 2]
(
cp
also Ref.
[
Hue20
]
for a recent rendering in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting), of (the cohomology class of) a de Rham 3-cocycle that couples to the charge current defined by the embedding of the worldsheet
\,\Sigma\,
in the (super)target. This higher-geometric object gives a rigorous meaning to the topological term in the DF amplitude (
cp
Ref.
[
Gaw88
]
), determines a prequantisation of the field theory through cohomological transgression (
ibid.
) and naturally encodes information on its (pre)quantisable global symmetries and their gauging (
cp
Refs.
[
GR03
,
GSW08
,
GSW11
,
GSW10
,
Sus12
,
Sus13
,
GSW13
]
). More generally, the bicategory of 1-gerbes provides us with geometric and cohomological data necessary for a quantum-mechanically consistent definition of a poly-phase
\sigma
-model in which various phases are separated by edges of a so-called defect localised on a graph in
\,\Sigma
,
cp
Refs.
[
FSW08
,
RS09
,
RS11
]
, and distinguished 1-cells of the bicategory model dualities between equivalent
\sigma
-models (with, sometimes, different targets),
cp
Refs.
[
Sus11a
,
Sus11b
]
. The Reader is urged to consult Refs.
[
Sus17
,
Sus19
,
Sus20
]
for a review of applications of gerbe theory in the field-theoretic setting of interest. Below, we merely recapitulate the logic behind the construction of the (super-)1-gerbe for the GS super-
\sigma
-model and present details of the construction itself.

A prerequisite of a meaningful geometrisation of the 3-form component of the (super)background of the loop’s propagation is the identification of the cohomology relevant to the field theory in hand. The discussion carried out in the preceding sections leaves no room for doubt – the cohomology to be considered in the setting of the super-
\sigma
-model is the supersymmetric refinement of the standard de Rham cohomology of the supertarget. Owing to the inherent noncompactness of the supersymmetry group
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
(and
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
, for that matter), the latter cohomology differs from the standard de Rham cohomology (trivial for
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
), and the GS super-3-cocycle of interest happens to define a nontrivial class in
\,{\rm CaE}^{3}({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1}))\equiv H^{3}_{\rm dR}({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1}))^{{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\,
(lifting to a nontrivial class in
\,{\rm CaE}^{3}({\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1}))
). A non-pragmatic rationale for a geometrisation of the Cartan–Eilenberg cohomology of the supertarget
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
, as stated – after Rabin and Crane,
cp
Refs.
[
RC85
,
Rab87
]
– in Ref.
[
Sus17
, Sec. 3]
, is a topologisation of the said cohomology as the dual of the singular homology of an orbifold
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})/\Gamma_{\rm KR}\,
of the super-Minkowski space by the natural action of the Kostelecký–Rabin discrete supersymmetry group
\,\Gamma_{\rm KR}\,
of Ref.
[
KR84
]
, generated by integer Graßmann-odd translations (in the
\mathcal{S}
-point picture, and for a suitable choice of the Majorana representation of
\,{\rm Cliff}({\mathbb{R}}^{d,1})
). Accordingly, the GS super-
\sigma
-model ought to be interpreted as an effective description of standard loop dynamics in
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})/\Gamma_{\rm KR}
. Prior to investigating the consequences of the latter idea, we pause to decode its meaning and present a concrete realisation, in a semi-heuristic approach in which we gloss over any (
e.g.
, topological) subtleties encountered along the way. Thus, we change the hitherto (Kostant’s) perspective on supermanifolds and present
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
– upon invoking
[
Bat80
, Def.-Cor.]
(
cp
also Refs.
[
Gaw77
,
Bat79
]
) – as (a direct limit
\,N\to\infty\,
of) a nested family, indexed by
\,{\mathbb{N}}^{\times}\ni N
, of DeWitt’s skeletons given essentially by (‘soul’) vector bundles

\displaystyle{\rm Skel}_{N}\bigl({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)\equiv\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,\bigr({\mathbb{R}}\oplus\bigoplus_{k=1}^{E(\frac{N}{2})}\,\bigwedge\hskip-3.0pt{}^{2k}\,{\mathbb{R}}^{\times N}\bigl)\oplus\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\bigoplus_{l=0}^{E(\frac{N-1}{2})}\,\bigwedge\hskip-3.0pt{}^{2l+1}\,{\mathbb{R}}^{\times N}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,{\mathbb{R}}\equiv{\mathbb{R}}^{\times d+1}

of rank
\,2^{N-1}\,(d+1+D_{d,1})-d-1\,
over the body
\,{\mathbb{R}}^{\times d+1}\equiv{\rm Mink}(d,1)
,
cp
Ref.
[
DeW84
, Sec. 2.1]
. Practically speaking, this amounts to realising the global coordinate generators
\,(\theta^{\alpha},x^{a})\,
of the structure sheaf
\,\mathcal{O}_{{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\,
in the
N{}^{\rm th}
skeleton as (functional) linear combinations of elements of a basis
\,\{{\boldsymbol{1}}\}\cup\{e^{i_{1}}\wedge e^{i_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge e^{i_{m}}\}_{i_{1}<i_{2}<\ldots<i_{m}\in\overline{1,N},\ m\in\overline{1,N}}\,
of the corresponding exterior algebra
\,\bigwedge\hskip-3.0pt{}^{\bullet}\,{\mathbb{R}}^{\times N}\,
as

\displaystyle\theta^{\alpha}_{(N)}=\sum_{l=0}^{E(\frac{N-1}{2})}\,\theta^{\alpha}_{i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{2l+1}}\,e^{i_{1}}\wedge e^{i_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge e^{i_{2l+1}}\,,\qquad\qquad x^{a}_{(N)}=x^{a}_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{E(\frac{N}{2})}\,x^{a}_{i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{2k}}\,e^{i_{1}}\wedge e^{i_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge e^{i_{2k}}

This presentation seems naturally compatible with Freed’s identification of the super-
\sigma
-model mapping space
\,[\Sigma,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})]\,
as the appropriate inner-
{\rm Hom}
functor – indeed, we may think of the morphisms from
\,[\Sigma,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})]({\mathbb{R}}^{0|N})\,
as probing the
N{}^{\rm th}
skeleton. Now, the Rabin–Crane argument
at level

N
refers to the subgroup
\,\Gamma_{\rm KR}^{(N)}\subset{\rm Skel}_{N}({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1}))\,
of the
N{}^{\rm th}
skeleton (with respect to super-minkowskian multiplication, realised in terms of the exterior product) generated
9
9

9

In a suitable Majorana representation of the Clifford algebra with integer-valued matrices of the generators,
cp
Ref.
[
KR84
]
.
by odd vectors

\displaystyle\nu^{\alpha}_{(N)}=\sum_{l=0}^{E(\frac{N-1}{2})}\,n^{\alpha}_{i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{2l+1}}\,e^{i_{1}}\wedge e^{i_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge e^{i_{2l+1}}\,,\qquad\alpha\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}

with all (pure-soul) coefficients in

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Z}}\ni n^{\alpha}_{i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{2l+1}}\,.

An example of the orbifold

\displaystyle{\rm Skel}_{N}\bigl({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)/\Gamma_{\rm KR}^{(N)}

whose homology is readily seen to encode the CaE cohomology of
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
(owing to the polynomial character of the binary operation of this Lie supergroup in the

(and even) fibre directions. The nested character of the of DeWitt’s skeletal presentation implies that the latter observation carries over to the direct limit, and so in view of our earlier remark on the interpretation of Freed’s prescription in the present context, it becomes clear that we should allow for monodromies of the embedding fields of both parities (
i.e.

\sigma
-model with the supertarget
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
when modelling the field theory with the Rabin–Crane orbifold
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})/\Gamma_{\rm KR}
as the supertarget. Taking into account the top line in Eq. (
3.11
), we are led to consider a supercentral wrapping-charge extension

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{0}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d|D_{d,1}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathfrak{smink}}(d,1|D_{d,1})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{0}}

of the original supersymmetry algebra
\,\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
with the supervector space structure

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{smink}}(d,1|D_{d,1})=\bigl(\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\widetilde{\underline{Q}}{}_{\alpha}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,\left\langle\widetilde{\underline{P}}{}_{a}\right\rangle\bigr)\oplus\bigl(\bigoplus_{\beta=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\underline{Z}{}^{\beta}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{b=1}^{d}\,\left\langle\underline{R}{}^{b}\right\rangle\bigr)\equiv\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus{\mathbb{R}}^{d|D_{d,1}}

and with the Lie-superalgebra structure determined by the relations

\displaystyle\{\widetilde{\underline{Q}}{}_{\alpha},\widetilde{\underline{Q}}{}_{\beta}\}=\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}_{\alpha\beta}\,\bigl(\widetilde{\underline{P}}{}_{a}+\eta_{ab}\,\underline{R}{}^{b}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\underline{P}}{}_{a},\widetilde{\underline{P}}{}_{b}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\underline{Q}}{}_{\alpha},\widetilde{\underline{P}}{}_{a}]=\overline{\Gamma}_{a\,\alpha\beta}\,\underline{Z}{}^{\beta}\,,

(4.1)

(4.2)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\underline{Q}}{}_{\alpha},\underline{R}{}^{a}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\underline{P}}{}_{a},\underline{R}{}^{b}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad\{\widetilde{\underline{Q}}{}_{\alpha},\underline{Z}{}^{\beta}\}=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\underline{P}}{}_{a},\underline{Z}{}^{\alpha}]=0\,,

(4.3)

(4.4)

\displaystyle[\underline{R}{}^{a},\underline{R}{}^{b}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad\{\underline{Z}{}^{\alpha},\underline{Z}{}^{\beta}\}=0\,,\qquad\qquad[R_{a},\underline{Z}{}^{\alpha}]=0\,.

We note, parenthetically, that considerations similar to ours were employed in Ref.
[

in a derivation of central topological charges associated with the (even) wrapping states of the super-

The Graßmann-even (
d
-vector) component of the extension is trivial

– it can be removed by the simple redefinition

with the supervector-space structure

and the Lie superbracket

alongside a decoupled abelian algebra

The odd extension, which is none other than the Green superalgebra of Ref.
[
Gre89
]
, has an attractive cohomological feature, to wit, the pullback of the nontrivial 3-cocycle
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{{\rm H}}\,
to
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
trivialises. Indeed, denote the super-1-forms dual to the new generators
\,\underline{Z}{}^{\alpha}\,
as
\,\underline{z}{}_{\alpha}\,
to obtain
11
11

11

In the present paper, the super-forms appear in a (seemingly) double rôle: as sections of the sheaf of superdifferential forms on the Lie supergroup (regarded as a supermanifold) and as (super-)forms on its tangent Lie superalgebra. We use the same symbol(s) for both rôles, which, however, should not lead to confusion as it is always clear from the context which rôle is currently under consideration (in particular, we reserve the symbol
\,\widehat{\delta}\,
for the coboundary operator of the Lie-superalgebra cohomology).

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{{\rm H}}=\widehat{\delta}\underline{z}{}_{\alpha}\wedge\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}^{*}\underline{q}^{\alpha}=\widehat{\delta}\bigl(\underline{z}{}_{\alpha}\wedge\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}^{*}\underline{q}^{\alpha}\bigr)\,.

This is a manifestation of a
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded variant of the classic Lie-algebraic phenomenon: For any Lie (super)algebra
\,\mathfrak{g}
, classes in the second group
\,H^{2}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{a})\,
of the Chevalley–Eilenberg cohomology of
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
with values in a trivial (super)module
\,\mathfrak{a}\,
are in a one-to-one correspondence with equivalence classes of (super)central extensions

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{0}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{a}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{0}}

of
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
by
\,\mathfrak{a}
,
cp
Ref.
[
Sus17
]
. Now, the supervector space

\displaystyle\Omega^{1}\bigl({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)^{{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\underline{q}{}^{\alpha}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,\left\langle\underline{p}{}^{a}\right\rangle

carries a natural structure of a

(4.9)

\displaystyle\,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{\cdot}\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ \mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times\Omega^{1}\bigl({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)^{{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}\bigl({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)^{{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ \bigl(X,\omega\bigr)\longmapsto\,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{X}\omega\,,

and its Graßmann-odd subspace

\displaystyle\bigl(\Omega^{1}\bigl({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)^{{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\bigr)^{(1)}\equiv\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\underline{q}{}^{\alpha}\right\rangle

is a trivial submodule. Accordingly, the GS super-3-cocycle

acquires the interpretation of a nontrivial
\bigl(\Omega^{1}\bigl({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)^{{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\bigr)^{(1)}
-valued super-2-cocycle on
\,\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
, and as such it gives rise to the supercentral extension (
4.5

-cocycles that codefine the Green–Schwarz-type super-
\sigma

p

The Lie-superalgebra extension (

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{1}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{0|D_{d,1}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\longrightarrow{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{1}}

with the supermanifold structure

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})={\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|D_{d,1}}\ni\bigl(\theta^{\alpha},x^{a},\xi_{\beta}\bigr)

and the Lie-supergroup structure determined by the binary operation

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\underline{{\rm m}}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})

with the coordinate presentation

(4.12)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\underline{{\rm m}}\bigl(\bigl(\theta_{1}^{\alpha},x_{1}^{a},\xi_{1\,\beta}\bigr),\bigl(\theta_{2}^{\alpha},x_{2}^{a},\xi_{2\,\beta}\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(\theta_{1}^{\alpha}+\theta_{2}^{\alpha},x_{1}^{a}+x_{2}^{a}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\theta_{2},\xi_{1\,\beta}+\xi_{2\,\beta}+\overline{\Gamma}{}_{b\,\beta\gamma}\,\theta_{1}^{\gamma}\,x_{2}^{b}-\tfrac{1}{6}\,\bigl(\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{b}\,\theta_{2}\bigr)\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{b}_{\beta\gamma}\,\bigl(2\theta_{1}^{\gamma}+\theta_{2}^{\gamma}\bigr)\bigr)\,,

ensuring the desired left-invariance of the super-1-form

\displaystyle\underline{z}{}_{\alpha}(\theta,x,\xi)={\mathsf{d}}\xi_{\alpha}-\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a\,\alpha\beta}\,\theta^{\beta}\,\bigl({\mathsf{d}}x^{a}+\tfrac{1}{6}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\bigr)\,.

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\underline{Q}{}_{\alpha}(\theta,x,\xi)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\vec{\partial}\ }{\partial\theta^{\alpha}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}_{\alpha\beta}\,\theta^{\beta}\,\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial x^{a}}+\tfrac{1}{3}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a\,\alpha\beta}\,\theta^{\beta}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}_{\gamma\delta}\,\theta^{\gamma}\,\tfrac{\vec{\partial}\ }{\partial\xi_{\delta}}\,,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\vec{\partial}\ }{\partial\xi_{\alpha}}\,.

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\rm F}}=\bigl({\rm pr}_{2}^{*}-{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\bigr)\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm B}}\,,

with the coordinate presentation

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\rm F}}\bigl((\theta,x,\xi_{1}),(\theta,x,\xi_{2})\bigr)={\mathsf{d}}\theta^{\alpha}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(\xi_{2\,\alpha}-\xi_{1\,\alpha}\bigr)\,.

\displaystyle[(X_{1},r_{1}),(X,r_{2})\}_{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\rm F}}}=\bigl([X_{1},X_{2}\}_{\oplus},X_{2}\righthalfcup X_{1}\righthalfcup\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\rm F}}\bigr)\,,

Finally, we consider the fibred-product Lie supergroup

We reserve the suggestive (symbolic) notation

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathscr{L}}\equiv{\boldsymbol{1}}

for an isomorphism of the above trivial form. The isomorphism satisfies the coherence (groupoid) identity

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{1,3,4}^{*}\mu_{\mathscr{L}}\circ\bigl({\rm pr}_{1,2,3}^{*}\mu_{\mathscr{L}}\otimes{\rm id}_{{\rm pr}_{3,4}^{*}\mathscr{L}}\bigr)={\rm pr}_{1,2,4}^{*}\mu_{\mathscr{L}}\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm pr}_{1,2}^{*}\mathscr{L}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2,3,4}^{*}\mu_{\mathscr{L}}\bigr)

over the quadruple fibred product

, the latter being equipped with the canonical projections

(1,2,4)\}\,

(defined in an obvious manner). Clearly,
\,\mu_{\mathscr{L}}\,
is also a Lie-supergroup isomorphism,

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathscr{L}}\circ[\mathscr{L}_{1,2;2,3}\underline{{\rm m}}]=\mathscr{L}_{1,3}\underline{{\rm m}}\circ\bigl(\mu_{\mathscr{L}}\times\mu_{\mathscr{L}}\bigr)\,.

The 1-gerbe

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}:=\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1}),\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})},\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm B}},\mathscr{L},\pi_{\mathscr{L}},\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{{\rm A}}{}_{\mathscr{L}},\mu_{\mathscr{L}}\bigr)

was named the
Green–Schwarz super-1-gerbe over
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
in Ref.
[
Sus17
, Def. 5.9]
. It is an example of a
Cartan–Eilenberg super-1-gerbe
(over the Lie supergroup
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
), that is a distinguished 1-gerbe in the category of Lie supergroups. Its existence and equivariance properties, the latter to be discussed at length in Sec.
5
, are markers of a quantum-mechanical consistency of the GS super-
\sigma
-model of the superstring in
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. As it stands, the super-1-gerbe is naturally associated with the NG formulation that can be phrased in terms of the differential-geometric data of the homogeneous space
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
of the mother supersymmetry group
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
exclusively. Instrumental in its construction is the ‘accidental’ Lie-supergroup structure on this particular homogeneous space. In the case of a generic homogeneous space
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}\,
of a supersymmetry Lie supergroup
\,{\rm G}\,
associated with a Lie subgroup
\,{\rm H}\subset|{\rm G}|\,
of its body
\,|{\rm G}|
, the geometrisation scheme exemplified above, making use of the relations between the Cartan–Eilenberg cohomology of the Lie supergroup and the Chevalley–Eilenberg cohomology of its tangent Lie superalgebra and the interpretation of the distinguished second cohomology group of the latter, has to start on
\,{\rm G}\,
and only in the end
descend
to
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}
. The cohomology to be geometrised under such circumstances is
\,{\rm CaE}^{\bullet}({\rm G})\,
further restricted to
{\rm H}
-basic super-forms, and – in the light of the findings of Refs.
[
GSW10
,
Sus11b
,
Sus12
,
GSW13
,
Sus13
]
– the CaE super-1-gerbe that we seek to erect over
\,{\rm G}\,
has to carry a
descendable

{\rm H}
-equivariant structure
15
15

15

This notion shall be recalled and illustrated in Sec.
5
. The advantage of carrying out the geometrisation over
\,{\rm G}\,
is that it yields a higher-geometric object that can be restricted directly to the HP section
\,\Sigma^{\rm HP}\,
and used there in a rigorous study of the dual HP formulation upon incorporating a suitable correction coming from the LI volume super-2-form
\,{\rm Vol}(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac})
. In order to attain the same goal in the super-minkowskian setting under consideration, we need to walk the path laid out above in the reverse direction, that is we must lift the GS super-1-gerbe to
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. An obvious thing to do would be to pull back
\,\mathcal{G}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\,
along the canonical projection
\,\pi\,
of Eq. (
2.25
) – this operation was actually employed in the original studies of supersymmetry-equivariance of the GS super-1-gerbe of the superstring in
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
that was reported in Ref.
[
Sus19
]
. The obvious drawback of this idea is that it depends on the supermanifold morphism
\,\pi\,
that is
not
a Lie-supergroup homomorphism, and so the lift takes us out of the category of CaE super-1-gerbes that seems to be the most adequate one for our physically motivated purposes. Below, we propose an alternative construction that effectively circumnavigates the obstacle encoutered on our way towards ‘gerbification’ of the super-minkowskian GS super-
\sigma
-model in the supergeometrically largely tractable HP formulation.

The idea that we wish to pursue now consists in lifting the supercentral extension
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
of the super-minkowskian Lie superalgebra
equivariantly
to an extension of the full supersymmetry Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. Taking a closer look at the lift (
2.18
) of the GS super-3-cocycle (
4.10
),

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\chi}\equiv\bigl(p^{a}\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a\,\alpha\beta}\,q^{\beta}\bigr)\wedge q^{\alpha}\,,

and the precise relation between the lifted LI super-1-forms
\,q^{\alpha}\,
in it and the
\,\underline{q}{}^{\alpha}\,
that we previously identified as the basis of the (trivial)
\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
-module defining the extension,

(4.31)

\displaystyle q^{\alpha}(\theta,x,\phi)=S(\phi)^{-1\,\alpha}_{\ \ \ \ \beta}\,\underline{q}{}^{\beta}(\theta,x)\,,

we are readily led to postulate the lift

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})=\bigl(\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,\left\langle{\mathsf{Y}}P_{a}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\beta=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle Z^{\beta}\right\rangle\bigr)\oplus\bigoplus_{b<c=0}^{d}\,\left\langle{\mathsf{Y}}J_{bc}\right\rangle\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)

in the form of a
\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)
-module Lie superalgebra with superbrackets

\displaystyle\{{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha},{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\beta}\}=\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}_{\alpha\beta}\,{\mathsf{Y}}P_{a}\,,\qquad\qquad[{\mathsf{Y}}P_{a},{\mathsf{Y}}P_{b}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha},{\mathsf{Y}}P_{a}]=\overline{\Gamma}_{a\,\alpha\beta}\,Z^{\beta}\,,

(4.32)

(4.33)

\displaystyle\{{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha},Z^{\beta}\}=0\,,\qquad\qquad[{\mathsf{Y}}P_{a},Z^{\alpha}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad\{Z^{\alpha},Z^{\beta}\}=0\,,

(4.34)

(4.35)

\displaystyle[{\mathsf{Y}}J_{ab},{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha}]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{ab}{}^{\beta}_{\ \alpha}\,{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\beta}\,,\qquad\qquad[{\mathsf{Y}}J_{ab},{\mathsf{Y}}P_{c}]=\eta_{bc}\,{\mathsf{Y}}P_{a}-\eta_{ac}\,{\mathsf{Y}}P_{b}\,,\qquad\qquad[{\mathsf{Y}}J_{ab},Z^{\alpha}]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{ab}{}^{\alpha}_{\ \beta}\,Z^{\beta}\,,

(4.36)

(4.37)

\displaystyle[{\mathsf{Y}}J_{ab},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{cd}]=\eta_{ad}\,{\mathsf{Y}}J_{bc}-\eta_{ac}\,{\mathsf{Y}}J_{bd}+\eta_{bc}\,{\mathsf{Y}}J_{ad}-\eta_{bd}\,{\mathsf{Y}}J_{ac}\,,

that extends the analogous structure on the equivariant lift
\,\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
of
\,\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. Thus, the extra generators
\,Z^{\alpha}\,
transform under
\,\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)\,
as spinors, in conformity with Eq. (
4.31
). The only components of the ensuing super-Jacobiator that are not trivially null (
e.g.
, because of being identical with their un-extended counterparts) read

\displaystyle{\rm sJac}\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha},{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\beta},{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\gamma}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3!\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a\,(\alpha\beta}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}_{\gamma)\delta}\,Z^{\delta}\,,

(4.39)

\displaystyle{\rm sJac}\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha},{\mathsf{Y}}P_{a},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{bc}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(\bigl(\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\Gamma_{bc}\bigr)_{(\alpha\beta)}-\eta_{ab}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{c\,\alpha\beta}+\eta_{ac}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{b\,\alpha\beta}\bigr)\,Z^{\beta}\,,

(4.41)

\displaystyle{\rm sJac}\bigl(Z^{\alpha},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{ab},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{cd}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\,\bigl(\Gamma_{ab}\,\Gamma_{cd}-\Gamma_{cd}\,\Gamma_{ab}-2\eta_{ad}\,\Gamma_{bc}+2\eta_{ac}\,\Gamma_{bd}-2\eta_{bc}\,\Gamma_{ad}+2\eta_{bd}\,\Gamma_{ac}\bigr)^{\alpha}_{\ \beta}\,Z^{\beta}\,.

The first of these vanishes in virtue of the Fierz identities (
2.1
). Next, we compute

\displaystyle\bigl(\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\Gamma_{bc}\bigr)^{\rm T}=\overline{\Gamma}{}_{cb}\,\Gamma_{a}\,,

and use it to perform the reduction

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\Gamma_{bc}+\bigl(\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\Gamma_{bc}\bigr)^{\rm T}-2\eta_{ab}\,\overline{\Gamma}_{c}+2\eta_{ac}\,\overline{\Gamma}_{b}=0\,,

which implies that the second component of the super-Jacobiator is zero. Finally, the third component can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{\rm sJac}\bigl(Z^{\alpha},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{ab},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{cd}\bigr)\equiv-q^{\alpha}\bigl({\rm sJac}\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\beta},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{ab},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{cd}\bigr)\bigr)\,Z^{\beta}=0\,,

which concludes the proof of existence of a Lie-superalgebra structure on
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
with the superbracket postulated above. Denote as

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})

the Lie-superalgebra epimorphism obtained by linearly extending the assignment

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\ :\ \bigl({\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha},{\mathsf{Y}}P_{a},Z^{\beta},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{bc}\bigr)\longmapsto\bigl(Q_{\alpha},P_{a},0,J_{bc}\bigr)\,,

and let the duals of the generators
\,Z^{\alpha}\,
be
\,z_{\alpha}
. We then obtain the desired identity

\displaystyle\widehat{\delta}\bigl(z_{\alpha}\wedge\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})}^{*}q^{\alpha}\bigr)=\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})}^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\chi}\,.

Next, we readily enhance the Lie superalgebra
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
to a super-Harish–Chandra pair (
i.e.
, to a Lie supergroup)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})=\bigl(\widetilde{\rm ISO}(d,1),{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\bigr)

with the body Lie group
\,\widetilde{\rm ISO}(d,1)\,
realised on the Graßmann-odd component

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{(1)}\equiv\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\beta=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle Z^{\beta}\right\rangle

of
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
as

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\rho\ :\ {\mathbb{R}}^{\times d+1}\rtimes_{L}{\rm Spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\rm End}\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{(1)}\bigr)\ :\ (x,\phi)\longmapsto S(\phi)^{\rm T}\oplus S(\phi)^{-1}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}\rho(x,\phi)\,.

Thus,
\,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\,
integrates to a Lie-supergroup epimorphism

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}={\rm pr}_{1}\times{\rm id}_{{\rm Spin}(d,1)}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\rtimes_{L,S,S^{-{\rm T}}}{\rm Spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,,

and we have a coordinate description of the Lie supergroup

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\rtimes_{L,S,S^{-{\rm T}}}{\rm Spin}(d,1)\ni\bigl(\theta^{\alpha},x^{a},\xi_{\beta},\phi^{bc}\bigr)

in which the binary operation

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})

takes the form

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}\bigl(\bigl(\theta_{1}^{\alpha},x_{1}^{a},\xi_{1\,\beta},\phi_{1}^{bc}\bigr),\bigl(\theta_{2}^{\alpha},x_{2}^{a},\xi_{2\,\beta},\phi_{2}^{bc}\bigr)\bigr)=\bigl(\theta_{1}^{\alpha}+S(\phi_{1})^{\alpha}_{\ \gamma}\,\theta_{2}^{\gamma},x_{1}^{a}+L(\phi_{1})^{a}_{\ d}\,x_{2}^{d}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,S(\phi_{1})\,\theta_{2},

(4.42)

(4.43)

\displaystyle\xi_{1\,\beta}+\xi_{2\,\gamma}\,S(\phi_{1})^{-1\,\gamma}_{\ \ \ \ \beta}+\overline{\Gamma}{}_{d\,\beta\gamma}\,\theta_{1}^{\gamma}\,L(\phi_{1})^{d}_{\ e}\,x_{2}^{e}-\tfrac{1}{6}\,\bigl(\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{d}\,S(\phi_{1})\,\theta_{2}\bigr)\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{d}_{\beta\gamma}\,\bigl(2\theta_{1}^{\gamma}+S(\phi_{1})^{\gamma}_{\ \delta}\,\theta_{2}^{\delta}\bigr),\bigl(\phi_{1}\star\phi_{2}\bigr)^{bc}\bigr)\,.

The LI super-1-forms
\,z_{\alpha}\,
admit the explicit coordinate presentation

\displaystyle z_{\alpha}(\theta,x,\xi,\phi)=\underline{z}{}_{\beta}(\theta,x,\xi)\,S(\phi)^{\beta}_{\ \alpha}

and we arrive at the anticipated identity

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\chi}={\mathsf{d}}\bigl(z_{\alpha}\wedge\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}^{*}q^{\alpha}\bigr)\,,

whence also the choice of the curving

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}:=z_{\alpha}\wedge\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}^{*}q^{\alpha}

of the CaE super-1-gerbe over
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
under reconstruction. The canonical projection
\,\pi\,
of Diag. (
2.25
) lifts to the extensions as the supermanifold morphism

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\pi\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\rm Spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})

with the property

\displaystyle\pi\circ\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}=\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\circ{\mathsf{Y}}\pi\,,

and we establish the descent relation

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}\pi^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm B}}\,.

On the level of the underlying supervector spaces, we have the corresponding linear maps

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}p\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})

trivially satisfying the identity

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\circ{\mathsf{Y}}p=p\circ\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\,,

and the corresponding relation

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}p^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm B}}

between super-2-forms on the respective Lie superalgebras.

From this point onwards, the construction proceeds along the same lines as for
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. Thus, we take the fibred-square Lie super group

(4.45)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\rtimes_{L,S,S^{-{\rm T}}\times S^{-{\rm T}}}{\rm Spin}(d,1)\,,

endowed with the canonical projection

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\pi\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}\pi\times{\mathsf{Y}}\pi\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})

(4.46)

(4.47)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times_{{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})}{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,,

and its tangent Lie superalgebra

(4.49)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus_{\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})}{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha},{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha}\bigr)\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,\left\langle\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}P_{a},{\mathsf{Y}}P_{a}\bigr)\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\beta=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\bigl(Z^{\beta},0\bigr)\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\gamma=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\bigl(0,Z^{\gamma}\bigr)\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{b<c=0}^{d}\,\left\langle\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}J_{bc},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{bc}\bigr)\right\rangle

(4.51)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)\,,

coming with the supervector-space projection

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}p\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}p\oplus{\mathsf{Y}}p\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,.

The nontrivial super-2-cocycle

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\mathcal{F}}=\bigl({\rm pr}_{2}^{*}-{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\bigr)\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}p^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\rm F}}

on
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
engenders a central extension

(4.52)

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{0}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}}\ }{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{0}}

with the supervector-space structure

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}=\bigl(\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q_{\alpha}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,\left\langle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P_{a}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\beta=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(1)}^{\beta}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\gamma=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(2)}^{\gamma}\right\rangle\bigr)\oplus\left\langle\mathcal{Z}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{b<c=0}^{d}\,\left\langle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{bc}\right\rangle\equiv\mathfrak{l}\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)

with respect to which

\displaystyle\pi_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}}\equiv\pi_{\mathfrak{l}}\oplus{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)}\ :\ \mathfrak{l}\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,,

and with the Lie superbracket

\displaystyle\{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q_{\beta}\}=\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}_{\alpha\beta}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P_{a}\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P_{a},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P_{b}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P_{a}]=\overline{\Gamma}_{a\,\alpha\beta}\,\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(1)}^{\beta}+\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(2)}^{\beta}\bigr)\,,

(4.53)

(4.54)

\displaystyle-\{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(1)}^{\beta}\}=\delta_{\alpha}^{\ \beta}\,\mathcal{Z}=\{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(2)}^{\beta}\}\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P_{a},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(m)}^{\alpha}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad\{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(m)}^{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(n)}^{\beta}\}=0\,,

(4.55)

(4.56)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q_{\alpha},\mathcal{Z}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P_{a},\mathcal{Z}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(m)}^{\alpha},\mathcal{Z}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}]=0\,,

(4.57)

(4.58)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{ab},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q_{\alpha}]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{ab}{}^{\beta}_{\ \alpha}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q_{\beta}\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{ab},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P_{c}]=\eta_{bc}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P_{a}-\eta_{ac}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P_{b}\,,

(4.59)

(4.60)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{ab},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(m)}^{\alpha}]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{ab}{}^{\alpha}_{\ \beta}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(m)}^{\beta}\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{ab},\mathcal{Z}]=0\,,

(4.61)

(4.62)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{ab},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{cd}]=\eta_{ad}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{bc}-\eta_{ac}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{bd}+\eta_{bc}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{ad}-\eta_{bd}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{ac}\,.

With
\,\zeta\,
denoting the super-1-form dual to
\,\mathcal{Z}\,
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}p\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ \mathfrak{l}\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{l}\,,

we obtain, similarly as before,

\displaystyle\widehat{\delta}\zeta=\pi_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}}^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\mathcal{F}}\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}p^{*}\pi_{\mathfrak{l}}^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\rm F}}\,.

The above Lie-superalgebra extension integrates to a central Lie-supergroup extension

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{1}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\ }{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{1}}

with the supermanifold structure

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}={\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\cong\mathscr{L}\times{\rm Spin}(d,1)

for which

\displaystyle\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\equiv\pi_{\mathscr{L}}\times{\rm id}_{{\rm Spin}(d,1)}\ :\ \widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\rm Spin}(d,1)\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,,

and with the Lie-supergroup structure determined by the binary operation

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}{\rm m}\ :\ \widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\times\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}

with the coordinate presentation

(4.66)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}{\rm m}\bigl(\bigl((\theta_{1},x_{1},\xi_{1,1},\phi_{1}),(\theta_{1},x_{1},\xi_{1,2},\phi_{1}),z_{1}\bigr),\bigl((\theta_{2},x_{2},\xi_{2,1},\phi_{2}),(\theta_{2},x_{2},\xi_{2,2},\phi_{2}),z_{2}\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}\bigl((\theta_{1},x_{1},\xi_{1,1},\phi_{1}),(\theta_{2},x_{2},\xi_{2,1},\phi_{2})\bigr),{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}\bigl((\theta_{1},x_{1},\xi_{1,2},\phi_{1}),(\theta_{2},x_{2},\xi_{2,2},\phi_{2})\bigr),

\displaystyle{\rm e}^{{\mathsf{i}}\,\theta_{1}^{\alpha}\,(\xi_{2,2}-\xi_{2,1})_{\beta}\,S(\phi_{1})^{-1\,\beta}_{\ \ \ \ \alpha}}\cdot z_{1}\cdot z_{2}\bigr)\,.

Its form ensures left-invariance of the super-1-form

\displaystyle\zeta\bigl((\theta,x,\xi_{1},\phi),(\theta,x,\xi_{2},\phi),z\bigr)=\tfrac{{\mathsf{i}}\,{\mathsf{d}}z}{z}+\theta^{\alpha}\,{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(\xi_{2\,\alpha}-\xi_{1\,\alpha}\bigr)\equiv\tfrac{{\mathsf{i}}\,{\mathsf{d}}z}{z}+{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\pi^{*}{\rm a}\bigl((\theta,x,\xi_{1},\phi),(\theta,x,\xi_{2},\phi)\bigr)\,.

Writing

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\pi\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ \mathscr{L}\times{\rm Spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow\mathscr{L}\,,

with

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathscr{L}}\circ\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\pi=\pi\circ\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\,,

we obtain

\displaystyle\zeta\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\pi^{*}\underline{\zeta}\,.

Once again, we end up with the structure of a (trivial) principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle

\displaystyle\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\ :\ \widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})

with the LI principal connection super-1-form

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\equiv\zeta

of curvature
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\mathcal{F}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}=\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\mathcal{F}}\,.

A reasoning fully analogous to the one presented in the case of
\,\mathscr{L}\,
leads to the trivial groupoid structure

\displaystyle\mu_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\equiv{\boldsymbol{1}}\ :\ {\rm pr}_{1,2}^{*}\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2,3}^{*}\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }{\rm pr}_{1,3}^{*}\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}

that isomorphically maps the Lie-supergroup structure on its domain to the one on its codomain. Also the latter admits a Lie-superalgebraic description, which we state hereunder for later reference. Its reconstruction starts with the self-explanatory definition of the pullback Lie superalgebras

with the respective bases

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{1,2}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}
