and the superbracket obtained from the direct-sum one on
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[3]}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\,
through restriction. Finally, we form the ‘tensor product’ of the first two,

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{1,2}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2,3}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\equiv\bigl({\rm pr}_{1,2}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\hskip 2.0pt{}_{{\rm pr}_{1}}\hskip-2.0pt\oplus_{{\rm pr}_{1}}{\rm pr}_{2,3}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\bigr)/_{\sim_{\mathbb{R}}}\,,

by identifying the generators

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathcal{Z}^{(1,2)},0\bigr)\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(0,\mathcal{Z}^{(2,3)}\bigr)\,,

so that

(4.96)

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{1,2}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2,3}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,2)}_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(2,3)}_{\alpha}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,2;2,3)}_{\alpha}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,\left\langle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,2)}_{a},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(2,3)}_{a}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,2;2,3)}_{a}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\bigoplus_{\beta=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(1)}^{(1,2)\,\beta},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(3)}^{(2,3)\,\beta}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(1,3)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\beta}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\gamma=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(3)}^{(1,2)\,\gamma},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(2)}^{(1,2)\,\gamma}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(3,2)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\gamma}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\bigoplus_{\delta=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(2)}^{(1,2)\,\delta},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(1)}^{(2,3)\,\delta}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(2,1)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\delta}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\bigl[\bigl(\mathcal{Z}^{(1,2)},0\bigr)\bigr]_{\sim_{\mathbb{R}}}\equiv\mathcal{Z}^{1,2;2,3}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\bigoplus_{b<c=0}^{d}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2)}_{bc},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(2,3)}_{bc}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2;2,3)}_{bc}\right\rangle\,,

with the superbracket (defined as the projection of the restricted direct-sum superbracket on
\,{\rm pr}_{1,2}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\oplus_{{\mathsf{Y}}^{[3]}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})}{\rm pr}_{2,3}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}
, computed for arbitrary representatives of the equivalence classes of arguments, back to the quotient)

\displaystyle\{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,2;2,3)}_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,2;2,3)}_{\beta}\}=\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}_{\alpha\beta}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,2;2,3)}_{a}\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,2;2,3)}_{a},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,2;2,3)}_{b}]=0\,,

(4.97)

(4.98)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,2;2,3)}_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,2;2,3)}_{a}]=\overline{\Gamma}_{a\,\alpha\beta}\,\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(1,3)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\beta}+\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(3,2)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\beta}+\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(2,1)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\beta}\bigr)\,,

(4.99)

(4.100)

\displaystyle-\{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,2;2,3)}_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(1,3)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\beta}\}=\delta_{\alpha}^{\ \beta}\,\mathcal{Z}^{(1,2;2,3)}=\{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,2;2,3)}_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(3,2)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\beta}\}\,,\qquad\qquad\{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,2;2,3)}_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(2,1)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\beta}\}=0\,,

(4.101)

(4.102)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,2;2,3)}_{a},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(m,n)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\alpha}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad\{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(m,n)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(r,s)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\beta}\}=0\,,

(4.103)

(4.104)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,2;2,3)}_{\alpha},\mathcal{Z}^{(1,2;2,3)}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,2;2,3)}_{a},\mathcal{Z}^{(1,2;2,3)}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(m,n)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\alpha},\mathcal{Z}^{(1,2;2,3)}]=0\,,

(4.105)

(4.106)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{Z}^{(1,2;2,3)},\mathcal{Z}^{(1,2;2,3)}]=0\,,

(4.107)

(4.108)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2;2,3)}_{ab},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,2;2,3)}_{\alpha}]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{ab}{}^{\beta}_{\ \alpha}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,2;2,3)}_{\beta}\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2;2,3)}_{ab},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,2;2,3)}_{c}]=\eta_{bc}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,2;2,3)}_{a}-\eta_{ac}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,2;2,3)}_{b}\,,

(4.109)

(4.110)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2;2,3)}_{ab},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(m,n)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\alpha}]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{ab}{}^{\alpha}_{\ \beta}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(m,n)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\beta}\,,\qquad\qquad[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2;2,3)}_{ab},\mathcal{Z}^{(1,2;2,3)}]=0\,,

(4.111)

(4.112)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2;2,3)}_{ab},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2;2,3)}_{cd}]=\eta_{ad}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2;2,3)}_{bc}-\eta_{ac}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2;2,3)}_{bd}+\eta_{bc}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2;2,3)}_{ad}-\eta_{bd}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2;2,3)}_{ac}\,.

Comparing the above with the superbracket of
\,{\rm pr}_{2,3}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}
, we infer the existence of a Lie-superalgebra isomorphism

\displaystyle\mu_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}}\ :\ {\rm pr}_{1,2}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2,3}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }{\rm pr}_{1,3}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}

given by the unique linear extension of the assignment

(4.114)

\displaystyle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,2;2,3)}_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,2;2,3)}_{a},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(1,3)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\beta},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(3,2)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\gamma},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(2,1)}^{(1,2;2,3)\,\delta},\mathcal{Z}^{1,2;2,3},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,2;2,3)}_{bc}\bigr)

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Q^{(1,3)}_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P^{(1,3)}_{a},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(1)}^{(1,3)\,\beta},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(2)}^{(1,3)\,\gamma},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}Z_{(3)}^{(1,3)\,\delta},\mathcal{Z}^{1,3},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J^{(1,3)}_{bc}\bigr)\,.

This is the Lie-superalgebraic counterpart of the groupoid structure
\,\mu_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}
, its triviality being reflected in the identity

\displaystyle\mu_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}}\bigl(\mathcal{Z}^{1,2;2,3}\bigr)=\mathcal{Z}^{1,3}\,,

which we encode in the same notation:

\displaystyle\mu_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}}\equiv{\boldsymbol{1}}

as the one used for the trivial
\,\mu_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}
.

By the end of the long day, we conclude that

Theorem 1
.

The GS super-1-gerbe over
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
canonically lifts (
{\rm Spin}(d,1)
-equivariantly) to a CaE super-1-gerbe over
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
.

The resulting CaE super-1-gerbe

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}:=\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1}),\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})},\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}},\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}},\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}},\mu_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\bigr)\equiv\pi^{*}\mathcal{G}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\,,

a
distinguished
(
{\rm Spin}(d,1)
-equivariant) pullback of
\,\mathcal{G}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}
, shall be called the
lifted Green–Schwarz super-1-gerbe over
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. It constitutes the point of departure of a full-fledged ‘gerbification’ of the GS super-
\sigma
-model in the purely topological HP formulation that we shall carry out in what follows. Its first step consists in extending
\,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\,
by the trivial CaE super-1-gerbe

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{2{\rm Vol}(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac})}\equiv\bigl({\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1}),{\rm id}_{{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})},2{\rm Vol}\bigl(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}\bigr),{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times},{\rm pr}_{1},{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\vartheta_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}},{\boldsymbol{1}}\bigr)

over the supersymmetry group
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
associated with the LI super-2-form
\,2{\rm Vol}(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac})\,
(featuring as its curving). Above, the total space
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\ni(\theta^{\alpha},x^{a},z)\,
of the trivial principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ {\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\longrightarrow{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})

carries the product Lie-supergroup structure and comes equipped with the trivial principal connection super-1-form
\,{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\vartheta_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}}\,
with the coordinate presentation

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\vartheta_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}}\bigl((\theta,x),z\bigr)\equiv\vartheta_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}}(z):=\tfrac{{\mathsf{i}}\,{\mathsf{d}}z}{z}\,,

manifestly LI with respect to the said Lie-supergroup structure. The tensor product

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\otimes\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{2{\rm Vol}(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac})}=\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1}),\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})},\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}+2{\mathsf{Y}}\pi^{*}{\rm Vol}\bigl(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac}\bigr)\equiv\widehat{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}},\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}},\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}},\mu_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\bigr)\equiv\widehat{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm HP}

is also a CaE super-1-gerbe, to be referred to – after Ref.
[
Sus19
, Def. 6.5]
, but taking into account its supersymmetry established above – the
extended Hughes–Polchinski super-1-gerbe over

\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. The 1-gerbe over
\,\Sigma^{\rm HP}\,
with restrictions
\,\widehat{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm HP}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\,
over the components
\,\mathcal{V}_{i}\,
of that supermanifold shall be denoted as

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\Sigma^{\rm HP}}\equiv\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,\widehat{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm HP}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\,.

In the remainder of the present paper, we investigate at great length structural properties of its
vacuum restriction

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}{}^{(1)}_{\rm vac}\equiv\iota_{\rm vac}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\Sigma^{\rm HP}}\equiv\widehat{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\Sigma^{\rm HP}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\Sigma^{\rm HP}_{\rm vac}}=:\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}\Sigma^{\rm HP}_{\rm vac},\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\Sigma^{\rm HP}_{\rm vac}},\widehat{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}}{}_{\rm vac},\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}{}_{\rm vac},\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}{}_{\rm vac}},\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}{}_{\rm vac}},\mu_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}{}_{\rm vac}}\bigr)\,,

with view to understanding the quantum-mechanical aspect of the vacuum of the GS super-
\sigma
-model and of its global and local supersymmetry, as encoded by the (super-)gerbe theory of the field theory of interest.

5.
The supersymmetry of the super-1-gerbe(s)

Prequantisable symmetries of the (super-)
\sigma
-model have specific gerbe-theoretic manifestations that ensure the existence of their consistent lift to the Hilbert space of the (super)field theory. These have been known for quite some time from the extensive study of the subject carried out in the non-
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded geometric category. They fall into the two classes, mentioned previously, with a fundamentaly different ontological status and, accordingly, a different higher-geometric implementation, to wit, the global and the local symmetries that we discuss in sequence hereunder.

5.1.
Higher global supersymmetry

We begin with
global
symmetries that set in correspondence
inequivalent
field configurations. In the non-
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting, these are represented by families of 1-gerbe 1-isomorphisms indexed by elements of the symmetry group that identify the 1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}^{(1)}\,
of the
\sigma
-model as
invariant
under the element-wise realisation of the group, a fact established firmly in Refs.
[
Sus11a
,
Sus11b
]
. More specifically, given a Lie group
\,{\rm G}\,
of those isometries of the target
\,M\,
whose action on fields of the
\sigma
-model induced from its action

\displaystyle\lambda_{\cdot}\ :\ {\rm G}\times M\longrightarrow M\ :\ (g,m)\longmapsto\lambda_{g}(m)

on the target preserves the DF amplitude, we demand the existence of 1-isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Phi_{g}\ :\ \lambda_{g}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{(1)}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{G}^{(1)}\,,\qquad g\in{\rm G}\,.

In the case of a homogeneous space
\,{\rm G}/{\rm K}\,
of a supersymmetry Lie supergroup
\,{\rm G}\,
(relative to its Lie subgroup
\,{\rm K}
), this simple scenario requires, in general, a straightforward sheaf-theoretic adaptation that separately takes into account invariance under the element-wise action

\displaystyle[|\ell|]^{\rm K}_{\cdot}\ :\ |{\rm G}|\longrightarrow{\rm Aut}_{\rm{\bf sMan}}\bigl({\rm G}/{\rm K}\bigr)\ :\ g\longmapsto[\ell]^{\rm K}\circ\bigl(\widehat{g}\times{\rm id}_{{\rm G}/{\rm K}}\bigr)

of the body Lie group
\,|G|
,

\displaystyle|\Phi|_{g}\ :\ [|\ell|]^{{\rm K}\,*}_{g}\mathcal{G}^{(1)}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{G}^{(1)}\,,\qquad g\in|{\rm G}|\,,

and that under the element-wise tangential action of the Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
of the Lie supergroup
\,{\rm G}
,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}\Phi_{X}\ :\ \,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{\mathcal{K}_{X}}\mathcal{G}^{(1)}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{0}\,,\qquad X\in\mathfrak{g}\,.

Here,
\,\,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{\mathcal{K}_{X}}\mathcal{G}^{(1)}\,
is a super-1-gerbe obtained from
\,\mathcal{G}^{(1)}\,
by Lie-differentiating local data of the latter in the direction of the fundamental vector field
\,\mathcal{K}_{X}\,
for the induced action
\,[\ell]^{\rm K}\,
of
\,{\rm G}\,
on
\,{\rm G}/{\rm K}
, and
\,\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{0}\,
is the flat trivial super-1-gerbe with a null curving. In the super-minkowskian setting, we may readily put the components of the structure sheaf of the supersymmetry supergroup
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
of both parities on the same footing by using the global generators
\,\theta^{\alpha}\,
of the Graßmann-odd component of that sheaf, alongside the remaining coordinates
\,(x^{a},\phi^{bc})
, and demand the existence of 1-isomorphisms that we write, in a self-explanatory notation
16
16

16

That is, in the coordinate picture, in which
[\ell]^{{\rm Spin}(d,1)}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\equiv[\ell]^{{\rm Spin}(d,1)}((\varepsilon,y,\psi),\cdot)
.
, as

\displaystyle\Phi_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\ :\ [\ell]^{{\rm Spin}(d,1)\,*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\mathcal{G}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{G}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\,,\qquad(\varepsilon,y,\psi)\in{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,.

Actually, we shall go one step further and consider, instead, the corresponding 1-isomorphisms

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}{}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\ :\ \ell^{*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\,,\qquad(\varepsilon,y,\psi)\in{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,.

for its lift. In so doing, we get a chance to appreciate the structural merits of the geometrisation scheme adopted in which the implementation of the global supersymmetry is seen to essentially trivialise. Thus, we take as the surjective submersion of the pullback super-1-gerbe
\,\ell^{*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\,
the very same one as for
\,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\,
– that this makes sense follows from the identity

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\circ\widehat{\ell}{}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}=\ell_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\circ\pi_{\ell^{*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\,,\qquad\qquad\pi_{\ell^{*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\equiv\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\,,

written for
\,\widehat{\ell}{}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}\ell_{(\varepsilon,y,0,\psi)}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}\ell((\varepsilon,y,0,\psi),\cdot)\,
and ensured by the equivariance of
\,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})}
. For this choice of the surjective submersion, we find

\displaystyle\widehat{\ell}{}^{*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}=\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}\,,

and so we infer that
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}\,
is the curving of the pullback super-1-gerbe. Continuing along these lines, we take
\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\,
as the principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle of the pullback super-1-gerbe, a choice legitimised by the identity

\displaystyle\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\circ\widehat{\ell}{}^{[2]}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}={\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\ell_{(\varepsilon,y,0,\psi)}\circ\pi_{\widehat{\ell}{}^{\times 2\,*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\,,\qquad\qquad\pi_{\widehat{\ell}{}^{\times 2\,*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\equiv\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}

in which
\,\widehat{\ell}{}^{[2]}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\equiv\mathscr{L}\ell_{(\varepsilon,y,0,\psi,1)}\equiv\mathscr{L}\ell((\varepsilon,y,0,\psi,1),\cdot)
. The left-invariance of
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\ell}{}^{[2]\,*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}=\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\,,

now permits us to take
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\,
as the principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-connection on the pullback principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle. The construction is consistently completed by taking

\displaystyle\widehat{\ell}{}^{[3]\,*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}\equiv\mu_{\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}}

as the groupoid structure of the pullback super-1-gerbe (for an obvious definition of
\,\widehat{\ell}{}^{[3]}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}
). Altogether, then, we obtain

\displaystyle\ell^{*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\equiv\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\,,

whence also

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}{}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\equiv{\rm id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}}\,.

Given the nature of the trivial correction
\,\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{2{\rm Vol}(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}_{\rm vac})}
, we ultimately obtain 1-isomorphisms

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}{}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\equiv{\rm id}_{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}}\ :\ \ell^{*}_{(\varepsilon,y,\psi)}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\widehat{\mathcal{G}}{}^{(1)}_{\rm GS}\,,\qquad(\varepsilon,y,\psi)\in{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,.

This is the anticipated higher-geometric realisation of the global supersymmetry of the GS super-
\sigma
-model in the HP formulation.

5.2.
Higher
\kappa
-symmetry & the
{\bf sLieAlg}
-skeleton of the vacuum

Next, we pass to
local
symmetries that relate (
gauge
-)
equivalent
field configurations, or – according to the passive interpretation of symmetry – different (and equivalent) coordinate
descriptions
of a given field configuration, and signal reducibility of the set of degrees of freedom of the field theory to those charting the space of orbits of the action of the gauge group. Whenever an action
\,\lambda\,
of a group
\,{\rm G}\,
of global symmetries of a
\sigma
-model with the target
\,M\,
is rendered local, or gauged, the ensuing (gauged) field theory effectively describes a
\sigma
-model on the orbispace
\,M/{\rm G}
, or actually descends to the quotient manifold if the latter exists,
cp
Ref.
[
Sus12
, Sec. 8]
and Ref.
[
GSW13
, Sec. 9]
, which happens,
e.g.
, when
\,\lambda\,
is free and proper. A quantum-mechanical consistency of the descent of the
\sigma
-model to the orbispace calls for the existence of a
{\rm G}
-equivariant structure on the associated 1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}^{(1)}\,
(of, say, curvature
\,{\rm curv}\,(\mathcal{G}^{(1)})\in Z_{\rm dR}^{3}(M)
), to arise over the nerve
\,{\mathsf{N}}^{\bullet}({\rm G}{\hskip-1.13791pt\ltimes\hskip-1.42271pt}M)\equiv{\rm G}^{\bullet}\times M

(5.1)

. The first component of the structure is a 1-isomorphism

\displaystyle\Upsilon\ :\ d_{1}^{(1)\,*}\mathcal{G}^{(1)}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }d_{0}^{(1)\,*}\mathcal{G}^{(1)}\otimes\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{\varrho_{\theta_{\rm L}}}

, the

\,\mathfrak{g}
, and the
\,\kappa_{A}\,
are 1-forms on
\,M\,
satisfying the identities

\sigma

between the 1-isomorphisms over
\,{\rm G}^{\times 2}\times M
, satisfying, over
\,{\rm G}^{\times 3}\times M
, the coherence condition

\displaystyle d_{1}^{(3)\,*}\gamma\bullet\bigl({\rm id}_{(d_{2}^{(2)}\circ d_{1}^{(3)})^{*}\Upsilon}\circ d_{3}^{(3)\,*}\gamma\bigr)=d_{2}^{(3)\,*}\gamma\bullet\bigl(\bigl(d_{0}^{(3)\,*}\gamma\otimes{\rm id}_{{\rm id}_{\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{(d_{2}^{(2)}\circ d_{1}^{(3)})^{*}\varrho_{\theta_{\rm L}}}}}\bigr)\circ{\rm id}_{(d_{2}^{(2)}\circ d_{3}^{(3)})^{*}\Upsilon}\bigr)

in which
\,\circ\,
and
\,\bullet\,
are the horizontal and vertical compositions of 1-gerbe 2-isomorphisms, respectively. Thus, altogether, a 1-gerbe with a
{\rm G}
-equivariant structure relative to the 2-form
\,\varrho_{\theta_{\rm L}}\,
is the triple

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{(1)},\Upsilon,\gamma\bigr)

as defined and constrained by the conditions of coherence above. The principle of descent for
\,\lambda\,
such that
\,M/{\rm G}\,

is
a smooth manifold is now encoded in the equivalence

(5.9)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Grb}^{\nabla}(M/{\rm G})\cong\mathfrak{Grb}^{\nabla}(M)^{\rm G}_{\varrho_{\theta_{\rm L}}\equiv 0}

between the bicategory
\,\mathfrak{Grb}^{\nabla}(M/{\rm G})\,
of 1-gerbes (with a connective structure) over
\,M/{\rm G}\,
and the bicategory
\,\mathfrak{Grb}^{\nabla}(M)^{\rm G}_{\varrho_{\theta_{\rm L}}\equiv 0}\,
of 1-gerbes (with a connective structure) over
\,M\,
with a
{\rm G}
-equivariant structure
relative to

\,\varrho_{\theta_{\rm L}}\equiv 0
,
cp
Ref.
[
GSW10
, Thm. 5.3]
. Generically, the 2-form
\,\varrho_{\theta_{\rm L}}\,
does
not
vanish, and then a fairly complex construction of Refs.
[
GSW10
,
Sus12
,
GSW13
,
Sus13
]
has to be carried out to formulate loop dynamics with the global symmetry
\,{\rm G}\,
gauged. The construction employs a bundle
\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\rm G}\times_{\lambda}M\,
associated with a principal
{\rm G}
-bundle
\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\rm G}\,
over
\,\Sigma\,
and endowed with the Crittenden connection induced from that on
\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\rm G}\,
and with an action of the gauge group
\,\Gamma({\rm Ad}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\rm G})\,
of global sections of the adjoint bundle
\,{\rm Ad}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\rm G}\equiv{\mathsf{P}}_{\rm G}\times_{\rm Ad}{\rm G}
. It goes well beyond the classic minimal-coupling scheme. The loop dynamics descends to
\,M/{\rm G}\,
if the latter exists as a smooth manifold, or is taken to
model
it otherwise. It may also happen that the 1-gerbe of the
\sigma
-model carries a
{\rm G}
-equivariant structure relative to the vanishing 2-form
\,\varrho_{\theta_{\rm L}}\equiv 0
, in which case the 1-gerbe, and with it the
\sigma
-model, directly descends to resp. models loop dynamics on the orbispace
\,M/{\rm G}
. This is the very special situation that we encouter below.

Bearing in mind that the existence of a gerbe-theoretic realisation of a symmetry is requisite for its quantum-mechanical consistency, we shall, now, put the
\kappa
-symmetry of Sec.
3.2
in the above framework. In trying to do that, though, we stumble upon a peculiarity
17
17

17

For a general supertarget
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}_{\rm vac}\,
realised patchwise within
\,{\rm G}\,
by means of local sections of the principal
{\rm H}_{\rm vac}
-bundle
\,{\rm G}\longrightarrow{\rm G}/{\rm H}_{\rm vac}
, there is yet another problematic peculiarity that we encounter, to wit, the symmetry seems to be well-defined (
on

\,{\rm G}
) only in its infinitesimal (tangential) form due to the intrinsic ambiguities of the patchwise realisation over intersections of elements of the trivialising cover of
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}_{\rm vac}
.
of the symmetry that takes us out of the standard scheme: The symmetry is realised in its full and integrated form only after imposition of the Euler–Lagrange equations of the super-
\sigma
-model,
i.e.
, it is a gauge symmetry of the vacuum. Therefore, when looking for a higher-geometric signature of
\kappa
-symmetry, we should investigate the vacuum restriction
\,\mathcal{G}{}^{(1)}_{\rm vac}\,
of the extended HP super-1-gerbe. Taking into account the higher-geometric interpretation of gauge symmetries, we seek to establish a
{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}
-equivariant structure on the latter. The very definition of the
\kappa
-symmetry superdistribution (and of the limit of its weak derived flag) makes it obvious that the structure, if present, is descendable – indeed,
\,\mathcal{G}{}^{(1)}_{\rm vac}\,
is a flat 1-gerbe. But then the correspondence (
5.9
) in conjunction with our earlier description of the leaves of
\,\Sigma^{\rm HP}_{\rm vac}\,
as full orbits of
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\,
leads us to posit, as a hypothesis to be verified, the nullity of
\,\mathcal{G}{}^{(1)}_{\rm vac}
, that is the existence of a 1-isomorphism

\displaystyle\tau\ :\ \mathcal{G}{}^{(1)}_{\rm vac}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{0}\,,

in which
\,\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{0}\,
is to be understood as (the pullback of) the unique 1-gerbe over the 0-dimensional orbispace of a leaf of the lifted vacuum foliation with respect to the action of the
\kappa
-symmetry group. This hypothesis was first formulated (without a mention of the lift) in Ref.
[
Sus20
, Rem. 7.15]
,
cp
also Ref.
[
Sus21
, Sec. 7]
. Below, we prove it directly (essentially in the de Rham cohomology) and in a more manifestly supersymmetric procedure, suggested by the track of thought delineated in Ref.
[
Sus21
]
, in which we stay in the tangent sheaf of a leaf of
\,\Sigma^{\rm HP}_{\rm vac}\,
and exploit the Lie-superalgebra structure on its model
\,\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})
.

We begin our investigation on the leaf
\,\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\subset\Sigma^{\rm HP}_{\rm vac}\cap\mathcal{V}_{i}\,
of the vacuum superdistribution (
2.56
), embedded in the superdomain with the previously introduced local coordinates
\,(\theta^{\alpha},x^{a},\phi^{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}_{i})
. We have (keeping the pullbacks by
\,\iota_{\rm vac}\,
implicit to unburden the notation)

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\widehat{\chi}}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}=0\,,

and so we pass to consider the curving of the extended HP super-1-gerbe restricted to

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\ni\bigl(\theta^{\alpha},x^{a},\xi_{\beta},\phi^{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}_{i}\bigr)\,,

whereby we find

\displaystyle\widehat{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}}(\theta,x,\xi,\phi_{i})={\mathsf{d}}\xi_{\alpha}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\theta^{\alpha}+2L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,0}_{\ \ \ \ a}\,L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,1}_{\ \ \ \ b}\,{\mathsf{d}}x^{a}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}x^{b}+\widetilde{\Delta}(\theta,x,\phi_{i})\,,

with

(5.11)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}(\theta,x,\phi_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,0}_{\ \ \ \ a}\,L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,1}_{\ \ \ \ b}\,\bigl({\mathsf{d}}x^{a}\wedge\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{b}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta-{\mathsf{d}}x^{b}\wedge\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\wedge\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{b}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\bigr)

\displaystyle-p^{\underline{a}}(\theta,x,\phi_{i})\wedge S(\phi_{i})^{-1}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{\underline{a}}\,q(\theta,x,\phi_{i})\,.

However, on
\,\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}
, where

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma}{}^{1}\,q\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}=-\overline{\Gamma}{}^{0}\,q\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\,,

we obtain the identity

\displaystyle L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,0}_{\ \ \ \ a}\,L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,1}_{\ \ \ \ b}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\wedge\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{b}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta=0\,,

and so also

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}(\theta,x,\phi_{i})=0\,,

whence also (for the totally skew tensor
\,\epsilon_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}=-\epsilon_{\underline{b}\underline{a}}\,
with
\,\epsilon_{01}=1
)

(5.12)

\displaystyle\widehat{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}}(\theta,x,\xi,\phi_{i})={\mathsf{d}}\bigl(\theta^{\alpha}\,{\mathsf{d}}\xi_{\alpha}+\epsilon_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,\underline{a}}_{\ \ \ \ c}\,L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,\underline{b}}_{\ \ \ \ d}\,x^{c}\,{\mathsf{d}}x^{d}\bigr)\,.

Indeed, the identities

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,\underline{a}}_{\ \ \ \ b}=-j^{\underline{a}\underline{c}}(\theta,x,\phi_{i})\,\eta_{\underline{c}\underline{d}}\,L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,\underline{d}}_{\ \ \ \ b}

that obtain on
\,\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\,
yield the desired result

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(\epsilon_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,\underline{a}}_{\ \ \ \ c}\,L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,\underline{b}}_{\ \ \ \ d}\,x^{c}\,{\mathsf{d}}x^{d}\bigr)-2L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,0}_{\ \ \ \ a}\,L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,1}_{\ \ \ \ b}\,{\mathsf{d}}x^{a}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}x^{b}=0\,.

Following the standard gerbe-theoretic procedure, we erect a trivial principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle over
\,\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}
,

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ \mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\,,

and endow it with the principal connection super-1-form (
(\theta^{\alpha},x^{a},\phi^{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}_{i},\xi_{\beta},z)\in{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
)

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}(\theta,x,\xi,\phi_{i},z)=\tfrac{{\mathsf{i}}\,{\mathsf{d}}z}{z}-\theta^{\alpha}\,{\mathsf{d}}\xi_{\alpha}-\epsilon_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,\underline{a}}_{\ \ \ \ c}\,L(\phi_{i})^{-1\,\underline{b}}_{\ \ \ \ d}\,x^{c}\,{\mathsf{d}}x^{d}=:\tfrac{{\mathsf{i}}\,{\mathsf{d}}z}{z}+{\rm A}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}(\theta,x,\xi,\phi_{i})\,.

The bundle may subsequently be pulled back to
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\times_{\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\,
along the canonical projections
\,{\rm pr}_{n}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}},\ n\in\{1,2\}
, whereupon we obtain the two (trivial) principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundles

\displaystyle\pi_{{\rm pr}_{n}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ {\rm pr}_{n}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\hskip 2.0pt{}_{{\rm pr}_{n}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{\pi_{\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}}\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\,,\qquad n\in\{1,2\}

with

\displaystyle\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{n}\equiv{\rm pr}_{2}\ :\ {\rm pr}_{n}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}

and with the respective principal connection super-1-forms

\displaystyle\widehat{\rm pr}{}^{*}_{n}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\equiv{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\,.

Next, we tensor the second of these bundles,
\,{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}
, with the restriction of the principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle
\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\,
of the extended HP super-1-gerbe to
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\,
and look for a connection-preserving principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle isomorphism

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\ :\ \widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\,.

Direct comparison of the base components of the respective connection super-1-forms,

\displaystyle\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\pi^{*}{\rm a}+{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm A}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\bigr)\bigl((\theta,x,\xi_{1},\phi_{i}),(\theta,x,\xi_{2},\phi_{i})\bigr)={\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm A}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\bigl((\theta,x,\xi_{1},\phi_{i}),(\theta,x,\xi_{2},\phi_{i})\bigr)\,,

indicates that we may take the isomorphism in the trivial form, with the coordinate presentation

(5.14)

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\bigl(\bigl((\theta,x,\xi_{1},\phi_{i}),(\theta,x,\xi_{2},\phi_{i}),1\bigr)\otimes\bigl((\theta,x,\xi_{1},\phi_{i}),(\theta,x,\xi_{2},\phi_{i}),(\theta,x,\xi_{2},\phi_{i},z)\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl((\theta,x,\xi_{1},\phi_{i}),(\theta,x,\xi_{2},\phi_{i}),(\theta,x,\xi_{1},\phi_{i},z)\bigr)\,,

or, symbolically,

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\equiv{\boldsymbol{1}}\,.

This is automatically compatible with the (trivial) groupoid structure on (the fibres of)
\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}
, and so we conclude that the quadruple

\displaystyle\tau_{i,\upsilon_{i}}=\bigl(\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}},\pi_{\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}},\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}},\alpha_{\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\bigr)

defines a trivialisation

\displaystyle\tau_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\ :\ \mathcal{G}{}^{(1)}_{\rm vac}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{0}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{D}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\,.

Combining the local trivialisations over the entire vacuum foliation gives us the sought-after global trivialisation

(5.15)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\tau\equiv(\mathscr{E},\pi_{\mathscr{E}},\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\mathscr{E}},\alpha_{\mathscr{E}}\bigr)\ :\ \mathcal{G}{}^{(1)}_{\rm vac}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{0}\,,\qquad\qquad\tau\equiv\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,\bigsqcup_{\upsilon_{i}\in\Upsilon_{i}}\,\tau_{i,\upsilon_{i}}\,.

Of course, ultimately, we want to make statements about the extended HP super-1-gerbe
descended
to the
physical
vacuum foliation
\,\Sigma^{\rm HP}_{\rm phys\ vac}
. For the results of the above analysis to descend to the homogeneous space
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\supset\Sigma^{\rm HP}_{\rm phys\ vac}
, we need essentially to equip
\,\tau\,
with a
descendable

{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
-equivariant structure. Luckily, our construction provides us with such a structure, namely – the trivial one. Indeed, upon invoking the relation between the local sections
\,\sigma^{\rm vac}_{i}\,
of Eq. (
2.34
) over nonempty intersections
\,\mathcal{U}^{\rm vac}_{ij}\equiv\mathcal{U}^{\rm vac}_{i}\cap\mathcal{U}^{\rm vac}_{j}\,
of superdomains
\,\mathcal{U}^{\rm vac}_{i}\,
(with global coordinates
\,(\theta^{\alpha},x^{a},\xi_{\beta},\phi^{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}_{i})
) and
\,\mathcal{U}^{\rm vac}_{j}\,
(with global coordinates
\,(\theta^{\alpha},x^{a},\xi_{\beta},\phi^{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}_{j})
),

\displaystyle\sigma^{\rm vac}_{j}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{U}^{\rm vac}_{ij}}=|\wp|_{h_{ij}\circ{\rm pr}_{2}}\bigl(\sigma^{\rm vac}_{i}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{U}^{\rm vac}_{ij}}\bigr)\,,

expressed in terms of the transition maps
\,h_{ij}\ :\ \mathcal{O}_{ij}\longrightarrow{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
of the principal
{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}
-bundle
\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)\longrightarrow{\rm Spin}(d,1)/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
(inherited by that of Eq. (
2.26
)), we readily verify the desired gluing property for the base components of the relevant restrictions
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\mathscr{E}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}}\,
and
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\mathscr{E}_{j,\upsilon_{j}}}\,
of the principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-connection
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\mathcal{A}}{}_{\mathscr{E}}\,
on
\,\mathscr{E}
,

\displaystyle{\rm A}_{j,\upsilon_{j}}(\theta,x,\xi,\phi_{j})={\rm A}_{i,\upsilon_{i}}(\theta,x,\xi,\phi_{i})\,,

which follows from the block-diagonal structure of the matrix
\,L(h)\,
for
\,h\in{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
with respect to the decomposition
\,\mathfrak{mink}(d,1)=\left\langle P_{0},P_{1}\right\rangle\oplus(\bigoplus_{\widehat{a}=2}^{d}\,\left\langle P_{\widehat{a}}\right\rangle)
. We conclude that

Theorem 2
.

The super-1-gerbe of the GS super-
\sigma
-model in the HP formulation descended from the extended HP super-1-gerbe to the supertarget
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})/{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,
trivialises upon restriction to the vacuum of the (super)field theory.

The main principle underlying the scheme of geometrisation proposed in Ref.
[
Sus17
]
(and recalled in Sec.
4
) is the
invariance
of all structures under consideration with respect to the global supersymmetry
enforced through restriction
of the standard constructions (of 1-gerbes, their 1- and 2-isomorphisms) due to Murray
et al.
to the category of Lie supergroups. There seems to be no obvious way of implementing this principle in the last construction that leads up to Theorem 2. – quite simply because there is no natural Lie-supergroup structure on the vacuum of the super-
\sigma
-model. Rather than trying to save the day, at least partially, by imposing invariance with respect to a residual global supersymmetry of the vacuum
18
18

18

Cp
Ref.
[
Sus20
, Prop. 5.5]
.
(an idea that we leave for a future study), we rectify the present situation by passing to the tangent sheaves of the various supermanifolds entering the definition of the vacuum restriction
\,\mathcal{G}{}^{(1)}_{\rm vac}\,
of the extended HP super-1-gerbe of the superstring and read off the Lie-superalgebraic trace of the trivialisation (
5.15
).

The point of departure of our analysis is the concise restatement of a faithful Lie-superalgebraic model of the composite diagram

in which

(5.18)

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{0}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{e}\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{\mathfrak{e}}\ }{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{vac}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{0}}

is a central extension determined by the super-2-cocycle

\displaystyle\widehat{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}}{}_{\rm vac}={\mathsf{Y}}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widehat{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}}

and such that the super-1-form
\,\breve{\zeta}{}_{\mathscr{E}}\,
on
\,\mathfrak{e}\,
dual to the central generator given as the image of
\,1\in{\mathbb{R}}\,
in
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})\,
trivialises the pullback of
\,\widehat{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}}{}_{\rm vac}\,
along
\,\pi_{\mathfrak{e}}
,

(5.19)

\displaystyle\widehat{\delta}\breve{\zeta}{}_{\mathscr{E}}=-\pi_{\mathfrak{e}}^{*}\widehat{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}}{}_{\rm vac}\,,

and in which
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\,
is a central extension

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{0}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ }{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathfrak{vac}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{0}}

consistent with that of Eq. (
4.52
) in the sense expressed by the diagram

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 59.62825pt\raise-117.35712pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.70335pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 107.28749pt\raise-110.29266pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.15419pt\raise-110.29266pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 107.28749pt\raise-110.29266pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 78.02917pt\raise-144.21947pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-146.96762pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 66.2792pt\raise-146.96762pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{0}}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 147.52068pt\raise-146.96762pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

with the Lie-superalgebra monomorphism
\,\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\jmath_{\rm vac}
. A constructive positive answer to the question thus posed is laid out below.

The structure of the first extension,
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})
, is readily read off from the commutator
\,[{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha},{\mathsf{Y}}P_{a}]\,
of
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})
: Upon restricting to the generators
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\,
spanning
\,{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}{}^{\rm T}\,
within the sub-superspace
\,\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle{\mathsf{Y}}Q_{\alpha}\right\rangle\,
and to the
\,{\mathsf{Y}}P_{\underline{a}}\,
spanning the lift of
\,\mathfrak{t}_{\rm vac}^{(0)}
, we are naturally restricted to the subspace

\displaystyle{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\equiv\left\langle\ {\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}{}^{\alpha}_{\ \beta}\,Z^{\beta}\ |\ \alpha\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}\ \right\rangle\subset\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle Z^{\alpha}\right\rangle\,.

Denote its basis as
\,\{\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\}_{\underline{\alpha}\in\overline{1,\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}}\,
to postulate

(5.20)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigl(\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle{\mathsf{Y}}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle{\mathsf{Y}}P_{0},{\mathsf{Y}}P_{1}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{\beta}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}}\right\rangle\bigr)\oplus\bigl(\left\langle{\mathsf{Y}}J_{01}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\widehat{a}<\widehat{b}=2}^{d}\,\left\langle{\mathsf{Y}}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}\right\rangle\bigr)

(5.22)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}

with the Lie-superalgebra structure induced by the restriction of the superbracket of
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. We confirm the self-consistency of the postulate by inspecting the brackets

\displaystyle\bigl[{\mathsf{Y}}J_{01},\bigl({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\,Z\bigr)^{\alpha}]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{01}{}^{\alpha}_{\ \beta}\,\bigl({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\,Z\bigr)^{\beta}

and

\displaystyle\bigl[{\mathsf{Y}}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},\bigl({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\,Z\bigr)^{\alpha}]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}{}^{\alpha}_{\ \beta}\,\bigl({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\,Z\bigr)^{\beta}\,.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{d,1}\equiv\tfrac{D_{d,1}}{2}

and

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})}\equiv\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})}\,.

On the new Lie superalgebra, we find the super-2-form

\displaystyle\widehat{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}}{}_{\rm vac}=\breve{z}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\wedge\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})}^{*}\breve{q}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}+2\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})}^{*}\bigl(p^{0}\wedge p^{1}\bigr)\,,

written in terms of the duals
\,\breve{z}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\,
of the
\,\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\,
and the duals
\,\breve{q}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\,
of the
\,\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}
. The super-2-form satifies the identity

\displaystyle\widehat{\delta}\widehat{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{{\mathsf{Y}}\beta}}{}_{\rm vac}=\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widehat{\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(3)$}}{\chi}}\equiv 0\,,

and so it determines a central extension (
5.18
) with the supervector-space structure

(5.24)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\mathscr{E}P_{0},\mathscr{E}P_{1}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{\beta}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\breve{\mathcal{Z}}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\mathscr{E}J_{01}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\widehat{a}<\widehat{b}=2}^{d}\,\left\langle\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}\bigr)\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}

and the associated projection

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathfrak{e}}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\oplus{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}}\ :\ \bigl({\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}\bigr)\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\oplus\mathfrak{spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\,,

and with the superbrackets

\displaystyle\{\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}},\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\beta}}\}=\overline{\gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\,\mathscr{E}P_{\underline{a}}\,,\qquad\qquad[\mathscr{E}P_{0},\mathscr{E}P_{1}]=2\breve{\mathcal{Z}}\,,\qquad\qquad[\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}},\mathscr{E}P_{\underline{a}}]=\overline{\gamma}_{\underline{a}}{}_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\,\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}}\,,

(5.25)

(5.26)

\displaystyle\{\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}},\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}}\}=-\delta_{\underline{\alpha}}^{\ \underline{\beta}}\,\breve{\mathcal{Z}}\,,\qquad\qquad[\mathscr{E}P_{\underline{a}},\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad\{\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}}\}=0\,,

(5.27)

(5.28)

\displaystyle[\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{\mathcal{Z}}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\mathscr{E}P_{\underline{a}},\breve{\mathcal{Z}}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\breve{\mathcal{Z}},\breve{\mathcal{Z}}]=0\,,

(5.29)

(5.30)

\displaystyle[\mathscr{E}J_{01},\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\gamma_{01}{}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\ \underline{\alpha}}\,\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\beta}}\,,\qquad\qquad[\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\gamma_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}{}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\ \underline{\alpha}}\,\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\beta}}\,,

(5.31)

(5.32)

\displaystyle[\mathscr{E}J_{01},\mathscr{E}P_{\underline{a}}]=\delta_{1\underline{a}}\,\mathscr{E}P_{0}+\delta_{0\underline{a}}\,P_{1}\,,\qquad\qquad[\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},\mathscr{E}P_{\underline{c}}]=0\,,

(5.33)

(5.34)

\displaystyle[\mathscr{E}J_{01},\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\gamma_{01}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ \underline{\beta}}\,\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}}\,,\qquad\qquad[\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\gamma_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ \underline{\beta}}\,\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}}\,,

(5.35)

(5.36)

\displaystyle[\mathscr{E}J_{01},\breve{\mathcal{Z}}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},\breve{\mathcal{Z}}]=0\,,

(5.37)

(5.38)

\displaystyle[\mathscr{E}J_{01},\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{c}\widehat{d}}]=\delta_{\widehat{a}\widehat{d}}\,\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}-\delta_{\widehat{a}\widehat{c}}\,\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{d}}+\delta_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\,\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{d}}-\delta_{\widehat{b}\widehat{d}}\,\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{c}}\,.

Let
\,\breve{\zeta}{}_{\mathscr{E}}\,
be the dual of
\,\breve{\mathcal{Z}}
. Clearly, it satisfies the desired relation (
5.19
), and so the first stage of the construction is complete.

In the next step, we form the pullback Lie superalgebra

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathfrak{vac}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\hskip 2.0pt{}_{{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}}\hskip-3.0pt\oplus_{\pi_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}

with

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathfrak{vac}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\jmath_{\rm vac}\equiv{\rm pr}_{2}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}

and with the basis

(5.42)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}},{\mathsf{Y}}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\bigr),\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{\rm vac}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{a}\in\{0,1\}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}P_{\underline{a}},{\mathsf{Y}}P_{\underline{a}}\bigr),\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}P_{\underline{a}}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{\rm vac}P_{\underline{a}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{\beta}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl(\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}},0\bigr),\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\breve{Z}{}_{(1)}^{\underline{\beta}}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{\rm vac}\breve{Z}{}_{(1)}^{\underline{\beta}}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{\gamma}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl(0,\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\gamma}}\bigr),\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}\breve{Z}{}_{(2)}^{\underline{\gamma}}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{\rm vac}\breve{Z}{}_{(2)}^{\underline{\gamma}}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\bigl((0,0),\mathcal{Z}\bigr)\equiv\mathcal{Z}_{\rm vac}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\left\langle\bigl(\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}J_{01},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{01}\bigr),\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{01}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{\rm vac}J_{01}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\widehat{a}<\widehat{b}=2}^{d}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}\bigr),\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{\rm vac}J_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}\right\rangle

and the superbracket obtained from the direct-sum one on
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathfrak{vac}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\,
through restriction. With this Lie superalgebra in hand, we may, at long last, finish the construction of the
{\bf sLieAlg}
-skeleton of Diag. (
5.16
). To this end, consider the pullback Lie superalgebras

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{n}^{*}\mathfrak{e}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\mathfrak{vac}\bigl(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2}\bigr)\hskip 2.0pt{}_{{\rm pr}_{n}}\hskip-3.0pt\oplus_{\pi_{\mathfrak{e}}}\mathfrak{e}\,,\qquad n\in\{1,2\}

with the respective bases

(5.46)

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathfrak{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}},{\mathsf{Y}}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\bigr),\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}^{(1)}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{a}\in\{0,1\}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}P_{\underline{a}},{\mathsf{Y}}P_{\underline{a}}\bigr),\mathscr{E}P_{\underline{a}}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}P^{(1)}_{\underline{a}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{\beta}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl(\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}},0\bigr),\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{(1)\,\underline{\beta}}_{(1)}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{\gamma}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl(0,\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\gamma}}\bigr),0\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{(1)\,\underline{\gamma}}_{(2)}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\bigl((0,0),\breve{\mathcal{Z}}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}\breve{\mathcal{Z}}{}^{(1)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\left\langle\bigl(\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}J_{01},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{01}\bigr),\mathscr{E}J_{01}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}J^{(1)}_{01}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\widehat{b}<\widehat{c}=2}^{d}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\bigr),\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}J^{(1)}_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\right\rangle\,,

(5.53)

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathfrak{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}},{\mathsf{Y}}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\bigr),\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}^{(2)}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{a}\in\{0,1\}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}P_{\underline{a}},{\mathsf{Y}}P_{\underline{a}}\bigr),\mathscr{E}P_{\underline{a}}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}P^{(2)}_{\underline{a}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{\beta}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl(\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\gamma}},0\bigr),0\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{(2)\,\underline{\beta}}_{(1)}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{\gamma}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl(0,\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}}\bigr),\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{(2)\,\underline{\gamma}}_{(2)}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\bigl((0,0),\breve{\mathcal{Z}}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}\breve{\mathcal{Z}}{}^{(2)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\left\langle\bigl(\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}J_{01},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{01}\bigr),\mathscr{E}J_{01}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}J^{(2)}_{01}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\widehat{b}<\widehat{c}=2}^{d}\,\left\langle\bigl(\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}},{\mathsf{Y}}J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\bigr),\mathscr{E}J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{E}J^{(2)}_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\right\rangle\,.

and superbrackets induced from the direct-sum ones, and form the ‘tensor-product’ Lie superalgebra

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathfrak{e}\equiv\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\hskip 2.0pt{}_{\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}}}\hskip-3.0pt\oplus_{{\rm pr}_{1}}{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathfrak{e}\bigr)/_{\sim_{\mathbb{R}}}\,,

based on the identification

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathcal{Z}_{\rm vac},0\bigr)\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(0,\mathscr{E}\breve{\mathcal{Z}}{}^{(2)}\bigr)\,.

The latter is the supervector space

(5.57)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathfrak{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{\rm vac}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}},\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}^{(2)}_{\underline{\alpha}}\bigr)\equiv\breve{Q}{}^{\otimes}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{a}\in\{0,1\}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{\rm vac}P_{\underline{a}},\mathscr{E}P^{(2)}_{\underline{a}}\bigr)\equiv P^{\otimes}_{\underline{a}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{\beta}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{\rm vac}\breve{Z}{}_{(1)}^{\underline{\beta}},\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{(2)\,\underline{\beta}}_{(1)}\bigr)\equiv\breve{Z}{}_{(1)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\beta}}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\underline{\gamma}=1}^{\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}\,\left\langle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{\rm vac}\breve{Z}{}_{(2)}^{\underline{\gamma}},\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{(2)\,\underline{\gamma}}_{(2)}\bigr)\equiv\breve{Z}{}_{(2)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\gamma}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\left\langle[(\mathcal{Z}_{\rm vac},0)]_{\sim_{\mathbb{R}}}\equiv\mathcal{Z}^{\otimes}\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{\rm vac}J_{01},\mathscr{E}J^{(2)}_{01}\bigr)\equiv J^{\otimes}_{01}\right\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{\widehat{b}<\widehat{c}=2}^{d}\,\left\langle\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{\rm vac}J_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}},\mathscr{E}J^{(2)}_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\bigr)\equiv J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\right\rangle

endowed with the superbracket

\displaystyle\{\breve{Q}{}^{\otimes}_{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{Q}{}^{\otimes}_{\underline{\beta}}\}=\overline{\gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\,P^{\otimes}_{\underline{a}}\,,\qquad\qquad[P^{\otimes}_{0},P^{\otimes}_{1}]=2\mathcal{Z}^{\otimes}\,,\qquad\qquad[\breve{Q}{}^{\otimes}_{\underline{\alpha}},P^{\otimes}_{\underline{a}}]=\overline{\gamma}{}_{\underline{a}\,\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\,\bigl(\breve{Z}{}_{(1)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\beta}}+\breve{Z}{}_{(2)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\beta}}\bigr)\,,

(5.58)

(5.59)

\displaystyle\{\breve{Q}{}^{\otimes}_{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{Z}{}_{(1)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\beta}}\}=-\delta_{\underline{\alpha}}^{\ \underline{\beta}}\,\breve{\mathcal{Z}}{}^{\otimes}\,,\qquad\qquad\{\breve{Q}{}^{\otimes}_{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{Z}{}_{(2)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\beta}}\}=0\,,

(5.60)

(5.61)

\displaystyle[P^{\otimes}_{\underline{a}},\breve{Z}{}_{(m)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\alpha}}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad\{\breve{Z}{}_{(m)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\alpha}},\breve{Z}{}_{(n)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\beta}}\}=0\,,

(5.62)

(5.63)

\displaystyle[\breve{Q}{}^{\otimes}_{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{\mathcal{Z}}{}^{\otimes}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[P^{\otimes}_{\underline{a}},\breve{\mathcal{Z}}{}^{\otimes}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\breve{Z}{}_{(m)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\alpha}},\breve{\mathcal{Z}}{}^{\otimes}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[\breve{\mathcal{Z}}{}^{\otimes},\breve{\mathcal{Z}}{}^{\otimes}]=0\,,

(5.64)

(5.65)

\displaystyle[J^{\otimes}_{01},\breve{Q}{}^{\otimes}_{\underline{\alpha}}]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\gamma_{01}{}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\ \underline{\alpha}}\,\breve{Q}{}^{\otimes}_{\underline{\beta}}\,,\qquad\qquad[J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},\breve{Q}{}^{\otimes}_{\underline{\alpha}}]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\gamma_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}{}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\ \underline{\alpha}}\,\breve{Q}{}^{\otimes}_{\underline{\beta}}\,,

(5.66)

(5.67)

\displaystyle[J^{\otimes}_{01},P^{\otimes}_{\underline{c}}]=\delta_{1\underline{c}}\,P^{\otimes}_{0}+\delta_{0\underline{c}}\,P^{\otimes}_{1}\,,\qquad\qquad[J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},P^{\otimes}_{\underline{c}}]=0\,,

(5.68)

(5.69)

\displaystyle[J^{\otimes}_{01},\breve{Z}{}_{(m)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\alpha}}]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\gamma_{01}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ \underline{\beta}}\,\breve{Z}{}_{(m)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\beta}}\,,\qquad\qquad[J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},\breve{Z}{}_{(m)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\alpha}}]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\gamma_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ \underline{\beta}}\,\breve{Z}{}_{(m)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\beta}}\,,

(5.70)

(5.71)

\displaystyle[J^{\otimes}_{01},\breve{\mathcal{Z}}{}^{\otimes}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},\breve{\mathcal{Z}}{}^{\otimes}]=0\,,

(5.72)

(5.73)

\displaystyle[J^{\otimes}_{01},J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{a}\widehat{b}},J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{c}\widehat{d}}]=\delta_{\widehat{a}\widehat{d}}\,J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}-\delta_{\widehat{a}\widehat{c}}\,J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{b}\widehat{d}}+\delta_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\,J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{a}\widehat{d}}-\delta_{\widehat{b}\widehat{d}}\,J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{a}\widehat{c}}\,.

Comparison of the above with the superbracket of
\,{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathfrak{e}\,
reveals the existence of the sought-after Lie-superalgebra isomorphism

\displaystyle\alpha_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{e}}}\equiv{\boldsymbol{1}}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\jmath_{\rm vac}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{l}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathfrak{e}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathfrak{e}

given by the unique linear extension of the assignment

\displaystyle\bigl(\breve{Q}{}^{\otimes}_{\underline{\alpha}},P^{\otimes}_{\underline{a}},\breve{Z}{}_{(1)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\beta}},\breve{Z}{}_{(2)}^{\otimes\,\underline{\gamma}},\mathcal{Z}^{\otimes},J^{\otimes}_{01},J^{\otimes}_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\bigr)\longmapsto\bigl(\mathscr{E}\breve{Q}{}^{(1)}_{\underline{\alpha}},\mathscr{E}P^{(1)}_{\underline{a}},\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{(1)\,\underline{\beta}}_{(1)},\mathscr{E}\breve{Z}{}^{(1)\,\underline{\gamma}}_{(2)},\mathscr{E}\breve{\mathcal{Z}}{}^{(1)},\mathscr{E}J^{(1)}_{01},\mathscr{E}J^{(1)}_{\widehat{b}\widehat{c}}\bigr)\,.

We summarise our findings in

Theorem 3
.

The null trivialisation
\,\tau\,
of the descended super-1-gerbe of the GS super-
\sigma
-model in the HP formulation stated in Theorem 2. admits a
{\bf sLieAlg}
-skeleton.

The
{\bf sLieAlg}
-skeleton determines a formal setting in which we may quite naturally address the question of existence of a
{\bf sLieGrp}
-model
of the vacuum, by which we mean a diagram

(5.74)

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

in
\,{\bf sLieGrp}\,
(decorated by constitutive CaE data) that integrates Diag. (
5.17
) in a self-explanatory manner, elaborated below. This is the anticipated Lie-supergroup structure that we were unable to associate
directly
with the vacuum foliation in Diag. (
5.16
) – it seems fitting to call it the
extended
\kappa
-symmetry group of the superstring in

\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. Here, we start by passing to the (sub-)supermanifold description of the Lie sub-supergroup
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\subset{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
as the locus of the coordinate equations

\displaystyle\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\bigr)^{\alpha}_{\ \beta}\,\theta^{\beta}=0\,,\qquad\alpha\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}\,,\qquad\qquad x^{\widehat{a}}=0\,,\qquad\widehat{a}\in\overline{2,d}\,,

(5.75)

(5.76)

\displaystyle\phi^{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}=0\,,\qquad(\underline{b},\widehat{c})\in\{0,1\}\times\overline{2,d}\,.

Using an adapted basis in
\,\mathfrak{siso}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
obtained through completion of the one for
\,\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})\,
given in Eq. (
2.49
), we thus obtain an embedding

\displaystyle J_{\rm vac}\ :\ {\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\equiv{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\rtimes_{L,S}{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\hookrightarrow{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,,

with
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\subset{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
defined by the top-line equations above. The embedding admits the explicit coordinate description

\displaystyle J_{\rm vac}\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}},\breve{\phi}{}^{\underline{S}}\bigr)=\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},0,\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}},0,\breve{\phi}{}^{\underline{S}},0\bigr)\,,

where the zeros correspond to the nullified coordinates on
\,{\rm ker}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}
, the
\,x^{\widehat{a}}\,
and the
\phi^{\underline{b}\widehat{c}}
, respectively. The binary operation
\,{\rm m}_{\rm vac}\,
on the embedded Lie supergroup is inherited, through restriction, from that on
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
,

\displaystyle{\rm m}_{\rm vac}\equiv J_{\rm vac}^{-1}\circ{\rm m}\circ\bigl(J_{\rm vac}\times J_{\rm vac}\bigr)\ :\ {\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\times{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\longrightarrow{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\,,

and reads, in coordinates,

\displaystyle{\rm m}_{\rm vac}\bigl(\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{1},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}}_{1},\breve{\phi}{}^{\underline{S}}_{1}\bigr),\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}}_{2},\breve{\phi}{}^{\underline{S}}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)=\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{1}+\breve{S}\bigl(\breve{\phi}_{1}\bigr)^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ \underline{\beta}}\,\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\beta}}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}}_{1}+\breve{L}\bigl(\breve{\phi}_{1}\bigr)^{\underline{a}}_{\ \underline{b}}\,\breve{x}{}^{\underline{b}}_{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\theta}{}_{1}\,\overline{\gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,\breve{S}\bigl(\breve{\phi}_{1}\bigr)\,\breve{\theta}{}_{2},\bigl(\breve{\phi}{}_{1}\star\breve{\phi}{}_{2}\bigr)^{\underline{S}}\bigr)\,,

where we have used the notation
\,\breve{S}(\breve{\phi}_{1})\,
and
\,\breve{L}(\breve{\phi}_{1})\,
for the ‘vacuum’ blocks of the block-diagonal (in the adapted basis) matrices
\,S(\breve{\phi}_{1},0)\,
and
\,L(\breve{\phi}_{1},0)
, respectively. In the next step, we take the sub-supermanifold

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\equiv{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\frac{D_{d,1}}{2}}

of
\,{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
with the second cartesian factor defined by the coordinate equations

\displaystyle\xi_{\beta}\,\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\bigr)^{\beta}_{\ \alpha}=0\,,\qquad\alpha\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}\,,

and use it to write the desired embedding

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}J_{\rm vac}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\rtimes_{L,S,S^{-{\rm T}}}{\rm Spin}(d,1)_{\rm vac}\hookrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})

in adapted coordinates (
cp
Eq. (
5.22
)) as

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}J_{\rm vac}\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}},\breve{\xi}{}_{\underline{\beta}},\breve{\phi}{}^{\underline{S}}\bigr)=\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},0,\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}},0,\breve{\xi}{}_{\underline{\beta}},0,\breve{\phi}{}^{\underline{S}},0\bigr)\,.

The sub-supermanifold submerses surjectively onto
\,{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}
,

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\longrightarrow{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\,,

as

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}}\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}},\breve{\xi}{}_{\underline{\beta}},\breve{\phi}{}^{\underline{S}}\bigr)=\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}},\breve{\phi}{}^{\underline{S}}\bigr)\,.

The inherited binary operation

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}_{\rm vac}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}J_{\rm vac}^{-1}\circ{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}\circ\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}J_{\rm vac}\times{\mathsf{Y}}J_{\rm vac}\bigr)\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\times{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}

uses the same objects
\,\breve{S}\,
and
\,\breve{L}\,
as
\,{\rm m}_{\rm vac}
,

(5.79)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}_{\rm vac}\bigl(\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{1},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}}_{1},\breve{\xi}{}_{1\,\underline{\beta}},\breve{\phi}{}^{\underline{S}}_{1}\bigr),\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}}_{2},\breve{\xi}{}_{2\,\underline{\beta}},\breve{\phi}{}^{\underline{S}}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{1}+\breve{S}\bigl(\breve{\phi}_{1}\bigr)^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ \underline{\beta}}\,\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\beta}}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}}_{1}+\breve{L}\bigl(\breve{\phi}_{1}\bigr)^{\underline{a}}_{\ \underline{b}}\,\breve{x}{}^{\underline{b}}_{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\theta}{}_{1}\,\overline{\gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,\breve{S}\bigl(\breve{\phi}_{1}\bigr)\,\breve{\theta}{}_{2},

\displaystyle\breve{\xi}{}_{1\,\underline{\beta}}+\breve{\xi}{}_{2\,\underline{\gamma}}\,\breve{S}\bigl(\breve{\phi}_{1}\bigr)^{-1\,\underline{\gamma}}_{\ \ \ \ \underline{\beta}}+\overline{\gamma}{}_{\underline{d}\,\underline{\beta}\underline{\gamma}}\,\breve{\theta}{}_{1}^{\underline{\gamma}}\,\breve{L}\bigl(\underline{\phi}_{1}\bigr)^{\underline{d}}_{\ \underline{e}}\,\breve{x}{}_{2}^{\underline{e}},\bigl(\breve{\phi}{}_{1}\star\breve{\phi}{}_{2}\bigr)^{\underline{S}}\bigr)\,,

and gives rise, as usual, to the left regular action

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\breve{\ell}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}_{\rm vac}\,.

of the Lie supergroup
\,{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\,
on itself. In the coordinate presentation of
\,{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}_{\rm vac}
, we have taken into account the identity

\displaystyle\overline{\gamma}{}_{\underline{a}}\otimes\overline{\gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}=0

and dropped the term in the coordinate expression for

trilinear in the Graßmann-odd coordinates accordingly.

On the Lie supergroup
\,{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}
, we find the standard
{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{vac}(\mathfrak{sB}^{{\rm(HP)}}_{1,2})
-valued LI Maurer–Cartan super-1-form

\displaystyle\breve{\theta}{}_{\rm L}=\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}}^{*}\breve{q}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\otimes{\mathsf{Y}}\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}+\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}}^{*}\breve{p}{}^{\underline{a}}\otimes{\mathsf{Y}}P_{\underline{a}}+\breve{z}{}_{\underline{\beta}}\otimes\breve{Z}{}^{\underline{\beta}}+\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}}^{*}\breve{j}^{\underline{S}}\otimes{\mathsf{Y}}J_{\underline{S}}

\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})

\,{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})
)

\displaystyle\breve{q}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x},\breve{\phi}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\breve{S}\bigl(\breve{\phi}\bigr)^{-1\,\underline{\alpha}}_{\ \ \ \underline{\beta}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\beta}}=:\breve{S}\bigl(\breve{\phi}\bigr)^{-1\,\underline{\alpha}}_{\ \ \ \underline{\beta}}\,\breve{\underline{q}}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x}\bigr)\,,

(5.81)

\displaystyle\breve{p}{}^{\underline{a}}\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x},\breve{\phi}\bigr)

\displaystyle\breve{z}{}_{\underline{\beta}}\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x},\breve{\xi},\breve{\phi}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl({\mathsf{d}}\breve{\xi}{}_{\underline{\beta}}-\overline{\gamma}{}_{\underline{a}\,\underline{\beta}\underline{\gamma}}\,\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\gamma}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}^{\underline{a}}\bigr)\,\breve{S}\bigl(\breve{\phi}\bigr)^{\underline{\beta}}_{\ \underline{\alpha}}=:\breve{\underline{z}}{}_{\underline{\beta}}\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x},\breve{\xi}\bigr)\,\breve{S}\bigl(\breve{\phi}\bigr)^{\underline{\beta}}_{\ \underline{\alpha}}\,,

enter the definition of the LI super-2-cocycle

(5.85)

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\breve{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

whose final (descended) form follows from the unimodularity of

\displaystyle\bigl(\breve{p}{}^{0}\wedge\breve{p}{}^{1}\bigr)\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x},\breve{\phi}\bigr)=\bigl(\breve{\underline{p}}{}^{0}\wedge\breve{\underline{p}}{}^{1}\bigr)\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x}\bigr)\,.

Following the logic of the geometrisation programme, we seek to associate with the latter a central extension

(5.86)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt{\boldsymbol{1}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\longrightarrow{\rm E}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{\rm E}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ }{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{1}}

integrating the formerly obtained Lie-superalgebra extension (
5.18
). To this end, we compute the non-LI primitive of

,

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\breve{\beta}}\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x},\breve{\xi},\breve{\phi}\bigr)={\mathsf{d}}\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{\xi}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}+\epsilon_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}^{\underline{b}}\bigr)\,,

and define the principal

-connection super-1-form

of the principal

\displaystyle\pi_{\rm E}\ :\ {\rm E}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}

explicitly as

\displaystyle\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\breve{\mathcal{A}}}{}_{\rm E}\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x},\breve{\xi},\breve{\phi},\breve{z}\bigr)=\tfrac{{\mathsf{i}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{z}}{\breve{z}}-\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{\xi}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}-\epsilon_{\underline{a}\underline{b}}\,\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}^{\underline{b}}=:\tfrac{{\mathsf{i}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{z}}{\breve{z}}+\breve{\rm A}\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x},\breve{\xi},\breve{\phi}\bigr)\,,

determining the Lie-supergroup structure on
\,{\rm E}\,
through imposition of the usual demand that the primitive
\,-\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(1)$}}{\breve{\mathcal{A}}}{}_{\rm E}\,
of the pullback
\,\pi_{\rm E}^{*}\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\breve{\beta}}\,
of the super-2-cocycle
\,\underset{\textrm{\tiny$(2)$}}{\breve{\beta}}\,
be LI. From the direct computation (carried out for
\,(\breve{\varepsilon}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{y}{}^{\underline{a}},\breve{\zeta}{}_{\underline{\beta}},\breve{\psi}{}^{\underline{S}})\in{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sISO}(d,1|D_{d,1})_{\rm vac}

(5.88)
